## 1. [[XPU][UT]Convert awq-packed MoE qweight to the gptq-equivalent layout on xpu](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/48552)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #48552
- **Author:** [Yejing-Lai](https://github.com/Yejing-Lai)
- **Merged By:** [jikunshang](https://github.com/jikunshang)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 23:54:11
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/48552/files) (1):**
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/fused_moe/oracle/int_wna16.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The XPU weight-processing path in int_wna16.py previously assumed a GPTQ-style K-packed nibble layout for all quant configs, which broke AWQ MoE loading with a RuntimeError about mismatched tensor dimensions. This change detects AutoAWQConfig and repacks the w13 and w2 qweights via AWQUtils.moe_repack to convert AWQ's N-packed layout into the GPTQ-equivalent layout expected downstream. It fixes the failing test_awq_load gemma4-moe-standard-awq-dot-suffix case.

**Technical impact**  
Adds a conditional branch in _process_weights_xpu that imports AutoAWQConfig and the vllm_xpu_kernels AWQUtils module, then repacks both expert weight tensors before the existing uint8 view/contiguous conversion. Behavior for non-AWQ quant configs is unchanged. It introduces a runtime dependency on vllm_xpu_kernels for the AWQ path and relies on the repack producing the exact layout the XPU fused MoE int4 kernel expects.

**Potential risks**  
The import of vllm_xpu_kernels is done lazily inside the function, so failures would surface at weight-load time rather than import time; environments lacking that module could break AWQ XPU loading. Correctness depends on AWQUtils.moe_repack matching the kernel's expected nibble ordering and shape, and only the single named test is cited as validation, so other AWQ MoE variants or non-MoE AWQ paths are not clearly covered. The little-endian guard remains but the new repack path's endianness assumptions are not independently verified.

**Key insights**  
The fix is narrowly scoped and correctly targets the root cause: layout mismatch between AWQ (N-packed) and GPTQ (K-packed) conventions. Consider adding or extending tests beyond the single gemma4-moe case, and documenting the expected output layout of moe_repack so future changes to either side do not silently regress. The lazy import is reasonable for optional XPU kernels but should be paired with a clear error message if unavailable.

## 2. [[Build] Point vllm-flash-attn at vllm-project/flash-attention#222 for CI](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68348)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68348
- **Author:** [chatouis](https://github.com/chatouis)
- **Merged By:** [MatthewBonanni](https://github.com/MatthewBonanni)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 23:49:13
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68348/files) (1):**
  - `cmake/external_projects/vllm_flash_attn.cmake`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The PR updates the vllm-flash-attn GIT_TAG in cmake/external_projects/vllm_flash_attn.cmake from 8dd9eaea to 1b32c5ca, pointing the build at the head commit of vllm-project/flash-attention#222, which disables the append-KV kernels in FA2. This is a temporary CI pin to validate the upstream change before it merges.

**Technical impact**  
The FA2 extension build drops the 864 Append_KV=true instantiations of flash_fwd_splitkv_kernel, shrinking _vllm_fa2_C.abi3.so from ~70.7 MiB to ~40.0 MiB and roughly halving build time. Remaining kernels are reported to have identical SASS, and vLLM's in-tree call sites (varlen_fwd, fwd_sparse, varlen_fwd_sparse) do not use fwd_kvcache, so runtime behavior should be unchanged.

**Potential risks**  
The pin points at a fork commit (1b32c5ca) rather than an upstream merge commit, so it must be reverted once the upstream PR lands; otherwise the build depends on a non-canonical ref. Any out-of-tree or future code calling torch.ops._vllm_fa2_C.fwd_kvcache with new K/V will now raise an error. Validation was done on a partial build and a single GPU (RTX 4080), so full-wheel CI is the real check.

**Key insights**  
This is a low-risk, well-justified build pin that removes dead append-KV code paths, yielding significant binary size and build-time savings. The main follow-up is to repoint GIT_TAG back to vllm-project/flash-attention at the merge commit after the upstream PR is merged.

## 3. [[LoRA][Model] Add LoRA support for GLM-5.3-Flash](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56327)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #56327
- **Author:** [HollowMan6](https://github.com/HollowMan6)
- **Merged By:** [jeejeelee](https://github.com/jeejeelee)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 23:44:45
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56327/files) (13):**
  - `tests/lora/test_layers.py`
  - `tests/models/glm5next/test_kda_conv_weight_refit.py`
  - `tests/quantization/test_fp8_dequant.py`
  - `vllm/lora/layers/__init__.py`
  - `vllm/lora/layers/column_parallel_linear.py`
  - `vllm/lora/layers/custom_layer.py`
  - `vllm/lora/utils.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/attention/mla_attention.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/fused_moe/fused_moe_modular_method.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/sparse_attn_indexer.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v32/nvidia/model.py`
  - `vllm/models/glm5next/common/attention.py`
  - `vllm/models/glm5next/common/model.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds LoRA support for GLM-5.3-Flash (Glm5NextForConditionalGeneration), including paths that previously bypassed LoRA wrappers: absorbed MLA kv_b_proj (new MLAKVBProjWithLoRA/ShardedLoRA applied after the cached W_UK/W_UV BMMs), the sparse indexer's fused wk/weights_proj with FP32 head gate (new Glm5NextIndexerWkWeightsLinear and Glm5NextIndexerWkWeightsWithLoRA), TP-replicated shards in MergedColumnParallelLinearWithLoRA via replicated_shard_ids, lm_head/embedding mapping, and weight-sync resolution through base_layer. It also fixes double post-processing of routed experts in FusedMoEModularMethod and adds the missing wk_weights_proj packed mapping for GLM-5.2.

**Technical impact**  
Touches core LoRA layer dispatch (custom_layer, column_parallel_linear, utils), MLA attention forward (disabling fused v_up_proj and aiter fused decode query when kv_b_proj LoRA is present), fused MoE modular method, sparse indexer init, and GLM-5.3-Flash/DeepSeek-V3.2 model loading. Behavior changes are gated on LoRA presence or new attributes (is_mla_kv_b_proj, replicated_shard_ids), but the MLA forward and weight-loading paths are shared by non-LoRA users, so regressions there would have broad impact.

**Potential risks**  
The MLA absorbed LoRA path is custom (Punica kernels bypassed) and depends on token_lora_indices slicing, DCP all-gather, and CUDA-graph capture correctness; subtle mismatches under MQA decode prefixes, PCP/DCP, or fully-sharded LoRA are plausible. Disabling fused v_up_proj and aiter fused decode query when LoRA is active changes performance characteristics. The FusedMoEModularMethod early-return relies on a routed_experts attribute heuristic that could skip legitimate processing if the module structure differs. Validation was limited: CUDA tests and model evals were not rerun on the rebased commit, and the PR checklist leaves testing/validation unchecked.

**Key insights**  
The core insight is that absorbed MLA and the FP32 indexer gate compute projections outside the wrapped module's forward, so adapters must be injected at the BMM/GEMM sites with per-token slot masking. Reusing replicated_shard_ids and base_layer name resolution keeps changes localized, and the dedicated wrapper classes avoid altering how models construct kv_b_proj. The MoE double-processing fix and GLM-5.2 mapping are small but necessary correctness fixes; the large new test coverage (absorbed projection, indexer gate, replicated shards, refit, FP8 dequant) is the main safety net given the missing end-to-end rerun.

## 4. [[Bugfix][LoRA] Allow non-divisible BLIP-2 encoder budgets](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67271)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #67271
- **Author:** [Ondre119](https://github.com/Ondre119)
- **Merged By:** [jeejeelee](https://github.com/jeejeelee)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 23:44:17
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67271/files) (1):**
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/blip2.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The BLIP-2 get_mm_lora_token_counts method previously asserted that the encoder token budget was an exact multiple of num_query_tokens, which failed during LoRA initialization when a sizing budget (e.g. 5000 with 32 query tokens) was not divisible. The fix skips the divisibility assertion when mm_kwargs is None (the startup sizing path) and sizes wrappers using integer division to the number of complete images that fit, while retaining the assertion for actual per-image input processing.

**Technical impact**  
LoRA initialization for BLIP-2 towers/connectors now tolerates arbitrary encoder budgets by flooring to B // Q images, leaving leftover tokens unused instead of aborting startup. The per-image path (mm_kwargs not None) still enforces divisibility, so real image token counts remain validated. Behavior for divisible and zero budgets is unchanged, and the change is localized to a single file with no API surface changes.

**Potential risks**  
The startup sizing path no longer validates that the budget is a multiple of query tokens, so misconfigured budgets silently drop up to Q-1 tokens rather than surfacing an error; this is intentional but could mask configuration mistakes. Validation relies on dummy-weight smoke tests and a removed regression test, so future regressions may not be caught by CI. Correctness of the floor-based image count depends on the assumption that only complete images can be processed, which should hold but is not independently asserted.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is conflating a sizing budget with actual per-image token counts; distinguishing the two via mm_kwargs is a minimal, targeted fix. The PR's evidence is strong (reproduced failure, byte-identical wheel comparison, end-to-end smoke test with LoRA adapter), but removing the regression test reduces durable coverage. Consider adding a lightweight unit test for the sizing path to prevent future regressions.

## 5. [[XPU][CI]Expand attention and worker tests in Intel GPU CI](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67833)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #67833
- **Author:** [zxd1997066](https://github.com/zxd1997066)
- **Merged By:** [jikunshang](https://github.com/jikunshang)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 22:54:56
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67833/files) (3):**
  - `.buildkite/intel_jobs/attention_intel.yaml`
  - `.buildkite/intel_jobs/misc_intel.yaml`
  - `.buildkite/intel_jobs/test-intel.yaml`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR reworks Intel GPU CI test coverage in Buildkite YAML. It expands the V1 Attention job to run the full v1/attention suite (with several CUDA/ROCm/flashinfer/MLA exclusions), splits the time-consuming MLA tests into a new optional V1 Attention MLA job, adds a standalone V1 Executor + Worker job covering v1/executor, v1/worker, and v1/streaming_input, and removes v1/engine and v1/sample from the commented V1 test block since they are covered elsewhere.

**Technical impact**  
Changes CI behavior only, not runtime code. Attention coverage broadens significantly and the timeout is raised from 60 to 90 minutes; MLA and Executor+Worker jobs are marked optional and gated by source_file_dependencies, so they only trigger on relevant changes. The new jobs use 1-2 GPU agent tags and spawn multiprocessing for worker tests.

**Potential risks**  
The broadened attention run relies on multiple --ignore/--deselect flags; if upstream test files are renamed or new incompatible tests are added, the job may fail or silently skip coverage. Marking the new jobs optional means failures may not block merges, reducing signal. The 90-minute timeout and 2-GPU requirement increase CI resource usage, and the removed v1/engine and v1/sample lines assume coverage in other YAML files remains intact.

**Key insights**  
The PR aligns Intel CI structure with CUDA by isolating worker/executor tests and moving heavy MLA suites to a separate job. Validation table shows reasonable runtimes (Attention ~33 min, MLA ~24 min, Executor+Worker ~16 min). Reviewers should verify the ignore/deselect lists stay in sync with the test tree and confirm the cross-file coverage assumptions for engine and sample tests.

## 6. [[Core] Configurable async scheduling depth for Model Runner V2](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64145)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #64145
- **Author:** [Schnitzel](https://github.com/Schnitzel)
- **Merged By:** [njhill](https://github.com/njhill)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 22:51:34
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64145/files) (17):**
  - `tests/test_config.py`
  - `tests/v1/core/test_async_scheduler.py`
  - `tests/v1/core/test_scheduler.py`
  - `tests/v1/e2e/general/test_async_scheduling.py`
  - `tests/v1/engine/test_engine_core.py`
  - `tests/v1/fault_tolerance/test_engine_core_sentinel.py`
  - `tests/v1/spec_decode/test_adaptive_verification.py`
  - `vllm/config/scheduler.py`
  - `vllm/config/vllm.py`
  - `vllm/engine/arg_utils.py`
  - `vllm/v1/core/sched/async_scheduler.py`
  - `vllm/v1/core/sched/output.py`
  - `vllm/v1/core/sched/scheduler.py`
  - `vllm/v1/engine/core.py`
  - `vllm/v1/fault_tolerance/engine_core_sentinel.py`
  - `vllm/v1/request.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/spec_decode/adaptive_verification.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a new SchedulerConfig.async_scheduling_depth option (default 2, minimum 2) exposed via --async-scheduling-depth, and changes VllmConfig.max_concurrent_batches from pp_size + 1 to pp_size + depth - 1 so async scheduling can keep more steps in flight. Supporting changes track in-flight placeholder steps per request (num_output_placeholder_steps) to keep max_tokens and draft-usage logic correct, make the engine core's deferred step persistent across calls (deferred_step) and wait until no queued step shares a request with it, clear it on fault drain, and size the adaptive-verification confidence ring buffer from max_concurrent_batches.

**Technical impact**  
Touches core scheduling, engine-core batch-queue stepping, request bookkeeping, fault-tolerance cleanup, and speculative-decoding adaptive verification. The default behavior is preserved (depth 2 yields the previous pp_size + 1), but deeper depths change how many steps overlap, how deferred sampling is gated, and how many host-side confidence slots and pinned buffers are allocated. The new deferred_step field becomes part of engine-core state that must be reset on faults and is now asserted in tests.

**Potential risks**  
The deferred-step gating (waiting until no queued step shares a request) is subtle and could introduce stalls or deadlocks if shares_requests_with logic is wrong or if deferred_step is not cleared on all paths. The placeholder-step accounting must stay consistent across preemption, stop, and stale-output handling; a mismatch could schedule steps past max_tokens or drop drafts incorrectly. Deeper queues increase pinned host memory and in-flight state, and the adaptive-verification ring-buffer change alters stale-confidence semantics that are only partially covered by tests. Validation was done on GB300 and a nightly main run, not full CI, and VLLM_GPU_SYNC_CHECK was not run.

**Key insights**  
The PR is well-scoped with targeted unit tests for config, scheduler draft logic, deep batch queues, fault-tolerance deferred-step clearing, and adaptive-verification ring behavior, plus e2e async-scheduling coverage at depth 3 and 4. The main review focus should be the correctness of deferred_step lifecycle and the num_output_placeholder_steps accounting under preemption and stale outputs, since those are the highest-risk behavioral changes. Performance claims are plausible but environment-specific and not independently reproduced in CI.

## 7. [[ROCm][Perf][DSv4.1] Insert the compressed KV cache inside the compression launch](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67364)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #67364
- **Author:** [okorzh-amd](https://github.com/okorzh-amd)
- **Merged By:** [shen-shanshan](https://github.com/shen-shanshan)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 22:40:34
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67364/files) (6):**
  - `tests/kernels/test_compressor_kv_cache.py`
  - `tests/models/test_dsv41_mono_decode.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v41/amd/mono_decode.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v41/amd/rocm.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v41/common/ops/fused_compress_quant_cache.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v41/compressor.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
On ROCm, the DeepSeek-V4.1 decode path previously ran a separate Triton launch (_rope_quant_insert_kernel) to write the compressed fp8_ds_mla record into the paged KV cache after fused_save_compress_norm produced the BF16 latent. This PR folds that insert into the compression kernel: fused_save_compress_norm gains optional kv_cache/kv_slot_mapping/cos_sin_cache arguments, a new _insert_latent helper and shared _store_v4_record routine, and DeepseekCompressor.forward accepts an optional rotary_emb to take the fused path. Two ROCm decode call sites (mono_decode._select and rocm.py _forward_csa2_full) switch to the fused form, while NVIDIA, prefill, PCP, and plain-row caches keep the separate insert. The goal is to cut the compress+insert chain from 8 to 4 launches per decode step.

**Technical impact**  
The fused path removes 4 kernel launches per decode step at the 4 kv-source layers, measured at -34.5% (21.78 to 14.26 us) in the microbenchmark, though end-to-end serving ITL change (~-0.17% of a 4.30 ms step) is below run-to-run noise. The refactor extracts _store_v4_record so both the fused and standalone insert kernels share identical record-writing logic, and the Triton kernel gains constexpr flags (INSERT_CACHE, SANITIZE_CACHE_NANS) plus a do_not_specialize on num_cache_tokens. Behavior is gated by dtype/shape checks (uint8, 584-byte rows) and pcp_size==1, so non-ROCm and unsupported paths fall back to the existing insert_cache call. Tests cover byte-exact cache equality against the separate path, plain-row fallback, and the updated call-site signatures.

**Potential risks**  
The fused path is only exercised on ROCm hardware, so the new Triton code paths (INSERT_CACHE, SANITIZE_CACHE_NANS on gfx950) rely on the added unit tests and the reported GSM8K/RULER parity rather than broad CI coverage. The correctness argument rests on byte-exact cache writes, but the stack is noted as non-bit-reproducible run-to-run, so subtle divergence could be masked by noise; the vt+cwe 128K acceptance drop (4.052 to 3.837) is attributed to noise from a single server pair and is not statistically significant. The gating condition (rotary_emb is not None and pcp_size==1 and uint8/584 shape) is implicit and could silently take the wrong branch if cache layout assumptions change. The kernel now does more work per program (norm + quant + RoPE + store), which could affect register pressure or occupancy on other shapes not benchmarked.

**Key insights**  
This is a launch-overhead optimization rather than an algorithmic change: the record bytes are identical, and the win is bounded by the number of launches removed, which is why the end-to-end effect is small and only clearly visible on the serial --monokernel path. The design correctly scopes the fusion to ROCm decode while preserving the separate insert for NVIDIA, prefill, PCP, and plain-row caches, limiting blast radius. The shared _store_v4_record extraction is a good refactor that keeps the two write paths from drifting. Reviewers should focus on the implicit gating conditions in DeepseekCompressor.forward and on whether the new kernel's added per-program work is acceptable across the 14-336-row graph range.

## 8. [[ROCm][Perf][DSv4.1] Build the MXFP4 indexer decode metadata in one launch](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67363)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #67363
- **Author:** [okorzh-amd](https://github.com/okorzh-amd)
- **Merged By:** [shen-shanshan](https://github.com/shen-shanshan)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 22:38:52
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67363/files) (4):**
  - `tests/v1/attention/test_rocm_paged_mxfp4_indexer_plan.py`
  - `vllm/v1/attention/backends/mla/indexer.py`
  - `vllm/v1/attention/backends/mla/rocm_paged_mxfp4_indexer.py`
  - `vllm/v1/attention/ops/rocm_paged_mxfp4_indexer.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a Triton kernel (RocmMxfp4DecodeRowsKernel) that builds the ROCm MXFP4 indexer decode metadata (row lens, expanded block table, decode lens, per-req lens, context lens, block ends) in one launch per KV group instead of a chain of eager torch ops. A new should_build_custom_decode_rows/_build_decode_rows hook is added to the shared DeepseekV32IndexerMetadataBuilder (default False/NotImplementedError), and build() routes through it on non-varlen, non-DCP decode steps, leaving CUDA and other builders on the existing path. Tests compare the fused path against the torch path across uniform and adaptive-verification step shapes.

**Technical impact**  
Cuts eager indexer-builder launches per decode step from 17 to 4 (C1) and builder GPU time by 58-77% in microbenchmarks, yielding a measurable ~23-26 us (-0.55% to -0.61%) ITL p50 improvement at C1 with no resolved regression at higher concurrency. The hook is opt-in and gated so non-ROCm backends and varlen/DCP steps are behaviorally unchanged; padding rows are now zeroed rather than left stale, and the compress-ratio division is skipped for custom rows since the kernel already emits compressed lengths. JIT warmup registration is added for the new kernel keyed on compress_ratio and block_cols.

**Potential risks**  
The kernel reimplements length/block-end/context-lens math that previously lived in torch, so any divergence in edge cases (zero-length rows, prefill-only steps, shard offsets, candidate_block=0) would silently corrupt indexer metadata; the new test covers many shapes but relies on monkeypatched cache layout and schedule helpers. The decode_block_ends buffer is now sliced at decode_shard.start, which changes indexing semantics and depends on the shard being consistent with the kernel's row ordering. The hook contract (return False on varlen/DCP) is enforced only by convention in subclasses, and the NotImplementedError default could surface if a builder returns True without overriding. Accuracy deltas are within noise but GSM8K shows a small -0.15 pt shift and GPQA has high run-to-run churn, so subtle correctness issues would be hard to detect from the reported evals alone.

**Key insights**  
This is a well-scoped, opt-in optimization: the shared builder gains a documented hook with a safe default, and only the ROCm MXFP4 builder opts in, keeping CUDA and other paths untouched. The PR is unusually rigorous about measurement, explicitly separating resolved gains (C1) from changes below the A/A noise floor (C2-C64) and reporting confidence intervals and McNemar tests for accuracy. The main review focus should be the Triton kernel's equivalence to the torch path on unusual step shapes and the interaction with the TP decode-row shard offset, since those are the places where a silent metadata bug could hide.

## 9. [[Frontend] generate: keep sample logprobs as engine rows and render them without objects](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60542)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #60542
- **Author:** [hao-aaron](https://github.com/hao-aaron)
- **Merged By:** [aoshen02](https://github.com/aoshen02)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 22:35:44
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60542/files) (11):**
  - `tests/entrypoints/scale_out/token_in_token_out/test_logprobs_render.py`
  - `tests/entrypoints/scale_out/token_in_token_out/test_serving_tokens.py`
  - `tests/test_logprobs.py`
  - `tests/v1/engine/test_logprobs_processor.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/scale_out/token_in_token_out/api_router.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/scale_out/token_in_token_out/logprobs_render.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/scale_out/token_in_token_out/protocol.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/scale_out/token_in_token_out/serving.py`
  - `vllm/logprobs.py`
  - `vllm/sampling_params.py`
  - `vllm/v1/engine/logprobs.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Reworks how generate logprobs are stored and rendered to cut memory and event-loop stalls after an RL abort. FlatLogprobs is rewritten from Python lists into append-only numpy columns (int32/float32 with widening), LogprobsProcessor appends engine rows in bulk, and the non-streaming tokens-mode generate endpoint renders logprobs JSON directly from the arrays via a new logprobs_render module, returning a RenderedGenerateResponse streamed in parts. A private SamplingParams._detokenize_logprobs flag skips decoded-token work for tokens mode.

**Technical impact**  
Large behavioral and performance change inside vLLM: response bytes are claimed identical to the pydantic path, with fallbacks to the per-entry path for irregular rows, duplicate ids, non-finite values, or sampled id not in slot 0. FlatLogprobs column attributes become read-only numpy-backed views, the class is no longer a dataclass, old pickles break, and stepped slices are fixed. A per-ServingTokens asyncio.Lock serializes multi-block renders; small renders bypass it. Streaming, text mode, logprobs=-1, and prompt-logprobs requests are unchanged.

**Potential risks**  
The custom JSON renderer must stay byte-identical to the pydantic/orjson path, and subtle divergences (float notation, clamping, rank 0 to null, key order) are a correctness risk despite extensive tests. The private _detokenize_logprobs flag and flat_logprobs enablement are implicit coupling between endpoint and engine. External users of FlatLogprobs relying on mutable lists, dataclass behavior, or pickling will break. The render lock could add latency if a large render is slow, and the numpy column widening logic is intricate. Benchmark claims are largely from mock engines and a non-committed driver script.

**Key insights**  
The PR targets a real memory/latency problem (82-86s pause, OOM at RL shape) with measured large gains (pause to ~0.3s, RSS roughly halved). The design keeps a single flat container, preserves the list-like API, and falls back to the existing path whenever rows cannot be mapped directly, which limits blast radius. The decision to render on the event loop in 8k-entry blocks with a lock, rather than a thread pool, is justified by GIL profiling. Reviewers should focus on byte-parity of the renderer, the fallback conditions, and the public API breakage of FlatLogprobs.

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Moves the default Python for all Docker images (CUDA, CPU, Zen, XPU, ROCm, gfx1250, s390x, ppc64le) and the CI that runs in them from 3.12 to 3.14, regenerates the test lock files for 3.14, and flips the nightly smoke test to cover 3.12 instead. It also carries the code fixes 3.14 requires: a depyf counter-copy workaround, LMCache 0.5.7 packed token-ID support, a datasets>=5 bump with a reimplemented 3.x streaming shuffle in mrcr_eval, and small test fixes for classmethod fixtures and argparse help colors.

**Technical impact**  
Touches the whole build and dependency surface: Dockerfile base images and how each gets Python (manylinux /opt/python, deadsnakes, uv venv, UBI RPMs), docker/versions.json, pre-commit pip-compile targets, and large regenerated lock files (pydantic, numpy, scipy, pandas, datasets, dill, multiprocess, audioop-lts/standard-* backports). Runtime behavior changes in the LMCache connector (token IDs now packed as big-endian uint32 bytes when LMCache exposes token_bytes, with a fallback for older versions), the depyf debug path (reads the Dynamo counter from its repr instead of copying it), and the MRCR eval sample selection (custom buffer shuffle to preserve calibrated thresholds).

**Potential risks**  
ROCm base builds Triton, FlashAttention, AITER and MORI from source and is untested on 3.14; AITER nightly wheel selection follows the running interpreter and needs cp314 nightlies, and the torchao ROCm 3.14 fix is still open. TPU's nixl==0.3.0 pin has no cp314 wheel, and source-only test deps (arctic-inference, pytrec-eval-terrier) are not yet built on 3.14. The depyf fix depends on the private torch._dynamo._unique_id_counter repr format, and the LMCache packing relies on a field-name probe plus a private request._all_token_ids attribute, both of which could break on upstream changes. Interpreter and torch.compile performance on 3.14 versus 3.12 still needs an end-to-end serving comparison.

**Key insights**  
The PR is well-scoped and evidence-backed: wheel availability was verified per requirement on x86_64 and aarch64, lock changes are limited to what 3.14 needs, and the LMCache byte format is checked against LMCache's own codec in a test. The riskiest areas are the from-source ROCm stack and the TPU nixl pin, which the description explicitly flags. The datasets>=5 bump is the most behaviorally subtle change because it required reimplementing datasets 3.x shuffle semantics to keep eval thresholds valid, so that code deserves careful review.

## 11. [[Bugfix][Model Loader] Preserve CPU-offloaded Engram storage during layerwise reload](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68048)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68048
- **Author:** [Ronald1995](https://github.com/Ronald1995)
- **Merged By:** [aoshen02](https://github.com/aoshen02)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 22:20:50
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68048/files) (8):**
  - `docs/training/layerwise.md`
  - `tests/distributed/test_sharded_rdt_plan.py`
  - `tests/kernels/test_engram.py`
  - `tests/model_executor/model_loader/test_reload_lifecycle.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/weight_transfer/sharded_rdt_engine.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/model_loader/reload/layerwise.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/model_loader/reload/meta.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v41/common/engram.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds an internal _skip_layerwise_reload marker on DeepSeek-V4.1 Engram tables when cpu_offload is enabled and peer_rows == 0, and honors it in the three meta helpers, the layerwise initializer, and the sharded_rdt call-plan builder. This keeps CPU-offloaded Engram tables in their existing host storage during layerwise reload instead of restoring them on meta, wrapping their loaders, and temporarily allocating a full table on GPU. Docs and tests are updated accordingly.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior changes only for the specific CPU-offloaded, non-peer Engram path: those modules are excluded from layerwise meta capture/restore/materialize and from the sharded_rdt first/last-chunk materialization plan, so checkpoint loaders write directly into pinned host storage. Generic layerwise lifecycle, finalization hooks, sharding, and GPU-resident or peer-HBM Engram paths are intended to be unchanged. The marker is a private attribute checked via getattr, so it is low-coupling but relies on convention.

**Potential risks**  
The PR itself states functional verification of the latest merged head is pending and the two focused tests were not executed, so the core claim is unvalidated on current code. The sharded_rdt plan change skips modules entirely, which could interact subtly with chunk ordering, dedup, or memory accounting if other code assumes every scattered layer appears in the plan. The marker is set based on cpu_offload and peer_rows at construction, so any later mutation of those attributes or subclassing could desynchronize the opt-out from actual storage layout.

**Key insights**  
The fix is narrowly scoped and well-justified: skipping only materialization would still wrap the host loader and buffer/replay inputs, so the entire module must be skipped, which the patch does. Tests cover A->B->A updates, storage/pointer/loader identity, post-load hooks, and real lookup correctness, which are the right invariants. The main gap is execution evidence on the merged head and real full-model reload/inference plus GPU peak-memory measurement before merge.

## 12. [[Bugfix][Spec Decode] Reject non-finite Newton steps in the acceptance estimator refit](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68164)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68164
- **Author:** [fuscof-ibm](https://github.com/fuscof-ibm)
- **Merged By:** [TheEpicDolphin](https://github.com/TheEpicDolphin)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 22:18:34
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68164/files) (2):**
  - `tests/v1/spec_decode/test_acceptance_estimator.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/spec_decode/acceptance_estimator.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The refit kernel in the online acceptance estimator previously masked only NaN steps (x == x), which allowed +inf slope steps through because w + inf >= 0 also passes the non-negative-slope guard. The fix replaces both guards with tl.abs(x) < float('inf'), which rejects NaN and ±inf while accepting all finite values, and adds two tests exercising the slope and intercept guards via OnlineAcceptanceEstimator.step().

**Technical impact**  
Behavioral change limited to the predicate of two existing tl.where guards in _refit_kernel; no new buffers, parameters, or host syncs. Finite steps are unaffected, and an infinite step now leaves that coefficient at its previous value while other coefficients still update, preventing a single infinite step from permanently poisoning the estimator with NaN predictions.

**Potential risks**  
The change is narrow and well-tested, but it overlaps with PR #66673 which clips steps to MAX_STEP_SLOPE/MAX_STEP_BIAS and keeps the x == x guard, so whichever lands second will have a small merge conflict on the two guard lines. The tests rely on fp32 rounding behavior (L2 cancellation at 2**16 moments) which could be sensitive to hardware or compiler differences, though the intercept test is more robust. No evidence of broader regression risk is provided beyond the targeted suite.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is subtle: a NaN-only guard is insufficient because +inf passes both the self-equality check and the non-negative-slope check, and once the slope becomes inf the intercepts follow to -inf and predictions become NaN, permanently masking all future updates. Using tl.abs(x) < inf is a clean, minimal predicate that correctly classifies NaN and ±inf as non-finite. The two fixes compose well with the clipping PR, and the tests are meaningful because they drive the real step() path and assert exact conditional updates.

## 13. [[Bugfix][Core] Fix Gemma-4 MTP prefix-cache hit loss when lookahead block hashes are off](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65263)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65263
- **Author:** [xuanzic](https://github.com/xuanzic)
- **Merged By:** [njhill](https://github.com/njhill)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 22:18:25
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65263/files) (2):**
  - `tests/test_config.py`
  - `vllm/config/speculative.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The PR fixes a prefix-cache hit loss for Gemma-4 MTP drafters by excluding them from the EAGLE trailing-block drop in needs_eagle_block_drop(). Gemma-4 MTP reads the target's KV and writes no draft KV of its own, so the EAGLE block drop was incorrectly applied, causing lost cache hits and a lookahead hash mismatch versus MTP0. A parametrized unit test covering gemma4_assistant and gemma4_unified_assistant checkpoint types was added.

**Technical impact**  
Changes behavior in vllm/config/speculative.py: Gemma-4 MTP no longer drops the last block on prefix-cache hits when disable_lookahead_block_hashes is set, and its default num_hash_lookahead_tokens effectively goes from 1 to 0, aligning block hashes with MTP0. This improves exact-boundary prefix-cache hits (e.g., N=2048/8192/16384 now fully cached) but also changes the block hashes Gemma-4 MTP produces, which affects hash-keyed external KV stores.

**Potential risks**  
The altered block hashes for Gemma-4 MTP could invalidate or mismatch existing external KV store entries keyed on prior hashes, requiring cache warmup. The fix is narrowly scoped to one drafter and overlaps with #66234 and #64875, so merge ordering matters; if those land first this PR reduces to the test. Correctness depends on the assumption that Gemma-4 MTP truly writes no KV, which the test asserts indirectly via config flags rather than runtime behavior.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is a coordinator fallback that flags all KV groups as EAGLE for KV-shared drafters, misapplying the EAGLE trailing-block drop. Excluding Gemma-4 MTP in needs_eagle_block_drop() is a targeted fix that also corrects the default lookahead hash count, which the more general fallback fix in #64875 does not address. Reported outputs and acceptance are unchanged, and the new test fails on main and passes with the change.

## 14. [[CI][Test] Ignore the key file path when checking that the watermark key is not quoted](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68592)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68592

- **Merged By:** [ZJY0516](https://github.com/ZJY0516)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 22:15:17
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68592/files) (1):**
  - `tests/test_config.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The test test_invalid_watermark_key_file_is_rejected_without_quoting_it now strips the key file path from the error message before asserting that the invalid key content is not quoted. This fixes a flaky CI failure where the temp dir path (e.g. /tmp/vllm-<job id suffix>) contained the substring '-5', causing the assertion to trip on the path rather than a leaked key.

**Technical impact**  
Test-only change with no production behavior impact. It preserves the test's intent: a message that quotes the file content still fails, while incidental path substrings no longer cause false failures. The fix is minimal and targeted.

**Potential risks**  
Low risk. The replace() approach assumes the path appears verbatim in the message; if the message formats the path differently (e.g. escaped repr), the strip could miss it, but the path is inserted via {path!r} so this is consistent. No coverage is lost since the content-not-quoted check remains.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is a substring collision between the invalid key content and the CI-generated temp directory name, affecting roughly 1 in 16 jobs. Stripping the known path before the assertion is a reasonable, localized fix for a flaky test.

## 15. [[Model Loader] Remove the torchao reload decorator](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68329)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68329

- **Merged By:** [aoshen02](https://github.com/aoshen02)

- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68329/files) (7):**

  - `vllm/model_executor/model_loader/reload/layerwise.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/model_loader/reload/torchao_decorator.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/model_loader/utils.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/utils.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Removes the torchao reload decorator (support_quantized_model_reload_from_hp_weights) and its supporting attributes (_do_torchao_reload, set_torchao_reload_attrs) that enabled reloading high-precision weights into an already-quantized torchao model via a direct model.load_weights call. The decorator is deleted, AutoWeightsLoader.load_weights is no longer decorated, process_weights_after_loading stops setting the reload attrs, and layerwise/sharded RDT engines drop the save/restore of the flag. The test that exercised this path is migrated to llm.collective_rpc("reload_weights").

**Technical impact**  
Eliminates a third, parallel reload lifecycle for torchao models and consolidates reload onto the shared reload_weights/weight-transfer path. This removes a special-case code path and the associated attribute bookkeeping in layerwise reload and the sharded RDT engine, simplifying the loader. torchao quantization, loading, and reload_weights behavior are stated to be unchanged.

**Potential risks**  
Any out-of-tree or downstream caller that relied on llm.apply_model(lambda m: m.load_weights(weights)) for torchao reload will break and must migrate to reload_weights or a weight-transfer engine. The decorator also ran model-level post-load hooks that the direct path now skips, so subtle behavioral differences could surface for users who depended on that path. Validation is limited to one test file on a single GB200 configuration, so broader regression coverage is thin.

**Key insights**  
This is a deliberate API/behavior removal tied to a larger effort (#54477) to unify load and update paths, with a documented migration path. The change is small in diff size but behavior-affecting, so it is correctly classified as substantial rather than trivial. Reviewers should confirm no remaining references to the removed symbols and that the migration guidance is surfaced in release notes or docs.

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a new XPU-specific eager Qwen3.5 model and MTP implementation (vllm/models/qwen3_5/xpu/) that replaces the torch.compile path on XPU, plus an opt-in specialized_decode package that fuses routed and shared MoE experts for small decode batches (1-8 rows) by calling the vllm-xpu-kernels provider directly. It also refactors XPUExpertsFp8 to separate weight initialization from apply, adds deployment qualification/guards, a CI smoke test, docs, and a config change enabling breakable cudagraphs for Qwen3.5 on XPU.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior changes on XPU: Qwen3.5 models now run eager with breakable graphs by default, and the MoE fusion path is gated behind additional_config decode_backend=latency_specialized with a narrow validated envelope (TP2, FP16, fp8_per_tensor, 40-layer MoE, 1-8 rows). The XPUExpertsFp8 refactor moves provider construction into initialize_weights/process_weights_after_loading, which affects all XPU FP8 MoE users, not just the new path. Reported gains are large for MoE B1 decode (roughly -20% TPOT) with near-neutral results elsewhere.

**Potential risks**  
Greedy outputs are not token-identical across configurations (e.g. 2/4 for 9B MTP), and accuracy drops exceed 1pp on GPQA-Diamond and AIME25, so correctness parity is not established. The fusion path is opt-in and narrowly qualified, but the shared XPUExpertsFp8 refactor touches the default MoE path and could regress other XPU models. CI coverage is limited to a single B1 layer smoke test; full-model, reload, and TP2 regressions are local-only. Remote CI for this revision is unconfirmed, and the specialized path depends on a specific kernel wheel version.

**Key insights**  
The design cleanly isolates XPU behavior and reuses stock routing, TP reduction, and KV/SSM state, with explicit fallback to stock MoE for ineligible batches and no device-to-host sync for planning. The main review focus should be the XPUExpertsFp8 weight-binding/rebinding logic (identity and pointer checks, reload interaction) and whether the accuracy deltas are acceptable or need further investigation before merge. The opt-in default mitigates risk but the shared refactor and the breakable-graph default change deserve broader XPU regression coverage.

## 17. [[RL][Weight Transfer] Packed transfer: one buffer-sized chunk layout for NCCL and IPC](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68043)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68043
- **Author:** [hao-aaron](https://github.com/hao-aaron)
- **Merged By:** [aoshen02](https://github.com/aoshen02)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 21:42:11
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68043/files) (9):**
  - `docs/training/weight_transfer/ipc.md`
  - `docs/training/weight_transfer/nccl.md`
  - `examples/rl/rlhf_http_ipc_no_vllm.py`
  - `examples/rl/rlhf_http_nccl_no_vllm.py`
  - `tests/distributed/test_packed_tensor.py`
  - `tests/distributed/test_weight_transfer.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/weight_transfer/ipc_engine.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/weight_transfer/nccl_engine.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/weight_transfer/packed_tensor.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR reworks the packed weight-transfer paths (NCCL and IPC) to share a single chunk layout derived from tensor metadata: an ALIGN(16)-byte-aligned byte stream cut every buffer_size, with tensors larger than the buffer split across chunks and joined on the receiver. It replaces pack_tensors/PackedChunk/unpack_tensor/packed_ipc_consumer/PackedBufferImporter with chunks()/Chunk/Piece, grow_buffer, _pack_copies, _Unpacker and PackedIpcConsumer, adds caller-stream ordering and in-order unpacking, fixes misaligned packed IPC entries, and updates examples, docs and tests accordingly.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior changes in both packed backends: producer buffers now grow dynamically (starting at 64 MiB, doubling, capped at packed_buffer_size_bytes) instead of being fixed-size; tensors exceeding the buffer are split rather than warned about or rejected; the IPC wire format changes (tensor_sizes removed, chunk_capacity and last added, handle only on the first chunk, per-chunk barriers); finish_weight_update now raises on a cut-short transfer; and buffer sizes under 16 bytes are rejected. Unpacked NCCL/IPC paths and the reload lifecycle are unchanged. The shared layout and copy helper reduce duplication between the two engines.

**Potential risks**  
The IPC wire format is incompatible with older trainers/workers, so mixed-version deployments would break. The new stream-ordering and buffer-growth logic is subtle (caller-stream waits, record_stream, doubling with carried-over bytes, caching-allocator retention of old buffers), and the PR itself notes the 16 GiB OOM scenario was not reproduced and AMD/ROCm is only covered by CI. The bounded-memory benefit is producer-side only; workers and relaying ranks still materialize full-size joined tensors. A failed update leaves the trainer buffer mapped until the next start or shutdown.

**Key insights**  
The core design win is a single metadata-derived layout shared by producer and consumer, which lets any tensor be split at chunk boundaries and removes the need for a buffer as large as the biggest tensor. Validation is thorough for the packed paths (oracle-based layout tests, quantized dtypes, staging-tensor reuse, ordering bug probes, IPC misalignment and cut-short cases, plus end-to-end gpt-oss-20b and Qwen3-0.6B runs), though the headline OOM fix is only exercised indirectly via a 64 MiB buffer on 1.16 GB expert stacks.

## 18. [[CPU] Warm up the remaining sampling kernels before serving](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64522)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #64522
- **Author:** [ganeshr10](https://github.com/ganeshr10)
- **Merged By:** [bigPYJ1151](https://github.com/bigPYJ1151)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 21:31:57
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64522/files) (6):**
  - `.buildkite/hardware_tests/cpu.yaml`
  - `tests/v1/worker/test_cpu_sampling_warmup.py`
  - `vllm/v1/sample/ops/topk_topp_sampler.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/cpu/shm.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/cpu_worker.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/model_runner.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR fixes CPU TTFT latency spikes caused by Triton sampling kernels compiling during the first requests after server start. It addresses two root causes: CPUWorker.compile_or_warm_up_model never invoked the JIT warmup registry, and async_tensor_h2d returned numpy-backed tensors whose pointer alignment (8 mod 16) differed from warmup, triggering Triton recompiles. The fix drives the registry directly on CPU, registers top-k/top-p warmups on CPU, and copies numpy inputs into aligned torch allocations.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior changes in CPU serving: sampling kernels are now precompiled at startup, eliminating 1-3 post-ready Triton compiles and reducing p95/p99 TTFT by roughly 1.3-1.5s at small request counts. The async_tensor_h2d change adds a copy for numpy inputs when no output buffer is provided, which slightly increases per-call overhead but guarantees 16-byte alignment and stable Triton specialization. The model_runner change also registers top-k/top-p warmups on CPU, which previously only happened on ROCm or CUDA with speculative decoding.

**Potential risks**  
The unconditional copy in async_tensor_h2d could add measurable overhead on hot paths that pass numpy arrays frequently, though these are typically small index/mapping tensors. The CPUWorker change assumes model_runner exposes jit_warmup_registry, guarded by a type ignore, so a runner variant lacking that attribute would fail at startup. The test monkeypatches platform predicates and worker internals, so it may not catch integration regressions in real CPU worker construction. Removing the is_cpu guard in register_top_k_top_p_warmups relies on HAS_TRITON being accurate on CPU builds.

**Key insights**  
The PR correctly identifies that Triton pointer-alignment specialization is a hidden source of unpredictable recompiles, which is a subtle and valuable insight beyond the registry wiring fix. The benchmark data is well-controlled (interleaved arms, same wheel/pod) and clearly shows the fix removes post-ready compiles across greedy, top-p, top-k, and combined configurations. The test coverage targets both root causes separately, which is good practice, though the alignment test only checks a single unaligned numpy view case.

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR temporarily retargets all MI300 DPX CI jobs from the mi300_dpx agent pool/device to the mi300_1 pool across 9 Buildkite YAML files, and renames the corresponding job labels from '(MI300 DPX)' to '(MI300)'. The change is explicitly a temporary workaround ahead of scheduled maintenance on the mi300_dpx queue and is intended to be reverted once maintenance completes.

**Technical impact**  
CI scheduling behavior changes: all affected AMD MI300 test jobs will now be dispatched to the mi300_1 queue instead of mi300_dpx. No test logic, source code, or runtime behavior is altered; only Buildkite agent_pool/device fields and display labels are updated consistently across the pipeline definitions.

**Potential risks**

Because this is a temporary change, there is a risk the revert is forgotten, leaving jobs permanently on the wrong pool. Label renaming may also break any external dashboards or tooling that key off the '(MI300 DPX)' label string.

**Key insights**  
The change is a mechanical, consistent find-and-replace of pool/device and label strings across all MI300 DPX job definitions, which reduces the chance of partial migration. Reviewers should confirm no mi300_dpx references remain and that the revert is tracked (e.g., via a follow-up issue). Since it is infra/config affecting CI behavior, it is classified as SUBSTANTIAL despite the low code risk.

## 20. [[Refactor] Move CUDA graph sizing policy into Platform](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68552)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68552
- **Author:** [Pb314314](https://github.com/Pb314314)
- **Merged By:** [Isotr0py](https://github.com/Isotr0py)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 21:19:50
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68552/files) (6):**
  - `docs/design/plugin_system.md`
  - `tests/compile/test_config.py`
  - `vllm/config/compilation.py`
  - `vllm/config/vllm.py`
  - `vllm/platforms/cuda.py`
  - `vllm/platforms/interface.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Refactors CUDA graph capture sizing so hardware-specific defaults and grid generation live behind new Platform extension points (get_cudagraph_capture_defaults plus prefill/decode/varlen grid methods), while shared config code retains user-setting precedence, limits, and shape calculation. A new CUDAGraphCaptureDefaults dataclass and validate_cudagraph_capture_grid helper were added, and CUDA's SM100 defaults moved into CudaPlatform. This also fixes a platform-selection bug where the capability-10 check wrongly matched ROCm gfx10xx.

**Technical impact**  
Introduces a documented plugin interface for capture sizing, moving SM100 selection out of VllmConfig into CudaPlatform and routing grid generation through overridable classmethods with validation of returned sizes. Behavior is intended to be identical for CUDA and unchanged for XPU, but gfx10xx now inherits traditional defaults (initial 512-token ceiling, no implicit 8192-token decode budget), which can enlarge or change captures for explicit split grids. Tests were updated to use a native-platform subclass and new regression cases cover overrides, invalid grids, and CUDA-only capability-10 ownership.

**Potential risks**  
The intentional gfx10xx behavior change may alter capture sizes and memory usage for ROCm users relying on the former implicit 8192-token decode budget, requiring explicit settings to restore it. AMD/XPU physical validation is still outstanding, so platform-specific regressions may be undetected. The new interface relies on platforms returning deterministic, in-range grids; validation rejects bad output but a misbehaving override could still change capture behavior. Test monkeypatching of current_platform could mask real platform-selection issues if not representative.

**Key insights**  
The refactor cleanly separates hardware policy from shared configuration rules and fixes a genuine ROCm/CUDA capability collision. Validation is thorough for CUDA (27 config cases, GB200 generation with and without drafting, controlled failure of the new adaptive test), but ROCm/XPU coverage is limited to unit-level tests. The documented migration path for gfx10xx users is a good mitigation, though the behavior change should be highlighted in release notes.

## 21. [[Bugfix][Frontend] Preserve DP rank in beam search and speech-to-text requests](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67001)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #67001
- **Author:** [Chr0mium24](https://github.com/Chr0mium24)
- **Merged By:** [DarkLight1337](https://github.com/DarkLight1337)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 21:05:00
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67001/files) (7):**

  - `tests/entrypoints/openai/completion/test_completion_error.py`
  - `tests/entrypoints/speech_to_text/test_speech_to_text_cancellation.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/generate/beam_search/online.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/openai/chat_completion/serving.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/openai/completion/serving.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/speech_to_text/base/serving.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The PR forwards the parsed X-data-parallel-rank header as data_parallel_rank to engine_client.generate for online beam search (chat completions and completions) and for speech-to-text requests, including language detection and per-chunk generation. Previously these paths dropped the rank, so the engine fell back to its own DP load balancing, unlike chat/completions non-beam paths and the Responses API. Source change is small (about 15 lines) plus three new tests.

**Technical impact**  
Adds an optional data_parallel_rank parameter to BeamSearchOnlineMixin.beam_search, SpeechToTextBaseServing._preprocess_speech_to_text and _detect_language, and threads it through to engine_client.generate. Behavior is unchanged when the header is absent (default None), so existing routing is preserved. This aligns beam search and speech-to-text with the already-established DP rank routing used by other endpoints.

**Potential risks**  
Validation stops at the data_parallel_rank argument of engine_client.generate; actual serving with --data-parallel-size > 1 was not exercised, so end-to-end routing correctness relies on the shared code path. The per-chunk speech-to-text pass-throughs lack dedicated tests, and the realtime WebSocket transcription path, pooling (encode), and generative scoring remain unaddressed, leaving partial coverage of DP rank propagation. The 10 TestGPTOSSChat fixture errors are pre-existing and unrelated.

**Key insights**  
This is a focused consistency fix mirroring prior PRs (#62450, #62253) that propagate request headers to the engine. Tests are well-constructed, using real Request objects and the real beam_search with a mocked engine, and they fail on base and pass with the fix. The main gap is the absence of multi-DP integration testing, which should be considered for CI or follow-up validation.

## 22. [[Bugfix] Keep Marlin out of MXFP4 linear selection under VLLM_BATCH_INVARIANT](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68008)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68008
- **Author:** [sfeng33](https://github.com/sfeng33)
- **Merged By:** [sfeng33](https://github.com/sfeng33)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 21:01:09
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68008/files) (5):**
  - `tests/kernels/quantization/test_mxfp4_kernel_selection.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/kernels/linear/__init__.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/kernels/linear/mxfp4/emulation.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/kernels/linear/mxfp4/flashinfer.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/kernels/linear/mxfp4/humming.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a supports_batch_invariance() capability to the MXFP4 linear kernels (FlashInfer, Humming, Emulation return True; Marlin does not) and filters out non-batch-invariant kernels in init_mxfp4_linear_kernel when VLLM_BATCH_INVARIANT is set on CUDA. This mirrors the earlier MXFP8 fix and prevents Marlin, whose output varies with batch size, from being selected under batch-invariant mode. A new parametrized selection test covers both auto and explicit marlin backends.

**Technical impact**  
Under VLLM_BATCH_INVARIANT=1 on CUDA, MXFP4 layers now select only FlashInfer, Humming, or Emulation in existing priority order; an explicit --linear-backend marlin falls back to automatic selection with the existing warning. Behavior is unchanged with the flag off, on ROCm/XPU, and for default Blackwell/Hopper selections. The filter runs before backend resolution, so it composes cleanly with the existing --linear-backend logic.

**Potential risks**  
Users who explicitly requested marlin under batch-invariant mode silently get a different kernel, and the PR shows gsm8k accuracy shifting from ~0.648 to ~0.521 because Marlin is weight-only (W4A16) while FlashInfer runs W4A4; this is a meaningful accuracy change for that configuration. The filter is gated on platform is CUDA, so any future CUDA-like platform or non-CUDA path relying on the same invariant would not be covered. The new test relies on heavy monkeypatching of Humming internals, which could become brittle if those classmethods are renamed.

**Key insights**  
The change is small and well-targeted, reusing the established MXFP8 pattern and adding a focused regression test that fails without the two-line filter. The perf numbers show the fallback is also faster on B200, but the accuracy drop for explicit marlin users is the main behavioral tradeoff and is documented transparently. Overall a sound bugfix with clear scope and good test coverage.

## 23. [[Bugfix][Multimodal] Size image placeholders from request-level processor kwargs in DeepSeek-OCR/VL2, LightOnOCR and Molmo2](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68127)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68127
- **Author:** [Jaeyeong-CHOI](https://github.com/Jaeyeong-CHOI)
- **Merged By:** [DarkLight1337](https://github.com/DarkLight1337)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 20:43:17
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68127/files) (9):**
  - `tests/models/multimodal/processing/test_deepseek_ocr.py`
  - `tests/models/multimodal/processing/test_deepseek_vl2.py`
  - `tests/models/multimodal/processing/test_lightonocr.py`
  - `tests/models/multimodal/processing/test_molmo2.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/deepseek_ocr.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/deepseek_ocr2.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/deepseek_vl2.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/lightonocr.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/molmo2.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes a bug where per-request mm_processor_kwargs overrides (crop_mode, candidate_resolutions, size, max_crops) changed the HF processor output but the prompt placeholder count was still recomputed from engine-level settings, causing mismatched placeholders and EngineDeadError or processor assertion failures. The adapters for DeepSeek-OCR/OCR-2, DeepSeek-VL2, LightOnOCR, and Molmo2 now derive placeholder sizes from the actual processor output (num_image_tokens, request-configured hf_processor, resized pixel_values shape, and image_num_crops/image_tokens respectively).

**Technical impact**  
DeepSeek-OCR/OCR-2 add a CPU-side batched num_image_tokens field and read it in the replacement; DeepSeek-VL2 threads the request hf_processor into get_num_image_tokens; LightOnOCR computes token count from pixel_values shape and patch/merge stride; Molmo2 reuses processor-returned image_num_crops and image_tokens, removing duplicated tiling logic (net -57 lines). Engine-level kwargs and default behavior are claimed unchanged, and new processing tests compare per-request vs engine-wide kwargs on real processors.

**Potential risks**  
Validation was limited to SM121 with specific checkpoints; other variants in these model families share the code paths but were not exercised. The new num_image_tokens field for DeepSeek-OCR/OCR-2 adds a required processor output field, so any alternate processor path or embedding input that omits it could break. Molmo2's removal of tiling logic relies on the processor's image_num_crops being consistent with pixel_values, guarded only by an assertion. End-to-end tests are not included in the PR, so regression coverage for the actual engine failure relies on the processing-level tests.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is a general pattern: placeholder sizing must follow the request-configured processor output rather than re-deriving from engine defaults, matching prior fixes like #68096. Deriving counts from actual processor outputs is more robust than duplicating sizing logic and also simplifies Molmo2. The tests are well-targeted, checking prompt ids, placeholder lengths against features, and per-request vs engine-wide equivalence, but broader checkpoint coverage would strengthen confidence.

## 24. [[Model Loader] Load plan: plan copy-time casts and setitem writes as direct; faster overlap check](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67836)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #67836
- **Author:** [aoshen02](https://github.com/aoshen02)
- **Merged By:** [aoshen02](https://github.com/aoshen02)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 20:38:20
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67836/files) (4):**
  - `tests/model_executor/model_loader/test_load_plan.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/model_loader/reload/geometry.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/model_loader/reload/plan.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/model_loader/reload/trace.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The load plan now treats two previously-fallback loader patterns as direct writes: cross-dtype copies (recording the destination dtype and replaying the conversion, with equal-width integer casts kept as plain byte copies) and basic-index `param[...] = weight` setitem, which is recorded as a view copy while advanced indexing still falls back. `load_planned` batches direct copies into one `torch._foreach_copy_` per ~2 GiB of source with pair-by-pair retry on failure, and the overlap check now sweeps byte spans first and expands only intersecting copies into runs. This targets the weight-update speed work in #54477, with large reported latency reductions.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior changes in the hot reload path: more tensors avoid the Python `load_weights` fallback, copies are issued in batches rather than per tensor, and overlap detection is restructured (span sweep plus cached sorted runs and a two-pointer intersection test). New `byte_span` helper is shared with `byte_runs`. The batched-copy contract now requires each yielded source tensor to remain unchanged until `load_planned` returns, which is a new caller obligation documented in the docstring.

**Potential risks**  
Batching holds up to ~2 GiB of source tensors alive and changes the failure surface: a single bad tensor now fails a whole batch before the pair-by-pair retry isolates it, and the retry re-copies already-succeeded pairs (idempotent but extra work). The setitem fast path relies on view/storage identity checks and `_is_basic_index` correctness; subtle index forms could be misclassified. The overlap rewrite must preserve exact semantics, and the new span-based prefilter plus run intersection is more complex than the prior run-based sweep. Correctness of dtype-conversion replay for exotic casts is asserted but only lightly covered by tests.

**Key insights**  
The PR is well-scoped with targeted tests for batching flush boundaries, last-write-wins, retry-on-failure, interleaved column overlap, and setitem direct/fallback cases. The performance claims are substantial and backed by before/after tables and GSM8K parity checks, though the DSv4.1 colocated score moved within the stated pre-update spread. Reviewers should focus on the setitem view-detection edge cases, the overlap-check equivalence, and the new source-buffer lifetime contract for streaming callers.

## 25. [[Bugfix] Apply max_tokens caps to explicit null on generate](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/66233)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #66233
- **Author:** [xu797](https://github.com/xu797)
- **Merged By:** [DarkLight1337](https://github.com/DarkLight1337)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 20:33:50
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/66233/files) (2):**
  - `tests/entrypoints/scale_out/token_in_token_out/test_serving_tokens.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/scale_out/token_in_token_out/serving.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The serving path for /inference/v1/generate previously applied server-side max_tokens defaults and caps only when max_tokens was omitted, so an explicit max_tokens: null bypassed default_sampling_params, --override-generation-config max_new_tokens, and the platform output-token cap, letting generation run to the model context limit. The fix extends the get_max_tokens resolution to also trigger when sampling_params.max_tokens is None, and re-validates min_tokens <= max_tokens after the fill, returning HTTP 400 on violation. Two tests were added covering the explicit-null cap and the min_tokens-above-server-cap rejection.

**Technical impact**  
Explicit null max_tokens now behaves identically to an omitted field, aligning with the issue's Option A semantics and closing a guardrail bypass. Integer max_tokens and fully omitted requests are unchanged. The added post-fill min_tokens validation is necessary because SamplingParams validation runs before the server-side fill mutates max_tokens, so the ordering matters for correctness.

**Potential risks**  
Behavior change for clients that relied on explicit null meaning 'no cap' could surprise users, though this is the intended fix. The new 400 on min_tokens exceeding the resolved cap is a new rejection path that could affect existing callers. The condition relies on is_sampling_param_provided and max_tokens is None being consistent; edge cases where max_tokens is explicitly set to a non-null value but min_tokens still exceeds it are not re-validated here, so coverage depends on existing SamplingParams checks.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is a subtle distinction between 'field absent' and 'field present but null' in request parsing, which the fix correctly collapses for cap resolution. Re-validating min_tokens after the fill is a good catch since validation ordering would otherwise miss the mutated value. Tests are targeted and use the override-generation-config path to prove the cap is actually enforced rather than just defaulted.

## 26. [[Fast Start] Remove copy mode for fast start IPC loader](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68388)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68388
- **Author:** [Isotr0py](https://github.com/Isotr0py)
- **Merged By:** [jeejeelee](https://github.com/jeejeelee)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 20:32:55
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68388/files) (8):**
  - `docs/features/preload.md`
  - `tests/kernels/moe/test_flashinfer.py`
  - `tests/model_executor/model_loader/test_weight_cache.py`
  - `tests/v1/worker/test_gpu_worker.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/model_loader/weight_cache/daemon.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/model_loader/weight_cache/ipc_loader.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/model_loader/weight_cache/utils.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/model_loader/weight_cache/weight_seed/seed.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Removes the 'copy' mode from the ipc_cache fast-start loader, leaving only zero-copy CUDA IPC mapping. Copy mode doubled VRAM usage and slowed startup versus cold start, so the loader now always shares the daemon's GPU memory. The PR also drops standalone in-place weight reload (load_weights now raises NotImplementedError), removes the daemon 'release' command and the daemon_lost watch event, and reworks shutdown to use a lease-based drain with a bounded timeout instead of a release thread. External weight charge accounting is simplified from (socket_path, is_draft) keys to socket_path only, and docs/tests are updated accordingly.

**Technical impact**  
Behavioral and API changes across the loader, daemon, and accounting layers. The 'mode' extra-config key is no longer accepted, so users passing mode=copy will now hit the unexpected-keys ValueError. Standalone reload paths are removed, meaning any caller relying on IpcModelLoader.load_weights for in-place reload will fail. Daemon shutdown now stops accepting new copy leases, drains acknowledgements up to WEIGHT_COPY_LEASE_TIMEOUT_S, and raises TimeoutError if copies remain, with the launcher allowing 30s (or 10s after rank failure) before killing ranks. The _state_lock RLock is replaced by a dedicated lease lock, changing concurrency semantics for seed resolution and state export.

**Potential risks**  
Removing copy mode is a breaking change for deployments that depended on the daemon freeing GPU memory after handoff; those users must now keep the daemon resident or accept a second weight copy on restart. The NotImplementedError in load_weights could surface as a hard failure in reload flows that previously worked. The shutdown rework changes timing and error propagation: a lease timeout now raises from _shutdown rather than being collected, and the launcher's kill grace period must match the new lease timeout or ranks may be killed mid-drain. Dropping _state_lock may introduce races between seed resolution and state export that were previously serialized. The PR description provides no validation evidence, so the correctness of these concurrency and shutdown changes is unverified from the provided information.

**Key insights**  
The change trades flexibility for startup performance and memory predictability by standardizing on zero-copy, which is the documented default and the only mode that avoids duplicating VRAM. The lease-based shutdown is a cleaner design than the previous release thread plus state lock, but it couples daemon shutdown latency to the lease timeout and requires the launcher's kill grace to be kept in sync. The simplified charge accounting is a reasonable cleanup since is_draft was redundant with the socket path. Reviewers should focus on the removed reload path's callers, the new lock's coverage of _seed_state/_export_state, and whether the 30s/10s launcher grace periods are consistent with WEIGHT_COPY_LEASE_TIMEOUT_S.

---

## 27. [[Fast Start] Support compressed-tensors WNA16 pre-processed weights](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68328)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68328
- **Author:** [UNIDY2002](https://github.com/UNIDY2002)
- **Merged By:** [Isotr0py](https://github.com/Isotr0py)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 20:12:46
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68328/files) (2):**
  - `docs/features/preload.md`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/quantization/compressed_tensors/schemes/compressed_tensors_wNa16.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds Fast Start pre-processed weight support for compressed-tensors WNA16 dense linear layers, gated to the Marlin kernel with FP16/BF16 activations. It introduces a supports_pre_processed_weights property, registers an empty symmetric zero-point parameter (w_zp) during create_weights, and short-circuits process_weights_after_loading when weights are already pre-processed, raising an error for unsupported backends. Docs are updated to describe the new scope and exclusions.

**Technical impact**  
Enables reuse of Marlin-packed WNA16 weights and scales across CUDA IPC engine restarts, avoiding a second repack on the pre-processed path. The change touches weight creation and post-load processing in the WNA16 scheme, so it affects the quantization load path for a specific backend/activation combination while leaving other paths unchanged.

**Potential risks**  
The new w_zp registration and w_zp_name override could interact with existing symmetric-weight handling or other kernels if the Marlin-only guard is ever bypassed. The pre-processed path returns early, so any future post-load logic added to this method must be explicitly handled for pre-processed weights. Validation is limited to an external GPU parity test on one checkpoint; no upstream CI or quantization-quality benchmark is claimed, and Humming, Machete, Exllama, batch-invariant dequant, and WNA16 MoE remain unsupported.

**Key insights**  
The capability is correctly scoped as a kernel property rather than a global flag, keeping the feature opt-in per backend. Registering the empty zero-point before attaching processed tensors is a subtle but necessary ordering detail for Marlin. The explicit RuntimeError on unsupported pre-processed configurations provides a clear failure mode instead of silent misbehavior.

## 28. [[Perf][Step5] Keep PrimTS SWA verify batches on the decode plan](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68129)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68129
- **Author:** [JaredforReal](https://github.com/JaredforReal)
- **Merged By:** [Isotr0py](https://github.com/Isotr0py)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 20:08:47
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68129/files) (3):**
  - `tests/models/step5/test_prims_ts_attention.py`
  - `vllm/models/step5/nvidia/prims_ts.py`
  - `vllm/models/step5/nvidia/sparse_attention.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Step5PrimsTSSWA previously selected its paged decode plan only for uniform batches with qlen <= 4, causing speculative verify batches with more draft tokens to fall back to the slower prefill plan. This PR threads num_speculative_tokens into the SWA constructor and raises the decode threshold to max(4, num_speculative_tokens + 1), plus adds a test covering a five-token verify batch against an FP32 reference under eager and CUDA graph replay.

**Technical impact**  
Keeps speculative verify batches of every draft length on the faster decode plan, which the PR reports as 1.6-22x faster than the prefill plan in a microbenchmark, yielding 8.2% throughput at concurrency 64 and 3.6% at concurrency 16 with FP8 KV and MTP num_speculative_tokens=6. Non-speculative uniform batches up to four tokens retain their current plan, and BF16 KV is unaffected since routing already sends those batches to the native path. The threshold stays bounded because the decode plan is keyed by exact request count and query length.

**Potential risks**  
The new test skips in CI on H200 and MI355 because it requires SM100/SM103/SM107, so the behavior is only validated out-of-band on GB300, leaving a coverage gap for regressions. Correctness depends on the decode plan producing identical results to the prefill plan at larger query lengths, which is asserted only at 5 and 8 tokens in the PR description. The change also relies on the merged #66951 for the MTP=6 configuration to start at all, so it is coupled to that fix.

**Key insights**  
The fix is a small, well-scoped threshold change with a clear performance rationale and a targeted regression test that fails on the old threshold. The main review concern is CI coverage: the relevant tests skip on the platforms CI runs, so reviewers should weigh the documented GB300 validation and consider whether broader hardware coverage is feasible. Output parity and acceptance-rate claims are within noise, which supports the safety of the change.

## 29. [[Model] Inkling 2: Hopper (SM90) support](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67880)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #67880
- **Author:** [WoosukKwon](https://github.com/WoosukKwon)
- **Merged By:** [WoosukKwon](https://github.com/WoosukKwon)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 20:07:12

- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67880/files) (19):**
  - `CMakeLists.txt`
