  - `tests/models/inkling2/test_attention.py`
  - `tests/models/inkling2/test_dspark.py`
  - `tests/models/inkling2/test_model.py`
  - `tests/models/inkling2/test_moe.py`
  - `tests/models/inkling2/test_vision.py`
  - `vllm/models/inkling2/nvidia/attention.py`
  - `vllm/models/inkling2/nvidia/moe.py`
  - `vllm/models/inkling2/nvidia/ops/__init__.py`
  - `vllm/models/inkling2/nvidia/ops/allreduce.py`
  - `vllm/models/inkling2/nvidia/ops/attention.py`
  - `vllm/models/inkling2/nvidia/ops/attention_sm90.py`
  - `vllm/models/inkling2/nvidia/ops/linear.py`
  - `vllm/models/inkling2/nvidia/ops/moe_sm90.py`
  - `vllm/models/inkling2/nvidia/ops/nvfp4.py`
  - `vllm/models/inkling2/nvidia/ops/trtllm_bmm.py`
  - `vllm/models/inkling2/nvidia/vision.py`
  - `vllm/models/inkling2/quant_config.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds Hopper (SM90) support for Inkling 2 Small by introducing Triton replacements for the Blackwell-only kernels: a paged relative-bias attention kernel (ops/attention_sm90.py), expert GEMMs (ops/moe_sm90.py), software e2m1 rounding in ops/nvfp4.py, and scalar fallbacks in conv_mix.cu. Dispatch is gated on a new cached use_sm100_kernels() helper, the CMake arch list gains 9.0a, and the min capability drops from 100 to 90. Tests are broadened to run on Hopper.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior on Blackwell is intended to be unchanged since every Hopper path is gated on use_sm100_kernels(). On Hopper the model now runs via Triton attention, Triton expert GEMMs on row-major weights (skipping the shuffle/swizzle weight transforms), cuBLAS small linears, FA3 vision attention, and the multicast all-reduce. The attention refactor extracts the SM100 verify launch into _launch_rel_verify and shares the combine kernel across both paths.

**Potential risks**  
The CMakeLists arch change has not been through a full source build, and validation predates the rebase onto main, so the merged state is unverified on H200. The Hopper MoE GEMMs are notably slower (~180 TFLOPS prefill) and the software e2m1 rounding and scalar conv_mix fallbacks add overhead. Two DSpark multi-GPU tests hang under pytest Gloo setup, and no Inkling2 tests run in CI, so regressions could slip through. Correctness of the exact-dequantization and tile-invariance claims rests on the author's H200 runs.

**Key insights**  
The design cleanly isolates Hopper code under nvidia/ops with a single capability gate, keeping Blackwell paths intact. The attention kernel's interleaved KV splits are chosen so split counts depend only on batch, preserving CUDA graph replay stability. The main follow-ups are a full source build of the 9.0a arch, re-running validation post-rebase, and adding CI coverage for tests/models/inkling2.

## 30. [[Bugfix][NIXL] Push: stop WRITE submission stalls and early block release; lock the NIXL agent](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/66795)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #66795
- **Author:** [zyongye](https://github.com/zyongye)
- **Merged By:** [ivanium](https://github.com/ivanium)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 20:06:48
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/66795/files) (5):**
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/test_nixl_connector.py`
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/test_nixl_push_connector.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/nixl/base_worker.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/nixl/push_worker.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/nixl_utils.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes NIXL push-mode (P/D disaggregation) stalls and a use-after-free risk under sustained load. Push now defaults to UCX num_threads=0 so WRITE posting runs in the caller instead of blocking on the UCX thread pool, both modes create the NIXL agent with NIXL_THREAD_SYNC_RW to make the agent lock real, and push send leases are held (and TTL eviction of peers deferred) while WRITEs are being submitted or in flight. A registration arriving after a request's blocks were released is dropped instead of pushed.

**Technical impact**  
Changes default agent configuration (num_threads 4->0 for push, sync_mode set for both modes) and adds cross-thread state (_inflight_pushes, _next_held_lease_check) guarded by _sending_transfers_lock. _do_start_push_kv is split into a guarded wrapper plus _start_push_kv, and _reap_expired_send_leases, _evict_stale_engines and _engines_with_inflight_transfers are overridden in the push worker. The e2e run shows 0 slow submissions, 0 UCX endpoint timeouts, 0 remote disconnects and 0 segfaults versus aborted unpatched runs, though the before/after comparison is not fully isolated.

**Potential risks**  
A WRITE that never completes (e.g. UCX error handling disabled) now keeps its blocks leased indefinitely, only surfaced by a 30s warning, which could cause memory pressure or stalls. NIXL_THREAD_SYNC_RW serializes calls during loadRemoteMD handshakes, adding latency once per remote rank per handshake, and pull-mode locking has no e2e performance validation yet. The segfault root cause is not proven, and the sync_mode assignment is skipped silently if the installed NIXL lacks nixl_thread_sync_t. Locking discipline around _sending_transfers_lock across writer and engine threads is subtle and a source of potential deadlock or missed-release bugs.

**Key insights**  
The core bug was that a send lease could expire and free blocks while their WRITE was still being submitted or running, letting the scheduler reuse memory on the wire; the fix ties lease expiry and peer eviction to in-flight push tracking. The unlocked NIXL agent (no sync mode makes locks no-ops) is a plausible cause of the observed crashes/hangs, and RW is chosen over STRICT to keep posting concurrent with status checks. Test coverage is strong, including a test that fails on main, but the e2e evidence predates some changes and the pull-mode locking lacks a performance run.

## 31. [[XPU][Test] Enable Ray-based test_comm_ops tests on XPU](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/66975)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #66975
- **Author:** [yisustc](https://github.com/yisustc)
- **Merged By:** [jikunshang](https://github.com/jikunshang)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 19:27:28
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/66975/files) (2):**
  - `tests/distributed/test_comm_ops.py`
  - `tests/utils.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The PR enables Ray-based test_comm_ops tests to run on XPU by replacing hard-coded 'cuda' device strings with current_platform.device_type in worker functions, and by explicitly declaring num_gpus in multi_process_parallel on XPU so Ray workers do not depend on dpctl for XPU resource detection. This fixes failures where 'cuda' mismatched the XPU accelerator and where @ray.remote(num_gpus=1) workers hung indefinitely.

**Technical impact**  
Test-only changes that generalize device selection across CUDA, ROCm, and XPU while preserving existing CUDA/ROCm behavior since current_platform.device_type resolves to 'cuda' there. The Ray init now conditionally passes num_gpus only on XPU, avoiding resource declaration changes on other platforms. No production code paths are affected.

**Potential risks**  
The fix relies on current_platform.device_count() correctly reflecting available XPUs at Ray init time; if it over- or under-reports, workers may fail to schedule or oversubscribe. Using device_type with rank as index assumes a one-to-one rank-to-device mapping, which holds for these tests but could be fragile if device visibility differs. Limited validation details are provided, so XPU CI coverage is unclear.

**Key insights**  
The change is a targeted portability fix for test infrastructure rather than core logic, and the conditional num_gpus declaration is a reasonable workaround for Ray's dpctl-dependent XPU detection. Reviewers should confirm XPU CI actually exercises these tests and that device_count matches the Ray-visible GPU set.

## 32. [[XPU] SamplingMaskTensors use sycl kernel on XPU](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64231)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #64231
- **Author:** [mayuyuace](https://github.com/mayuyuace)
- **Merged By:** [jikunshang](https://github.com/jikunshang)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 19:26:48
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64231/files) (2):**
  - `tests/v1/test_outputs.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/sample/output.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The PR refactors SamplingMaskTensors.from_logits to dispatch between the existing Triton kernel and a new SYCL kernel (torch.ops._xpu_C.compact_sampling_mask) on XPU platforms, extracting the Triton path into a helper. It also removes the XPU skipif marker from test_sampling_mask_tensors_match_finite_support, enabling that test on XPU now that a SYCL kernel is available.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior on non-XPU platforms is unchanged since the Triton logic is moved verbatim into _compact_sampling_mask_triton. On XPU, mask computation now relies on the external vllm-xpu-kernels SYCL op, which the description notes depends on two upstream PRs (582, 673) being merged first. The platform check adds a runtime branch per call, which is negligible.

**Potential risks**  
The XPU path depends on torch.ops._xpu_C.compact_sampling_mask existing and matching the Triton kernel's semantics (clamping max_num_kept to vocab_size and MAX_COMPACT_SUPPORT, output shapes, strides, rows_per_request handling); any mismatch could silently produce incorrect masks. There is no fallback if the XPU op is unavailable, and the dependency on the vllm-xpu-kernels PRs creates a merge-ordering constraint. Test coverage on XPU is only validated via the un-skipped unit test and the attached UT screenshot.

**Key insights**  
The refactor cleanly isolates backend-specific implementations behind a small dispatch, keeping the public API stable. Reviewers should verify the SYCL op applies the same max_num_kept clamping (min with vocab_size and MAX_COMPACT_SUPPORT) that the Triton helper does, since the XPU call passes MAX_COMPACT_SUPPORT directly and relies on the kernel to clamp. Confirm the upstream kernel PRs are merged before landing.

**What changed and why**  
The DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash recipe YAML is updated to add a new gb300_agentx_tp preset (TP4, Dynamo, concurrency 1/4/8) and to resync the gb300_agentx_pd preset to InferenceX #5015 head 1b1eec0, replacing the superseded #3941 settings. Both presets pin a new nightly aarch64 image plus Dynamo 1.6.0.dev20261010, and the GB300 guide sections are rewritten to point at the presets while stale #3941/#4266 settings are removed. B300 content is untouched.

**Technical impact**  
Changes rendered serving configurations for GB300: new EPLB, Engram peer-group splitting, NIXL push over NVLink, lazy host-KV offload, per-point CUDA graph sizes and KV-load limits, plus a new TP4 lane with latency-specialized decode. It also alters preset metadata (variants narrowed to fp8, frontend rust, tool_calling/reasoning disabled for PD since Dynamo parses in its frontend) and adds a json_args mechanism to drop rejection_sample_method for PD. Dropping tool_calling/reasoning and rejection_sample_method is intentional but could surprise consumers expecting those features. Validation evidence is pre-existing and not rerun for this PR.

**Key insights**  
This is a documentation/recipe-only change with no core vLLM code impact, but it materially changes the rendered serving configs, so it is classified SUBSTANTIAL. The PR is thorough: it documents renderer-vs-lane differences, benchmark and GSM8K evidence, and the multi-worker runbook caveat. Reviewers should focus on whether the pinned image/revision is acceptable while #5015 is unmerged and whether the single-worker rendering adequately conveys the real deployment topology.

## 34. [[ROCm] wvSplitK: keep bf16 N=5 at YTILE 2 on wave64 to avoid VGPR spills](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/66444)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #66444
- **Author:** [XiaobingSuper](https://github.com/XiaobingSuper)
- **Merged By:** [AndreasKaratzas](https://github.com/AndreasKaratzas)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 19:13:05
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/66444/files) (2):**
  - `csrc/rocm/skinny_gemms_wvsplitk.cu`
  - `tests/kernels/quantization/test_rocm_skinny_gemms.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a branch in the wvSplitK tile-selection macro so that bf16 at N=5 on wave64 (64 threads) always uses YTILE=2, UNRL=2 instead of the larger YTILE=3/4 rows, which spill VGPRs on gfx942/gfx950. The change is gated on std::is_same_v<fptype, __hip_bfloat16> so fp16 and wave32 keep their existing tiles. Two new N=5 test cases (4096x10240, 4096x16384) are added to exercise the previously untested YTILE 3/4 rows.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior change is confined to bf16 N=5 wave64 launches with out_dim > 8192; all other dtype/N/wave-size/shape combinations dispatch the same kernel instantiation as before. The PR reports 1.9-5.7x speedups on MI355X for affected shapes with bit-identical outputs, and the tile chosen is already instantiated so no new kernel or dispatch path is introduced. The added type_traits include and compile-time check are cheap and resolved at build time.

**Potential risks**  
Only gfx950 was timed; gfx942 behavior is inferred from register-usage analysis and gfx90a still spills (though less than before), so real-world gains on MI300X/MI250X are unverified. The K=512 large-out_dim shapes remain slower than hipBLASLt and are explicitly deferred. The change is a heuristic tied to specific architectures and CU counts, so future tile-table or compiler changes could invalidate the spill assumption without a test catching it (tests only check correctness, not launched tile).

**Key insights**  
The fix is a minimal, well-scoped dispatch tweak rather than a new kernel, and the bf16-only gating is justified by the f32-widening dot product that causes the spills. The PR is unusually well-evidenced with register-usage tables, profiler-confirmed launched instantiations, and bit-identical output checks. The main residual gap is that performance validation is single-architecture and the new tests verify correctness but not the selected tile, so regressions in tile selection would go uncaught in CI.

## 35. [[ROCm][Muse Spark] Online MXFP4 target for the BF16 first/last-layer experts](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67191)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #67191
- **Author:** [mawong-amd](https://github.com/mawong-amd)
- **Merged By:** [shen-shanshan](https://github.com/shen-shanshan)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 19:10:28
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67191/files) (5):**
  - `docs/features/quantization/online.md`
  - `tests/kernels/moe/test_muse_spark_rocm_bf16_moe.py`
  - `tests/models/test_muse_spark_rocm_loading.py`
  - `vllm/models/muse_spark/amd/model.py`
  - `vllm/models/muse_spark/amd/muse_moe_runner.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds opt-in online MXFP4 quantization for the BF16 first/last-layer routed experts of the Muse Spark ROCm model, so layers 0 and 61 run the same AITER W4A4 per-route experts (with post-expert norm) as the other layers. The ROCm model now accepts the generic OnlineQuantizationConfig, restricts it to routed experts with mxfp4 as the only spec, rejects online-quantized linear layers, and accumulates rounding time into the online summary. Docs and tests are added for the new path and rejected cases.

**Technical impact**  
Changes MoE method selection in muse_moe_runner._get_quant_method to consult online targets (both standalone and composed onto QuarkConfig), adds a _online_quant_method mapper that validates source/spec and rejects re-quantizing already-quantized experts or non-W4A4 specs, and adds a model-init guard rejecting online-quantized linears. Behavior is unchanged when no targets are supplied, but targeted layers switch numerics and run a different kernel path.

**Potential risks**  
The feature is a numerics change (opt-in) and adds KV cache memory (+10.8 GiB per rank per the description). The new validation logic depends on internal Quark/online APIs (resolve_quant_method_cls, get_quant_method_target, quant key comparisons) that could drift. Error paths and the linear-rejection check are only lightly covered by CPU tests, and the GPU test is nightly/optional, so regressions may not surface in standard CI.

**Key insights**  
Reuses the existing generic online-quantization mechanism rather than adding new config fields, keeping the change localized to the Muse ROCm model and MoE runner. The design correctly refuses generic online MoE methods because they lack Muse's per-route post-expert norm, falling back only to the Muse AITER W4A4 path. Reported results show modest decode/serving gains with negligible accuracy change, and tests explicitly verify that ignoring the target causes a large mismatch, confirming the test is meaningful.

## 36. [[Perf][Frontend] Seed the fast detokenizer with a prompt tail, not the full prompt](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67153)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #67153
- **Author:** [mawong-amd](https://github.com/mawong-amd)
- **Merged By:** [shen-shanshan](https://github.com/shen-shanshan)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 19:09:10
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67153/files) (2):**
  - `tests/detokenizer/test_decode_stream_seed.py`
  - `vllm/v1/engine/detokenizer.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The fast detokenizer previously seeded DecodeStream with the entire prompt, causing the first output token to decode the whole prompt on the output-processing loop and stalling other streams (17-40 ms at 100k tokens). This PR adds get_decode_stream_seed, which seeds DecodeStream with a short prompt tail (5 text-bearing tokens, extended up to 3 tokens if the tail text ends in U+FFFD, falling back to the full prompt if the prompt itself ends in U+FFFD or the walk exceeds 256 tokens), plus a large new test file validating that tail seeding produces identical deltas to full-prompt seeding across many tokenizers and edge cases.

**Technical impact**  
Changes the initialization path of FastIncrementalDetokenizer in vllm/v1/engine/detokenizer.py, reducing first-token detokenization cost from O(prompt) to O(1) for typical prompts. Behavior is preserved for the first emitted delta by carefully counting only tokens that decode to text and handling UTF-8 boundary cases. No new config or env vars; the fallback to full-prompt seeding keeps output unchanged for prompts ending in U+FFFD. Benchmarks show large first-token latency improvements and lower ITL p99, with throughput unchanged within noise.

**Potential risks**  
Correctness depends on the assumption that a short text-bearing tail reproduces the first delta exactly; decoders that rewrite text across tokens after Fuse (e.g. multi-character Replace) could in principle diverge, though none were found. The U+FFFD fallback retains O(prompt) cost for affected prompts (tracked separately in #48854). The new test file downloads 11 ungated hub tokenizers, adding CI network dependency and runtime. The heuristic constants (5-token offset, 3-token extension, 256-token window) are subtle and could be fragile if tokenizer decoder semantics change.

**Key insights**  
The fix is a targeted performance optimization that exploits the fact that DecodeStream only uses its seed to shape the first delta, so a carefully chosen tail suffices. The design correctly handles context-sensitive decoders (Metaspace, WordPiece, Strip) by counting only tokens that decode to text, and handles UTF-8 boundary cases via U+FFFD detection. The extensive test suite (121 tests, including in-process synthetic tokenizers and real hub tokenizers, with a mutation check showing 35 failures if a naive 5-token tail is used) provides strong evidence of correctness. The PR also documents a pre-existing bug (#48853) where prompts ending in U+FFFD leak the whole prompt text, deliberately preserving that behavior to avoid scope creep.

---

## 37. [[Model][LoRA] Enable LoRA for GTE (bert_with_rope) embedding and reranker models](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68049)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68049
- **Author:** [LantaoJin](https://github.com/LantaoJin)
- **Merged By:** [noooop](https://github.com/noooop)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 19:07:56
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68049/files) (4):**
  - `docs/models/pooling_models/embed.md`
  - `docs/models/pooling_models/scoring.md`
  - `tests/lora/test_gte_lora.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/bert_with_rope.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds LoRA support to the GTE bert_with_rope models (GteNewModel, SnowflakeGteNewModel, GteNewForSequenceClassification) by introducing a GteNewMLP that keeps the checkpoint's fused up_gate_proj [up; gate] layout instead of splitting/reordering into gate_up_proj, so single-slice LoRA adapters map correctly. GteNewModel opts in via fused_up_gate=True, gains packed_modules_mapping and SupportsLoRA, and the reranker gets a LoRA name mapper plus self.config. Docs tables are updated and a new test validates LoRA-vs-merged equivalence.

**Technical impact**  
Changes the MLP computation path for GTE models from fused GeluAndMul to explicit gelu(gate)*up with a MergedColumnParallelLinear, and removes the old split_up_gate_proj/bias-patching logic. This alters weight loading and module naming for these architectures, which is why LoRA adapters now align with the checkpoint layout. Other geglu bert_with_rope models retain the existing gated MLP path via the fused_up_gate flag defaulting to False.

**Potential risks**  
The new MLP path is only exercised by GTE models, so regressions in quantized or non-GTE geglu variants are less covered; the test runs on CPU fp32 and a single GPU config, so broader hardware/dtype coverage is limited. Removing the bias-patching and split logic could affect any checkpoint relying on the old up_gate_proj handling, and the reranker's separate hf_to_vllm_mapper must stay consistent with GteNewModel's mapper. Base outputs are reported bit-identical/within tolerance, but the fp16 GPU diffs (1.3e-4 embedding, 1.1e-3 logit) indicate minor numerical drift.

**Key insights**  
The core insight is that LoRA only renames adapter tensors, so preserving the checkpoint's fused [up; gate] layout avoids landing adapters on the wrong halves, mirroring ModernBERT's Wi approach. The test's sensitivity checks (swapped halves and dropped classifier head both fail) give strong evidence the mapping is correct. Performance is unchanged within noise, and the change is well-scoped with clear out-of-scope notes for Nomic and Jina.

## 38. [[Bugfix][KV Cache] Don't size KV cache groups from draft-only sliding-window buckets](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68533)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68533
- **Author:** [robertgshaw2-redhat](https://github.com/robertgshaw2-redhat)
- **Merged By:** [robertgshaw2-redhat](https://github.com/robertgshaw2-redhat)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 18:41:39
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68533/files) (2):**
  - `tests/v1/core/test_kv_cache_planning.py`
  - `vllm/v1/core/kv_cache_planning.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes KV cache group sizing in _get_kv_cache_groups_uniform_page_size so that draft-only sliding-window buckets (SWA, SWA-MLA, DFlash/DSpark tail-only managers) no longer determine the group size. Since #64109, DFlash/DSpark drafters get their own small bucket, which previously shrank the group size and forced padding onto the target's full-attention layers, wasting KV memory per token. The fix excludes draft-only windowed buckets from the min/max computation (falling back to all buckets if none remain), while keeping draft-only full-attention/MQA buckets counting as before. Two unit tests cover the Muse-shaped target with a DSpark draft and a full-attention draft.

**Technical impact**  
Changes group-size selection logic in the KV cache planner, affecting how layers are bucketed and padded. For models with a draft-only sliding-window bucket, the target's full-attention layers now form fewer, unpadded groups (e.g. 16 layers in 1 group instead of 4 groups of 5), reducing full-attention KV memory per token by ~20% in the cited Muse Spark case. Models without such a bucket see unchanged grouping. The draft bucket is instead padded up to the target group size, which is cheap since it backs only tail blocks.

**Potential risks**  
The change relies on the existing min/max heuristic and only special-cases draft-only windowed buckets; other pathological bucket distributions remain unaddressed (acknowledged FIXME). The is_draft plus SlidingWindowManager subclass check could misclassify future manager types, and the fallback to all buckets when no sizing buckets remain needs care. End-to-end validation on GB200 was still pending at PR time, so real-world memory and correctness impact is not yet fully confirmed. The 90 pre-existing test failures are environmental (no GPU) and not caused by this change.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is that can_share_block_table requires matching manager configs, so DFlash/DSpark drafters no longer join the target's sliding-window bucket after #64109. Padding full-attention layers is expensive (per-token) while padding windowed/tail-only draft layers is cheap, so the fix correctly shifts padding to the cheaper side. The two tests are well-targeted: one fails on main, one guards against over-skipping all draft-only buckets, demonstrating the fix is narrowly scoped.

---

## 39. [[Misc] Use torch 2.14 custom class API for LayerName registration](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64314)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #64314
- **Author:** [cjackal](https://github.com/cjackal)
- **Merged By:** [mgoin](https://github.com/mgoin)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 17:48:59
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64314/files) (1):**
  - `vllm/utils/torch_utils.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The PR updates LayerName registration in vllm/utils/torch_utils.py to use torch's new custom class API (CustomClassBase and register_custom_class with typ='constant') on torch >= 2.14, while retaining the legacy opaque-type API (OpaqueBase and register_opaque_type with typ='value') for torch 2.11-2.13. This eliminates three deprecation warnings emitted at import time on newer torch versions.

**Technical impact**  
Introduces a new HAS_CUSTOM_CLASS version gate and aliases the base class name to CustomClassBase for both code paths, keeping the LayerName class definition uniform. Behavior of LayerName as a hoisted constant/value type is preserved across supported torch versions, but it now depends on private torch internals (_custom_class_base, _library.opaque_object) whose APIs may still evolve.

**Potential risks**  
Relies on private torch modules and version-string detection (2.14.0.dev), which could break if torch renames or changes these APIs again or if dev-version comparisons are imprecise. The typ='value' to typ='constant' semantic shift and the base class swap could subtly affect serialization, FX repr, or hoisting behavior; validation appears limited to existing LayerName CI coverage rather than explicit new tests.

**Key insights**  
The change is a targeted compatibility shim that silences deprecation noise without altering the public LayerName interface, and the dual-path structure cleanly supports both old and new torch versions. Given the reliance on private APIs, adding a focused test asserting no deprecation warnings on torch >= 2.14 would strengthen confidence.

  - `vllm/distributed/ec_transfer/ec_connector/tiering/scheduler.py`
  - `vllm/v1/core/sched/scheduler.py`
  - `vllm/v1/engine/input_processor.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a new ECTieringConnector that extends the existing ECCPUConnector with an optional filesystem secondary tier for encoder-cache offloading. It introduces a tiering package (config, layout, io, executor, filesystem manager, lookup, primary adapter, orchestration manager, scheduler), registers the connector, adds scheduler hooks (on_new_request, bind_encoder_cache_query), and changes the multimodal identifier to hash LoRA name plus path. Extensive unit and integration tests plus a CI job are included, with claimed TTFT and throughput improvements.

**Technical impact**  
Introduces asynchronous CPU-to-filesystem write-through and filesystem-to-CPU promotion with LRU capacity accounting, prefetch pinning, backpressure via EMABackpressureDetector, atomic temp-file publication, and persistent namespace-scoped cache reuse across restarts. Touches core scheduler request admission and input processing, so behavior changes for any deployment using EC connectors, and the LoRA mm_hash change alters cache key identity for tower-connector LoRA users.

**Potential risks**  
Large surface area (~5.5k lines) with concurrency-sensitive pin/unpin and prefetch lifecycle logic that could leak pins or cause stale cache hits if bugs exist. The mm_hash change from lora_name to name+path invalidates existing caches and assumes immutable adapter paths. Filesystem tier relies on flock-based owner leases and local-only capacity accounting, so multi-instance sharing of a root_dir may cause eviction races or overcommit. New scheduler hooks run on every request and could add overhead. Integration test depends on specific log strings and a single-GPU H200 CI job.

**Key insights**  
The design cleanly layers on the existing ECCPU path by borrowing the CPU region/cache via an adapter rather than duplicating storage, and reuses native KV offload primitives (DualQueueThreadPool, AsyncLookupManager, backpressure detector). The namespace digest deliberately excludes process-local identity to enable cross-restart reuse, which is the key persistence mechanism. Validation is thorough at the unit level, but the benchmark claims are single-workload and should be treated as indicative rather than general.

## 41. [[Bugfix][KV Connector] Fix MultiConnector handshake metadata routing](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67174)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #67174
- **Author:** [xsxszab](https://github.com/xsxszab)
- **Merged By:** [njhill](https://github.com/njhill)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 17:25:47
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67174/files) (2):**
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/test_multi_connector.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/multi_connector.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
MultiConnector previously returned only the first non-None sub-connector handshake metadata and forwarded the merged engine-core metadata dict to every sub-connector. This PR introduces MultiKVConnectorHandshakeMetadata (a tuple with one entry per sub-connector, mirroring MultiKVConnectorWorkerMetadata) and a _sub_connector_handshake_metadata helper that splits merged reports so each child receives only its own entries, omitting its None entries. This fixes PP startup failures (e.g. MooncakeStoreConnector rejecting NIXL's pp_rank>0 metadata) and multi-producer cases like UMBP after NIXL.

**Technical impact**  
Changes the worker-side handshake metadata type returned by MultiConnector and the routing logic in both set_xfer_handshake_metadata and set_xfer_handshake_metadata_pp_aware. Subclasses that override get_handshake_metadata to return a single child's metadata (e.g. Dynamo's PdConnector) fall back to the old broadcast behavior via an isinstance check, preserving compatibility. Every sub-connector is still invoked, receiving an empty dict when it reported nothing.

**Potential risks**  
The isinstance-based fallback means any non-MultiKVConnectorHandshakeMetadata value triggers full broadcast, which could mask future routing bugs or silently misroute if a subclass mixes formats. The tuple indexing assumes the worker and scheduler sub-connector lists are in identical order, so any divergence in construction order would misroute metadata. Connectors that report handshake metadata without PP support still hit the base-class PP check, which is intentional but worth noting.

**Key insights**  
The fix aligns handshake metadata routing with the existing worker-metadata aggregation pattern, keeping the design consistent. The regression test cleverly inserts a real ExampleConnector (no metadata) between two metadata-producing connectors to reproduce the PP startup failure, and a second test guards the subclass broadcast behavior. Strong end-to-end validation across TP/PP configurations with matching token IDs and logprobs supports correctness, though the pull-mode PP2 path depends on a separate PR (#65336).

## 42. [[Bugfix][Responses] Keep empty reasoning blocks in Responses output](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67189)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #67189
- **Author:** [aaryamonvikram](https://github.com/aaryamonvikram)
- **Merged By:** [jeejeelee](https://github.com/jeejeelee)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 17:25:39
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67189/files) (5):**
  - `tests/entrypoints/openai/responses/test_responses_utils.py`
  - `tests/parser/test_streaming.py`
  - `tests/reasoning/test_k2_horizon_reasoning_parser.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/openai/responses/utils.py`
  - `vllm/parser/abstract_parser.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Two truthiness checks were changed to 'is not None' so that an empty reasoning string ("") is preserved rather than treated as absent reasoning. In responses/utils.py build_response_output_items now emits a ResponseReasoningItem for empty reasoning, and in parser/abstract_parser.py DelegatingParser.parse_delta no longer drops empty reasoning when it ends in the same delta that begins a tool call. This fixes replayed turns (e.g. Codex) losing reasoning items, which breaks chat templates like K2 Horizon that require a reasoning field on prior assistant turns.

**Technical impact**  
Behavioral change in the Responses API output (empty reasoning items now included) and in streaming parse_delta, which is shared with Chat Completions, so Chat Completions streams may now emit reasoning="" in this boundary case, aligning streaming with existing non-streaming behavior. Models without reasoning are unaffected since None still means no reasoning. Three targeted regression tests were added covering non-streaming output items, generic/Hermes streaming parsers, and K2 Horizon composed streaming.

**Potential risks**  
The parse_delta change affects Chat Completions streaming as a side effect, which could surprise clients that assume empty reasoning is never sent; downstream consumers relying on truthiness of reasoning may need review. The change is narrow and well-tested, but the shared parser path means broader blast radius than the Responses-only framing suggests. The PR notes pre-existing unrelated test failures (Cohere, Mistral) in the validation environment, so CI signal should be confirmed.

**Key insights**  
Root cause is a classic truthiness-vs-None distinction bug where "" was conflated with None. The fix is minimal and surgical, with strong evidence: three tests fail on main and pass here, and wider suites show only the new tests differing. The explicit callout of the Chat Completions side effect and the K2 Horizon real-world validation (~100 Codex sessions) are good signals of thoroughness.

It also touches shared infrastructure (speculator config selection, KV-connector M-RoPE delta transfer, FP8 scale loading, test fixtures) to support the new model.

**Technical impact**  
Broad and cross-cutting: new model registry entries, tokenizer/renderer modes, a new speculative method ('dfly') with target-family-specific config resolution, M-RoPE position-delta propagation through P/D KV transfer, and refactored test fixtures replacing mocks with real runners. Shared code paths (fp8.py, kv_connector utils, model_runner, scheduler) change behavior for all models, not just HY4.

**Potential risks**  
The PR itself reports failing Python tests (RoPE/vision compatibility, gated-model access) and pending end-to-end checkpoint validation, so correctness is unproven. Changes to shared components (FP8 store_dtype/expert_dtype fallback, speculator config dispatch, scheduler M-RoPE delta handling, get_shared_kv_cache_layers getattr) could regress other models. The DFly KV-alias toggling and Triton kernels are subtle and hard to validate without the checkpoint. Large surface area (~12k lines) raises review and maintenance risk.

**Key insights**  
Much of the diff is additive model code, but the highest-risk parts are the shared-infrastructure edits and the DFly KV-sharing/alias lifecycle. The test refactors (real GPUModelRunner, real connector) improve fidelity but increase CI hardware dependence. Merge should be gated on the outstanding Python test failures and fresh end-to-end validation being resolved.

## 44. [[Bugfix][Spec Decode] Bound multi-module MTP re-prefill to valid request history](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/66951)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #66951
- **Author:** [luyixiao95](https://github.com/luyixiao95)
- **Merged By:** [gau-nernst](https://github.com/gau-nernst)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 17:17:40
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/66951/files) (3):**
  - `tests/v1/e2e/spec_decode/mtp/qwen3_5/test_mtp.py`
  - `tests/v1/worker/test_gpu_target_dependent_ar_speculator.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/spec_decode/multi_module_mtp/speculator.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes a multi-module MTP re-prefill bug where a short request reusing a request-state slot could read stale token IDs, hidden states, and embeddings left by the previous occupant. The fix adds a per-slot int32 device counter (num_cached_tokens) recording the valid right-aligned history length, resets it on request admission via a device-side zero_(), updates it in the cache writer kernel, and bounds both re-prefill readers (input buffers and hidden states/embeddings) by min(num_rejected - 1, num_cached_tokens). Two regression tests are added: a GPU kernel-level test and an end-to-end real-request test.

**Technical impact**  
Touches the core speculator input-preparation path in vllm/v1/worker/gpu/spec_decode/multi_module_mtp/speculator.py, adding a new tensor argument threaded through three Triton kernels and their Python wrappers, plus a new add_request hook. Behavior changes only for slot-reuse scenarios with short requests; normal long-request paths are unaffected since the bound is a min with the existing rejection-derived count. No public config changes. The device-side zero_() avoids a host sync that strict-sync validation previously flagged.

**Potential risks**  
GPU validation was performed on a pre-rebase commit and not repeated on the current rebased head, so the final state is only locally checked on macOS without a GPU. The change adds a new tensor to kernel signatures, which could break out-of-tree callers of prepare_input_buffers, prepare_input_hidden_states_and_embeddings, or cache_inputs. The add_request hook must be reliably invoked on every admission path; if any admission bypasses it, stale counts could persist. The end-to-end test is slow (~200s) and eager-only, so CUDA-graph interactions are not directly exercised by the new request test.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is a mismatch between fixed-capacity cache rows and variable-length writes: the writer only fills the tail, so readers must know the valid tail length rather than inferring it from rejection counts alone. Recording the length in the same kernel launch that writes the cache keeps the counter consistent with the data. The baseline evidence (foreign token 9007 and negative position -1) is a clear, reproducible demonstration of the bug, and the fix is minimal and well-scoped. The main outstanding gap is re-running GPU validation on the rebased head.

## 45. [[CI] Enroll 8 CPU steps in automatic sharding](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67534)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #67534
- **Author:** [Thangnguyenvn98](https://github.com/Thangnguyenvn98)
- **Merged By:** [khluu](https://github.com/khluu)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 17:03:13
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67534/files) (4):**
  - `.buildkite/test_areas/docker.yaml`
  - `.buildkite/test_areas/misc.yaml`
  - `.buildkite/test_areas/models_basic.yaml`
  - `.buildkite/test_areas/models_multimodal.yaml`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Enrolls eight CPU test steps (and the AMD mirror of Docker Build Metadata) in automatic runtime sharding by adding automatic_shard: true to their Buildkite YAML definitions. The Multimodal Processor step drops its fixed parallelism: 4 and --num-shards/--shard-id flags in favor of planned shards (8), while the other steps are enrolled so they can split dynamically as their test counts grow. No tests are added, removed, or modified.

**Technical impact**  
CI pipeline behavior changes: multi-shard steps now run as multiple parallel jobs planned from recorded per-test timings, reducing time-to-signal (e.g., Multimodal Processor slowest job from ~37 to ~22 minutes). Machine time increases modestly (roughly +4% to +17% depending on step) due to repeated per-shard setup. If no plan is available or VLLM_CI_RUNTIME_SHARD is off, each step falls back to a single job, which for Multimodal Processor could exceed its 125-minute timeout.

**Potential risks**  
The fallback path is the main risk: without a shard plan, Multimodal Processor runs the full suite in one job and may time out since timeouts are unchanged. Increased machine time and repeated ~2-minute setup per shard add CI cost. Correctness depends on the sharding generator and recorded timings being accurate; validation claims each test runs exactly once, but any generator regression could drop or duplicate tests. Overlap with PR #68030 on the same step requires coordination.

**Key insights**  
The change is config-only but behavior-affecting, so it is SUBSTANTIAL. Validation is thorough (node-ID parity checks, harness tests, multiple builds), and the PR explicitly documents trade-offs and a follow-up option to warm CPU images in the AMI to cut repeated setup. The unaddressed fallback timeout for Multimodal Processor is the most notable gap and is deferred to a planned central timeout setting.

- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64128/files) (2):**
  - `.buildkite/test_areas/entrypoints.yaml`
  - `.buildkite/test_areas/rust_frontend.yaml`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Enrolls the Entrypoints Integration (API Server) step and its AMD mirror, plus the Rust Frontend Serve/Admin Coverage step and its AMD mirror, into automatic CI sharding by replacing fixed parallelism (4 shards) and manual --num-shards/--shard-id flags with automatic_shard: true. The API Server step's shard-1-only dev/rpc command is now run unconditionally under PYTHONPATH so the planner can place it, and the scale_out command drops its manual shard flags. This depends on ci-infra#825, which ensures the sharding plugin loads even when a command sets its own PYTHONPATH.

**Technical impact**  
Changes CI job topology: API Server goes from 4 fixed shards to 7 planned shards (4 on AMD), Rust Frontend from one job to 2 (1 on AMD). Test distribution is now driven by the runtime shard planner rather than static flags, so new test files no longer need manual placement. The dev/rpc and scale_out commands now run in every shard's command list but are partitioned by the plugin, and the plugin's guard fails any job whose pytest runs without a shard plan. Timeouts remain per-job and unchanged.

**Potential risks**  
The main risk is the fallback path: if no shard plan is produced (missing timings or VLLM_CI_RUNTIME_SHARD off), API Server runs as a single job instead of 4 fixed shards and could exceed the 75-minute timeout. Correctness depends on the external ci-infra#825 plugin-loading fix being present; without it, PYTHONPATH-setting commands fail with No module named runtime_shard_plugin. The PR claims exact test coverage parity (875 API Server, 100 Rust Frontend tests, 0 missing/added/duplicated), but this relies on the planner and the plugin guard behaving as described.

**Key insights**  
Validation is thorough: a passing /ci run with no retries, a test-ID diff against main showing parity, and machine-time comparisons showing ~40% savings for API Server and within-noise results for Rust Frontend. LM Eval Spec Decode was deliberately excluded because sharding increased machine time 15% and its slowest shard exceeded the time bar. The PR itself flags the fallback-timeout concern as something to address centrally, which is the most notable open gap.

## 47. [[CI] Upload command spans first and give big job-end flushes more time](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68208)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68208
- **Author:** [Thangnguyenvn98](https://github.com/Thangnguyenvn98)
- **Merged By:** [khluu](https://github.com/khluu)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 17:02:35
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68208/files) (4):**
  - `.buildkite/scripts/ci-otel/README.md`
  - `.buildkite/scripts/ci-otel/ci_otel.py`
  - `.buildkite/scripts/ci-otel/ci_otel.sh`
  - `.buildkite/scripts/ci-otel/tests/test_ci_otel.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The job-end span flush in ci_otel.py now sorts spans so command spans upload before test spans, and scales its upload budget from a 3 s base plus 3 s per extra batch up to a 20 s cap instead of a flat 3 s. The shell guard around the flush rises from timeout 4s to timeout 25s, and the batch-splitting logic is extracted into a reusable _batches helper. This fixes a real data-loss bug where large shards timed out mid-flush and dropped the command span, orphaning already-uploaded test spans and making the build unusable for the timings endpoint.

**Technical impact**  
Every traced CI job loads this script, so the change is trunk-wide. Small jobs (one batch) keep the 3 s Python budget but their hard shell wall-clock limit grows from 4 s to 25 s, slightly increasing worst-case job teardown time for a trickling endpoint. Ordering is enforced at flush time rather than via spool naming, which covers all writers (pytest, commands, GPU samples, BuildKit) without changing file conventions. The refactored _batches is called twice per flush (once for ordering, once for budget), a minor extra encode cost on large shards.

**Potential risks**  
The 25 s shell guard applies to all traced jobs, so a hung or trickling endpoint can now delay job completion up to 25 s instead of 4 s, which could matter for jobs near CI time limits. The sort key relies on the literal span name 'pytest.test' to distinguish test spans; any future span type that should be deprioritized or a rename of that name would silently break the ordering guarantee. End-to-end behavior is not validated on CI (PR builds don't trace and ingest only accepts main), so the fix is only confirmed by the first post-merge main builds. The submitter notes the human has not yet reviewed every changed line.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is a deadline-vs-ordering interaction: a single flat timeout combined with spool-file name order let the parent command span be the casualty of a timeout. Sorting parents first converts a correctness failure (orphaned tests) into a graceful degradation (some test spans dropped). Scaling the budget per batch rather than using a flat 20 s keeps the common one-batch job at today's 3 s, a sound trade-off. Validation is unusually thorough (mutation testing, real-process timing tables, hang simulation), but the lack of CI end-to-end coverage is the main residual gap and should be watched on the first main builds.

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The PR adds optional: true to the four-H100 Fault Tolerance E2E step and an explicit optional: false to the MI355 MoRI EP Healthy Baseline mirror step in .buildkite/test_areas/fault_tolerance.yaml. The goal is to defer the H100 E2E job to nightly and on-demand builds while keeping the AMD baseline on its existing schedule, since the H100 job passed but is being rescheduled rather than fixed.

**Technical impact**  
This is a CI scheduling change that alters when the H100 fault tolerance E2E job runs on ordinary PR builds, relying on the existing optional-job mechanism. The explicit optional: false on the AMD mirror prevents it from inheriting the H100 optional setting, preserving its current behavior. No test commands, hardware, sharding, or assertions are modified.

**Potential risks**  
Ordinary PR builds will no longer automatically exercise the H100 fault tolerance E2E job, so regressions in that path could go undetected until nightly runs. The inheritance behavior of optional across mirror steps is subtle, and correctness depends on the explicit override being honored by the pipeline generator. GPU tests were not rerun locally, though the change is config-only.

**Key insights**  
The change is a focused two-line CI config edit that reuses an existing mechanism rather than introducing new logic. The submitter validated selector behavior via the local pipeline generator and confirmed no duplicate scheduling PR exists. The main tradeoff is reduced per-PR coverage for the H100 job in exchange for nightly and on-demand coverage.

## 49. [[Bugfix][Core] Drop streaming updates that would overflow max_model_len](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63113)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #63113
- **Author:** [potoior](https://github.com/potoior)
- **Merged By:** [njhill](https://github.com/njhill)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 16:52:19
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63113/files) (6):**
  - `tests/v1/core/test_async_scheduler.py`
  - `tests/v1/core/test_scheduler.py`
  - `tests/v1/e2e/general/test_streaming_input.py`
  - `tests/v1/kv_offload/tiering/p2p/test_manager.py`
  - `tests/v1/streaming_input/test_scheduler_streaming.py`
  - `vllm/v1/core/sched/scheduler.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes a bug where streaming-input sessions merged an update chunk without checking the accumulated prompt length against max_model_len, causing the session to wedge in RUNNING (or crash EngineCore on V2 with negative num_scheduled_tokens). The scheduler now rejects an update before mutating the session if the merged token count plus one sampling step would exceed max_model_len, parks the session in WAITING_FOR_STREAMING_REQ so a shorter update can resume it, and emits an empty LENGTH-finished turn for the dropped chunk. Adds scheduler-level and e2e regression tests plus test fixture updates for the new dropped_streaming_updates field.

**Technical impact**  
Touches core scheduler merge logic (_update_request_as_session now returns bool, _handle_stopped_request loops over the queue), adds a dropped_streaming_updates buffer drained in update_from_output at two points (before and after turn outputs) to preserve ordering, and extends has_requests() so pending dropped updates keep the engine stepping. The guard only trips in the previously-fatal region, so sessions within max_model_len are unaffected. New FinishReason import and EngineCoreOutput emission change the output stream shape for overflow cases.

**Potential risks**  
The e2e test could not be run locally (aarch64 Jetson without CUDA build) and needs CI/human confirmation, so the end-to-end behavior of the empty LENGTH turn and resumption is unverified on GPU. The dropped chunk is only surfaced via a server-side logger.warning with no client-facing error, which may confuse clients. Sessions parked at the boundary reject all further updates until closed, holding KV blocks. The two-point emission of dropped updates and the while-loop change in _handle_stopped_request introduce ordering subtleties that could regress other streaming paths if assumptions about queue draining differ.

**Key insights**  
Root cause is an unguarded merge in _update_request_as_session combined with check_stop only firing on output append, so nothing bounds the session at turn boundaries; the negative clamp then wedges or crashes the engine. The fix reuses existing WAITING_FOR_STREAMING_REQ bookkeeping rather than introducing new abstractions, and implements the softer resumable outcome requested in the #42502 review instead of FINISHED_LENGTH_CAPPED. Boundary uses >= (merged == max_model_len rejected) matching #42502. Follow-ups noted: per-chunk client error surfacing and bounded-session design.

## 50. [[Multimodal] Allow disabling embed inputs per modality in `--limit-mm-per-prompt`](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67995)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #67995
- **Author:** [cjackal](https://github.com/cjackal)
- **Merged By:** [DarkLight1337](https://github.com/DarkLight1337)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 16:50:49
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67995/files) (12):**
  - `docs/configuration/conserving_memory.md`
  - `docs/features/multimodal_inputs.md`
  - `tests/config/test_multimodal_config.py`
  - `tests/multimodal/test_registry.py`
  - `tests/renderers/chat_utils/test_video.py`
  - `vllm/config/model.py`
  - `vllm/config/multimodal.py`
  - `vllm/engine/arg_utils.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/pixtral.py`
  - `vllm/multimodal/encoder_budget.py`
  - `vllm/multimodal/processing/context.py`
  - `vllm/renderers/chat_utils/item_tracker.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a per-modality `allow_embeds` option to `--limit-mm-per-prompt` so precomputed embedding inputs can be rejected for a single modality while `enable_mm_embeds` remains on globally. Introduces `BaseDummyOptions.allow_embeds` and `MultiModalConfig.is_mm_embeds_enabled(modality)`, then wires this into the encoder budget's embed-only modality set, `parse_mm_data` validation, the chat item tracker count-validation bypass, and Pixtral's encoder CUDA graph `pixels_only` check. Motivation is that EC/KV consumers auto-enable `enable_mm_embeds`, leaving no way to fully disable a modality's encoder cache reservation.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior changes in several core paths: zero-limit modalities with `allow_embeds=False` are excluded from the encoder cache budget, embedding inputs for such modalities now raise errors in both `parse_mm_data` and the item tracker, and Pixtral keeps its image encoder CUDA graph when image embeds are opted out. Defaults preserve existing behavior (`allow_embeds=True`), and type signatures for `limit_mm_per_prompt` are widened to accept bool values. Tests cover config resolution, encoder budget reduction, and chat parsing rejection.

**Potential risks**  
The item tracker refactor changes control flow: previously the embeddings bypass only applied when `enable_mm_embeds` was true and limit was 0; now any `*_embeds` input with `enable_mm_embeds` false will raise a new error path, which could affect callers relying on prior validation ordering. The encoder budget change relies on `mm_config` being non-None, and the Pixtral fix is noted as unreachable in upstream main, so its real-world coverage is limited. Error messages differ between `parse_mm_data` (ValueError) and the tracker (VLLMValidationError), which may be inconsistent for clients.

**Key insights**  
The design is opt-out rather than opt-in because automatic enablement by consumers would override any opt-in list, which is a sound rationale. The change correctly threads a single helper (`is_mm_embeds_enabled`) through all decision points, reducing duplication. The encoder cache budget reduction claim (114688 to 16384 tokens) is validated by a test, and the E2E log evidence supports the fix. Documentation updates in two files are appropriate, though the docs reference `allow_embeds` while the PR title/description sometimes say `embeds`, which could confuse users.

## 51. [[Perf][Ray] Kill RayExecutorV2 actors before joining the monitor thread](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68526)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68526
- **Author:** [AndreasKaratzas](https://github.com/AndreasKaratzas)
- **Merged By:** [njhill](https://github.com/njhill)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 16:37:58
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68526/files) (1):**
  - `vllm/v1/executor/ray_executor_v2.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
In RayExecutorV2.shutdown(), the order of operations was swapped: Ray actors are now killed before joining the monitor thread, instead of after. Previously the join waited out the monitor's 5 s ray.wait poll; killing actors first makes ray.wait return immediately and _should_stop() true, cutting teardown from a median ~4 s to ~1 s. This is a performance fix split from #68288 and must land after it.

**Technical impact**  
Behavioral change in teardown sequencing for the Ray backend executor. Faster shutdown means EngineCore can exit within the liveness monitor's 1 s wake window, which is why the dependent double-reap race fix (#68288) is a prerequisite. The change is small (2 lines) but affects shutdown ordering and timing semantics.

**Potential risks**  
The PR explicitly states that without #68288 this change triggers false 'force killing remaining process EngineCore' in 7/10 runs, so landing order is critical. Any other code relying on the monitor thread being stopped before actors are killed could be affected. Validation was done on MI300X/MI355X locally rather than broad CI, so coverage across other hardware/backends is limited.

**Key insights**  
The fix is a simple reordering that exploits the monitor's ray.wait returning early once actors are killed, avoiding the poll timeout. The tight coupling with #68288 is the main review concern; the dependency must be enforced at merge time. Reported teardown improvement (~4 s to ~1 s) is well quantified with 20-run validation on top of the prerequisite.

---

## 52. [[Bugfix] Peek at the EngineCore exit status instead of reaping it in the liveness monitor](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68288)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68288
- **Author:** [AndreasKaratzas](https://github.com/AndreasKaratzas)
- **Merged By:** [njhill](https://github.com/njhill)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 16:37:22
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68288/files) (1):**
  - `vllm/v1/engine/utils.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes a double-reaping race in CoreEngineProcManager where the liveness monitor read proc.exitcode (a waitpid(WNOHANG)) concurrently with shutdown() joining the process. The monitor now uses a new _exited_cleanly() helper that calls os.waitid with WNOWAIT to observe the exit status without reaping, leaving shutdown() as the sole reaper, with a fallback to proc.exitcode on platforms lacking os.waitid (macOS before Python 3.13).

**Technical impact**  
Eliminates false 'force killing remaining process EngineCore' logs and prevents SIGKILL being sent to an already-reaped (potentially reused) pid. Also corrects misclassification of clean exits as failures when the sentinel fires before the child becomes reapable. Change is localized to one file and one helper; no locking added since the monitor only observes.

**Potential risks**  
The WNOWAIT call without WNOHANG blocks through the brief window between sentinel firing and child reapability, which is intended but could theoretically stall the monitor thread if a child never becomes reapable. The ChildProcessError branch treats already-reaped children as clean, which is reasonable given shutdown is underway but assumes shutdown only reaps after a clean exit. The macOS fallback retains the original racy behavior on older Python versions, so the bug persists there.

**Key insights**  
The root cause traces to PR #35862 which introduced proc.exitcode in the monitor, creating a second reaper. The fix is well-scoped and validated with a targeted reproduction (122 false force-kills on main vs 0), plus real LLM teardown tests across mp and Ray backends. The 29 pre-existing test failures are correctly attributed to an unrelated CacheConfig issue on this snapshot.

---

## 53. [[Feature][KV Connector] Notify connectors after startup KV cache preparation](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/66574)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #66574
- **Author:** [maobaolong](https://github.com/maobaolong)
- **Merged By:** [aoshen02](https://github.com/aoshen02)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 16:35:36
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/66574/files) (6):**
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/test_kv_cache_ready.py`
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/test_multi_connector.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/base.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/multi_connector.py`
  - `vllm/v1/engine/core.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/worker_base.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a new startup lifecycle hook on_kv_cache_ready() to KVConnectorBase_V1, a no-op default, MultiConnector fan-out, a WorkerBase.notify_kv_cache_ready() that synchronizes the device then invokes the connector group, and a collective_rpc call in EngineCore._initialize_kv_caches after final KV sizing. The goal is to let connectors finish deferred registration at a well-defined boundary before the first request, plus new unit and LLM-entrypoint tests.

**Technical impact**  
Introduces a new public connector API and a synchronous startup RPC across all workers, gated on kv_transfer_config.is_kv_transfer_instance. Because the call is inline/blocking during engine initialization, any connector implementation that blocks or raises will stall or fail startup. The device synchronize before the hook ensures prior warmup/capture work is complete, except under VLLM_ELASTIC_EP_SCALE_UP_LAUNCH where the hook precedes deferred warm_and_capture(), an ordering exception documented in the docstring.

**Potential risks**  
No timeout or cancellation is provided, so a misbehaving connector callback can hang startup indefinitely; exceptions propagate and abort initialization. The elastic-EP ordering exception means connectors cannot assume warmup/capture has run in all modes, which could cause subtle bugs for connectors relying on that invariant. CUDA serving and E2E behavior are explicitly unvalidated, and the LLM-entrypoint test depends on cached OPT-125m weights and specific config flags, so coverage on real GPU paths is limited.

**Key insights**  
The design is minimal and backward compatible via a default no-op, with MultiConnector correctly forwarding to children and tests covering ordering, exception propagation, and capture-reset sequencing. The main open concern is the lack of a bounded-time guarantee for the hook, which the PR acknowledges and defers to connector authors. Validation is CPU-only with CUDA serving pending, so reviewers should weigh the unverified GPU path and the elastic-EP ordering caveat before merge.

## 54. [[Perf][Qwen4Exp] Fuse HyperConnection up projection and gate mix on SM90](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/66791)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #66791
- **Author:** [positive666](https://github.com/positive666)
- **Merged By:** [gau-nernst](https://github.com/gau-nernst)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 16:30:15
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/66791/files) (2):**
  - `tests/models/qwen4_exp/test_hc_ops.py`
  - `vllm/models/qwen4_exp/nvidia/ops/hc.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a new Triton kernel (_hc_up_gate_mix_sm90_kernel) that fuses the BF16 up projection, sigmoid gating and four-stream mixing for Qwen4Exp HyperConnection on SM90 (Hopper), avoiding materialization of a 10,240-column BF16 gate. The dispatch in hc_up_gate_mix_supported/hc_up_gate_mix now routes SM90 to the Triton path while keeping the existing SM100 custom op, and tests are extended to cover SM90 and the 512-row serving threshold.

**Technical impact**  
Changes runtime behavior on SM90 devices for the specific shape (BF16, 4 streams, hidden 2560, rank 320, >=512 rows), replacing a materialized gate with an on-chip fused kernel. Preserves BF16 gate rounding and FP32 mixing order per the description, and reports ~2-2.5% TTFT improvements on long-prefill H20 workloads. SM100, ROCm and other shapes are unaffected.

**Potential risks**  
The SM90 path is gated only on device capability and shape, not on the presence of the custom op, so it is a new code path with its own numerical behavior (BF16 rounding before sigmoid) that must match the reference. Performance is qualified only on H20; H100/H200, DP>1 and short-request (4K) TTFT show mixed/regressive results in the PR's own data, and long random greedy outputs are not bitwise equivalent. Hardcoded HIDDEN=2560, RANK=320 and HC=4 constants mean any model config change silently breaks assumptions, though asserts guard shapes.

**Key insights**  
The fusion is narrowly specialized and well-guarded by asserts and support checks, with extensive benchmarking and numerical validation. The main review focus should be correctness of the Triton tile indexing/split logic, the BF16-vs-FP32 sigmoid rounding difference between SM90 and SM100 paths, and the acknowledged short-request TTFT caveat and limited hardware coverage.

## 55. [[Multimodal] Pass placement with Omni embeddings to drop the audio-in-video host read](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68200)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68200
- **Author:** [AndreasKaratzas](https://github.com/AndreasKaratzas)
- **Merged By:** [njhill](https://github.com/njhill)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 16:29:11
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68200/files) (9):**
  - `tests/model_executor/test_qwen3_omni.py`
  - `tests/models/multimodal/processing/test_qwen2_5_omni_embed.py`
  - `tests/v1/worker/test_encoder_runner.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/lfm3_omni.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/qwen2_5_omni_thinker.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/qwen3_omni_moe_thinker.py`
  - `vllm/multimodal/utils.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/cpu/encoder_runner.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/mm/encoder_runner.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The Omni models (Qwen3-Omni, Qwen2.5-Omni, LFM3-Omni) previously copied input_ids to the host to locate video/audio positions for the interleaved audio-in-video merge. This PR has the GPU and CPU encoder runners attach each gathered embedding's batch placement (start, end, is_embed) alongside its modality tag, propagates it through copy_mm_embedding_modality, and adds get_mm_embedding_modality_masks so models rebuild per-modality masks without a device read. The old input_ids.cpu() path remains as a fallback but is no longer allow-listed, so VLLM_GPU_SYNC_CHECK surfaces any lost placement.

**Technical impact**  
Eliminates host syncs on the audio-in-video path (claimed 1 to 0, down from 66 on main) for Qwen3-Omni, and removes the gpu_sync_allowed() wrappers in the three models. The change reuses the existing tensor-attribute mechanism (modality tag) so no model or runner signatures change, and adds explicit device='cpu' to several host-side tensors in the Qwen3-Omni audio encoder. Behavior is intended to be output-identical, validated by 14-prompt comparisons and a 2,814-batch fuzz test.

**Potential risks**  
The fallback path still performs the input_ids host read and is now un-allow-listed, so any future code path that re-wraps embeddings without copy_mm_embedding_modality will trigger sync-check errors rather than silently working. The CPU worker and LFM3-Omni were not validated end to end (CPU suite skips on GPU hosts, no LFM3 checkpoint), so those paths rely on code reading and unit tests. Correctness depends on placement coordinates matching the runner's is_mm_embed fill logic, including the drafter look-ahead shift, which is subtle and could regress if that logic changes.

**Key insights**  
The design cleverly piggybacks placement metadata on the tensor via the same attribute mechanism already used for modality, avoiding invasive signature changes. Making the fallback read non-allow-listed is a good defensive choice that turns silent regressions into visible sync-check failures. The extensive validation (fuzz equivalence against is_mm_embed & token-id masks, chunked prefill, prefix hits) is strong evidence of output parity, though the untested CPU and LFM3 paths are the main residual gaps.

## 56. [[Bugfix][Kernel] Apply beta after the K loop in chunk_scaled_dot_kkt](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67812)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #67812
- **Author:** [JonSnow1807](https://github.com/JonSnow1807)
- **Merged By:** [gau-nernst](https://github.com/gau-nernst)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 16:26:46
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67812/files) (1):**
  - `vllm/third_party/flash_linear_attention/ops/chunk_scaled_dot_kkt.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The chunk_scaled_dot_kkt_fwd_kernel in the vendored FLA ops now applies beta after the K loop instead of scaling k inside it, matching upstream FLA. This removes the register user of k that caused Triton's pipeliner to under-allocate its shared-memory buffer, which produced a race with the async wgmma on Hopper. The RDNA-specific CAST_DOT_TO_K_DTYPE branch is also removed since the dot now always takes k's dtype.

**Technical impact**  
Fixes a nondeterminism/correctness bug on Hopper where autotune configs with BK < K and num_warps >= 4 gave run-to-run differing results and were ~1.7e-3 off a float32 reference; all 27 configs now agree and are within 2.8e-7. The autotuned kernel is also reported 4-6% faster on H100. Behavior on RDNA is preserved because the dot naturally uses k's dtype, and the change is confined to one vendored kernel file.

**Potential risks**  
The fix relies on the beta-after-loop formulation being mathematically equivalent, which it is, but it changes the numerical path for all backends, so any downstream consumer of this kernel (e.g. OLMo Hybrid via chunk_gated_delta_rule) should be re-validated. The race does not reproduce on B200/MI355 CI hardware, so CI alone cannot guard against regression on Hopper; the new test checks determinism and reference accuracy but its Hopper coverage depends on external validation. The GSM8K accuracy delta (31.5% to 32.2%) is within noise (p=0.19) and should not be read as a quality improvement.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is a Triton pipeliner bug (triton-lang/triton#12738) triggered by a buffer having both a shared-memory wgmma consumer and a register consumer; removing the register use of k sidesteps it without a Triton upgrade. This is a genuine correctness fix, not just a perf tweak, and the PR is well-validated with a targeted determinism test plus existing suite (1752 passed, 1 skipped). The related config-pruning PR #65927 would not have fully addressed the K=256 BK=128 num_warps>=4 cases, so this fix is complementary rather than duplicate.

## 57. [[ROCm][Perf][GLM-5.3-Flash] Drop int32/int64 copies in the kpool sparse indexer](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63910)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #63910
- **Author:** [amd-sriram](https://github.com/amd-sriram)
- **Merged By:** [dllehr-amd](https://github.com/dllehr-amd)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 15:56:52
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63910/files) (3):**
  - `tests/kernels/test_kpool_decode_update_batched.py`
  - `vllm/models/glm5next/amd/ops/kpool_compress.py`
  - `vllm/models/glm5next/amd/sparse_indexer.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The ROCm GLM-5.3-Flash kpool sparse indexer previously launched int32-to-int64 casts, a +1 add, a -1 fill, and a buffer copy around expand_pools_and_append_tail in every sparse-attention layer. This PR makes the Triton expand kernel read int32 pool ids and int64 positions directly, folds the +1 into the kernel via a new seq_len_offset argument, and adds an out= parameter so the kernel writes straight into topk_indices_buffer rows (including the -1 tail), removing six launches per sparse-attention layer (66 per decode step). The decode update kernel now also accepts int64 positions, and the caller's -1 fill is skipped for decode rows that self-fill full width.

**Technical impact**  
Reduces kernel count per decode step from 1,295 to 1,229 and cuts ~308 us of kernel time under the profiler, yielding measured TPOT improvements of roughly 1.7-4.4% on MI355X and ~1.9% throughput geomean on gfx942, with accuracy unchanged. The change is localized to the AMD kpool path (kpool_compress.py, sparse_indexer.py) plus tests; the NVIDIA indexer and other models are untouched. The new in-place out= contract requires callers to pass a unit-column-stride int32 row slice and to write full width, which is asserted and documented.

**Potential risks**  
The correctness of the -1 fill skip hinges on the decode_rows_self_filled invariant: every decode return taken under that condition must write full width, or stale indices from the previous step could survive in tail columns. This is a subtle coupling marked only by comments, so future edits to decode return paths could silently break it. The in-place write into a wider buffer row also relies on the kernel writing -1 past topk + pool_size - 1, and on positions/out views being correctly offset for prefill chunks. Benchmark noise is acknowledged (128k c8 and some MTP points move against the trend), so the perf claims are modest and partly within run-to-run spread.

**Key insights**  
The PR is well-validated: new tests cover int64 positions, the in-place call with seq_len_offset=1 and a wider out slice, row offsets, and untouched rows outside the slice, plus accuracy evals (GSM8K, GPQA, RULER NIAH) and a sync check. The main review focus should be the decode_rows_self_filled invariant and the out= preconditions (dtype, shape, stride, full-width writes), since these are enforced by asserts and comments rather than types. Overall a targeted, low-blast-radius optimization with credible evidence, but it changes kernel behavior and memory writes, so it is not trivial.

## 58. [[Perf][GLM-5.3-Flash] KDA decode and prefill through FlashInfer](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63544)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #63544
- **Author:** [suiyoubi](https://github.com/suiyoubi)
- **Merged By:** [wzhao18](https://github.com/wzhao18)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 15:55:10
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63544/files) (4):**
  - `tests/models/glm5next/test_kda_conv_weight_refit.py`
  - `tests/models/glm5next/test_kda_flashinfer.py`
  - `tests/models/glm5next/test_kda_recurrent.py`
  - `vllm/models/glm5next/common/kda.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR moves GLM-5.3-Flash's KDA (Kimi Delta Attention) layers onto FlashInfer kernels: a fused single-token decode, a packed multi-token draft-verify decode, and an opt-in CuTe DSL chunked prefill. It adds backend resolution/fallback logic (auto/flashinfer/triton for decode, auto/triton/flashkda/flashinfer for prefill), a decode-row peel for mixed steps, and new unit tests. The motivation is performance: replacing three Triton kernels per layer per decode step with one fused FlashInfer launch, yielding reported 1.3-2.1x decode speedups and 5-25% serving TPS/GPU gains.

**Technical impact**  
Substantially changes the KDA forward path in vllm/models/glm5next/common/kda.py (+504/-72): new backend selection with static support checks, FlashInfer conv/norm weight preparation, norm placement moved into the fused kernel (so _forward now returns normed output and takes an extra g2 argument), in-place recurrent state updates for prefill, and contiguous state indices for strided block-table columns. It depends on FlashInfer 0.7.3 (a separate pin bump) and only activates on SM10x with specific head counts, dtypes, and layouts; otherwise it falls back to Triton.

**Potential risks**  
Hard dependency on FlashInfer 0.7.3: with the current 0.7.2.post1 pin the static support checks accept configs that fail at first call, so merge ordering matters. The norm-placement change is subtle (must not depend on step type due to breakable CUDA graphs) and could silently produce wrong outputs if misapplied. The mixed-step decode-row peel and in-place state updates are correctness-sensitive; the PR reports a found-and-fixed bug with strided spec slot indices. CI runs on H200 (SM90) where these paths skip, so SM10x coverage relies on manual Blackwell validation. FlashInfer prefill is slower at low concurrency (higher TTFT), which is why it stays opt-in.

**Key insights**  
Well-validated PR with extensive unit tests (new test_kda_flashinfer.py), GSM8K accuracy parity (91.85 vs 91.88), MTP draft-acceptance parity, and detailed serving benchmarks on GB300/GB200. Numerics are as good or better than the Triton path against an fp32 reference. Main review focus should be the norm-placement invariant, the decode-row peel correctness in mixed steps, the strided-index fix, and confirming the FlashInfer pin bump lands first. The opt-in prefill and documented follow-ups (host launch overhead, few-sequence prefill) are reasonable scoping decisions.
