## 59. [[Quantization] Support ModelOpt INT8_PB_WO routed experts](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/66717)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #66717
- **Author:** [cjluo-nv](https://github.com/cjluo-nv)
- **Merged By:** [pavanimajety](https://github.com/pavanimajety)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 15:54:42
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/66717/files) (4):**
  - `docs/features/quantization/modelopt.md`
  - `tests/quantization/test_modelopt.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/fused_moe/oracle/int_wna16.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/quantization/modelopt.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Extends ModelOpt INT8_PB_WO quantization from linear layers to routed MoE experts in both modelopt_int8 and modelopt_mixed modes. Adds a new ModelOptInt8PbWoMoEMethod that reuses the existing WNA16 MoE oracle (Marlin/Triton/Humming) without new kernels, introduces a WNA16WeightFormat dataclass in the oracle for format-neutral group-quantized weights, shares the int8 packing helper between linear and MoE paths, and enforces a one-algo/one-group-size rule per MoE layer. Docs and tests are updated accordingly.

**Technical impact**  
Previously INT8_PB_WO experts raised NotImplementedError; now they load and run on existing WNA16 MoE kernels, letting ModelOpt MoE checkpoints (whose bulk weights are in experts) be served end to end without converting to compressed-tensors. The oracle gains a new accepted quant_config type threaded through backend selection, Marlin conversion, and a Humming adapter, while existing frontends remain unchanged. Validation is limited to NVIDIA GPUs (sm_120); other platforms and EPLB/biases raise NotImplementedError.

**Potential risks**  
Tests have not run on Hopper (the CI quantization step will be their first Hopper run), and ROCm/XPU, other GPU generations, real ModelOpt-written checkpoints, large MoE models, lm-eval accuracy, and EPLB are unvalidated. The new one-format-per-MoE-layer ValueError is a behavior change that could reject previously-loadable mixed configs. The WNA16WeightFormat additions touch shared oracle code paths (Marlin group-size checks, Humming conversion) that other frontends depend on. A pre-existing Marlin TP=2 deviation with narrow intermediate partitions is noted but not fixed.

**Key insights**  
The PR is well-scoped and reuses the WNA16 oracle rather than adding kernels, with strong validation: bit-identical logprobs vs compressed-tensors pack-quantized, an injected scale-bug test showing sensitivity, and a real OLMoE run showing differences within routing noise. The design mirrors a per-QuantKey MoE scheme for kInt8Static, easing future consolidation. Main open items are Hopper CI coverage, broader hardware validation, and confirming the new strictness rule does not break legitimate mixed-precision checkpoints.

## 60. [[CI] Say when /ci rocm cache comes before the base image is pushed](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68480)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68480
- **Author:** [AndreasKaratzas](https://github.com/AndreasKaratzas)
- **Merged By:** [AndreasKaratzas](https://github.com/AndreasKaratzas)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 14:45:51
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68480/files) (2):**
  - `.github/workflows/scripts/run_ci_command.py`
  - `.github/workflows/scripts/test_run_ci_command.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The /ci rocm cache handler now distinguishes between 'no build exists' and 'a build is still running but hasn't pushed the ROCm base image yet'. It refactors the build collection into a separate `builds` list, filters for sources as before, and when no source is found checks for an active build via `is_active_build` to emit a more accurate, actionable message linking the running build. A new test covers this path.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior change is limited to the user-facing reply of the /ci rocm cache command in a specific edge case (PR modifying docker/Dockerfile.rocm_base during the first CI run). No changes to build triggering, caching logic, or other refusal paths; the existing 'no build' and other refusal messages are unchanged. Reuses existing helpers (iter_builds, is_active_build) and constants, keeping complexity low.

**Potential risks**  
The new branch relies on `is_active_build` correctly identifying in-progress builds; if a build is stuck or misreported as active, authors may be told to wait indefinitely. The message assumes the base image step is the gating factor, which could be slightly misleading if the build is active for unrelated reasons. Minor risk of picking the latest running build when multiple exist, though the message links only one.

**Key insights**  
This is a good example of improving developer experience by making an error message reflect the actual state rather than a generic failure. The refactor cleanly separates build discovery from source selection, and the added test meaningfully validates the new branch (fails without the change). Scope is tight and well-justified by the described one-hour-plus window where the old message was misleading.

## 61. [[Frontend] Port the Muse Spark parsers to the streaming parser engine](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62728)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #62728
- **Author:** [samayp01](https://github.com/samayp01)
- **Merged By:** [sfeng33](https://github.com/sfeng33)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 14:14:30
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62728/files) (12):**
  - `docs/features/tool_calling.md`
  - `tests/parser/engine/test_muse.py`
  - `tests/parser/engine/test_replay.py`
  - `tests/parser/engine/trace_builder.py`
  - `tools/pre_commit/test_tethering_allowlist.txt`
  - `vllm/parser/engine/parser_engine.py`
  - `vllm/parser/engine/registered_adapters.py`
  - `vllm/parser/muse.py`
  - `vllm/reasoning/__init__.py`
  - `vllm/reasoning/muse_engine_reasoning_parser.py`
  - `vllm/tool_parsers/__init__.py`
  - `vllm/tool_parsers/muse_engine_tool_parser.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Ports the Muse Spark parsers (muse, musespark) from the legacy reasoning/tool parser path to the streaming ParserEngine by adding a new vllm/parser/muse.py with a custom MuseStreamingParserEngine, registering engine adapters, and repointing the reasoning and tool parser registries to new MuseEngineReasoningParser/MuseEngineToolParser classes. A small _streaming_engine_cls hook was added to ParserEngine, plus new engine tests, trace-builder support, docs, and a CI allowlist entry.

**Technical impact**  
Muse/musespark now run through the shared engine state machine with recipient-based routing (self/none=reasoning, user/commentary=content, developer=dropped, other=tool), token-id matched markers, header-tail holdback for to=NAME without <\\|eom\\|>, and rfind_tokens-based reasoning-end detection. Reported CPU time drops dramatically (parse_delta 3.79s to 0.027s; reasoning-end check 4.68s to 0.002s), and the change fixes quoted ATEM being misparsed as tool calls and dropped unframed output in streaming.

**Potential risks**  
Behavioral differences from the old parser are intentional but user-visible: reasoning now ends at the tool header, tool messages are dropped under tool_choice=none or no tools, truncated tool calls no longer leak framing, and non-streaming drops content after a tool call. The new engine tests are allowlisted out of CI, so regressions may not be caught automatically, and no model was actually served during validation. The custom streaming engine overrides several internals (_on_terminal, _emit_for_state, header buffering), which is fragile against future base-class changes.

**Key insights**  
The PR is a well-scoped migration that reuses the engine framework while isolating Muse-specific routing in a subclass, and it includes a parity harness over 62 outputs plus targeted tests for holdback, parallel calls, and reasoning-token counting. The main review focus should be the intentional semantic deltas, the correctness of the header-tail holdback state machine, and the lack of CI coverage for the new tests.

## 62. [[Bugfix] Fix GPU sync check failures in NemotronLabsDiffusion](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68376)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68376
- **Author:** [mirza-halilcevic](https://github.com/mirza-halilcevic)
- **Merged By:** [AndreasKaratzas](https://github.com/AndreasKaratzas)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 14:00:56
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68376/files) (6):**
  - `tests/models/language/generation/test_nemotron_dllm.py`
  - `tests/v1/sample/test_diffusion_gemma_reads.py`
  - `tests/v1/worker/test_gpu_nemotron_dllm_sampler.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/diffusion_gemma.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/nemotron_dllm.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/sample/logprob.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes GPU<->CPU sync check failures in NemotronLabsDiffusion by replacing scalar element assignments (t[i] = v) with fill_/index_fill_ and rewriting the logprobs path to avoid host reads, mirroring diffusion_gemma.py. It introduces a fixed-width top-k stash, per-step canvas logprobs rows, and a gpu_sync_allowed() fallback for top-k wider than MAX_LOGPROB_TOKEN_IDS. Shared helpers (concat_logprob_stashes, masked_stash_write) are moved into vllm/v1/worker/gpu/sample/logprob.py and reused by diffusion_gemma.

**Technical impact**  
Eliminates blocking host syncs in add_request, remove_request, init_canvas, and the sampler logprobs path, making the model compatible with VLLM_GPU_SYNC_CHECK=error. It also corrects logprobs=0 reporting in mixed batches (now reports the at-reveal logprob/rank instead of the converging step's), and adds a pre-profiling GPU stash allocation. Top-k requests now compute top-k on every denoising step, increasing sampler latency (~1.86ms to ~4.57ms in the all-topk benchmark) without raising peak memory.

**Potential risks**  
Performance regression for top-k logprobs workloads due to per-step top-k computation, and a new fixed GPU stash allocation that consumes memory for all runs with max_logprobs>0. The wide-logprobs path still performs explicit host syncs (inside gpu_sync_allowed) and uses a Python dict keyed by slot, which could be fragile under aborts or slot reuse. Behavior change for logprobs=0 in mixed batches is intentional but alters prior outputs, and the refactor of shared helpers touches diffusion_gemma, so regressions there are possible.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is subtle: assigning a Python scalar to a single element of a 1-D GPU tensor triggers a blocking copy_, whereas row assignment or fill_ does not. The fix cleverly avoids the host learning which slots converge or commit by writing per-step canvas rows and letting the scheduler slice num_sampled rows. The PR is well-validated with before/after test results, output comparisons against stock code, and new targeted tests, though the top-k latency tradeoff and wide-logprobs sync fallback are the main areas to scrutinize.

## 63. [[Feature] Add optional GPU timing to iteration metrics](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61690)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #61690
- **Author:** [tedzhouhk](https://github.com/tedzhouhk)
- **Merged By:** [benchislett](https://github.com/benchislett)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 13:26:12
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61690/files) (18):**
  - `tests/v1/core/test_scheduler.py`
  - `tests/v1/engine/test_iteration_logging.py`
  - `tests/v1/executor/test_multiproc_executor.py`
  - `tests/v1/metrics/test_logging_stat_logger.py`
  - `tests/v1/metrics/test_stats.py`
  - `tests/v1/worker/test_gpu_worker.py`
  - `tests/v1/worker/test_iteration_timing.py`
  - `vllm/config/observability.py`
  - `vllm/config/vllm.py`
  - `vllm/v1/core/sched/scheduler.py`
  - `vllm/v1/engine/core.py`
  - `vllm/v1/executor/abstract.py`
  - `vllm/v1/executor/multiproc_executor.py`
  - `vllm/v1/metrics/loggers.py`
  - `vllm/v1/metrics/stats.py`
  - `vllm/v1/outputs.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu_worker.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/iteration_timing.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds optional GPU-side iteration timing to the existing --enable-logging-iteration-details logs. The output worker records CUDA events around execute_model and sample_tokens (and dummy batches) via decorators in a new vllm/v1/worker/iteration_timing.py, and completed records ride on later step outputs or dummy-batch replies. EngineCore's wall-clock timing is removed: SchedulerStats.iteration_details is replaced by iteration_records, elapsed_ms becomes optional, and a new VllmConfig.gpu_iteration_timing property gates where the worker times. The logger omits the elapsed-time clause when unset.

**Technical impact**  
Touches the engine step paths (step, step_with_batch_queue, run_busy_loop, execute_dummy_batch), the executor interface (execute_dummy_batch now returns records), the scheduler's update_from_output, ModelRunnerOutput, SchedulerStats, and the logging stat logger. It is a public-ish stats interface change: SchedulerStats.iteration_details is removed and consumers must read iteration_records, where elapsed_ms may be None. Timing is non-blocking (event query only) and bookkeeping stays in the worker's main thread with no locks or new channels.

**Potential risks**  
Configurations outside the gate (non-cuda devices, pooling, encoder-only, ray/external_launcher/custom executors, VLLM_ENABLE_V1_MULTIPROCESSING=0) lose the elapsed time they previously logged, a behavior regression for those users. Records pending at shutdown are never logged, and with DP+PP+async scheduling records can be logged out of order. Custom workers/schedulers that bypass the decorated methods or drop iteration_records will log no Iteration line at all. The PR itself notes no server run has exercised the untimed line or the engine-side changes after the last three commits, and the CUDA-error path is only unit-tested with fakes.

**Key insights**  
The design is deliberately minimal: no flush, polling, thread or timeout, with records piggybacking on existing replies and a single producer per engine (worker where gpu_iteration_timing holds, EngineCore otherwise). Validation is extensive (25 server runs, ~48k iterations, record-integrity checker, latency comparisons) but largely predates the final engine-side commits, which are covered only by unit tests and a simulated driver. The main review concerns are the silent loss of elapsed time in unsupported configs, the removed SchedulerStats.iteration_details field, and the untested shutdown/ordering edge cases.

## 64. [[Structured Output] Simplify allocate_token_bitmask](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68484)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68484
- **Author:** [yzong-rh](https://github.com/yzong-rh)
- **Merged By:** [arpera](https://github.com/arpera)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 13:23:31
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68484/files) (1):**
  - `vllm/v1/structured_output/utils.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The allocate_token_bitmask function in vllm/v1/structured_output/utils.py was simplified by removing the try/except fallback that retried allocation without pin_memory when pinned allocation failed. The author argues PIN_MEMORY already reflects actual platform support, so the fallback copied from llguidance is unnecessary and an antipattern.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior changes only in the failure path: if pinned allocation raises a RuntimeError despite PIN_MEMORY being true, the error now propagates instead of silently degrading to non-pinned memory. The happy path is unchanged, and the function is simpler with fewer branches and no warning log.

**Potential risks**  
If PIN_MEMORY is ever true on a platform or environment where pinned allocation can still fail (e.g., resource exhaustion, driver issues, or misconfigured platform detection), callers will now see a hard failure rather than a graceful fallback. The PR relies on the invariant that PIN_MEMORY is true iff pinned memory is supported, which should be verified across platforms.

**Key insights**  
This is a small but behavior-affecting cleanup that trades resilience for simplicity based on a platform invariant. The provided test run (162 passed, 6 xfailed) covers structured output backends but likely does not exercise the removed failure path, so the invariant assumption is the main thing to scrutinize.

## 65. [[MoE][NVIDIA] Support MXFP4 experts on SM107 in flashinfer_moe_ep_cutedsl](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/66158)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #66158
- **Author:** [Victor49152](https://github.com/Victor49152)
- **Merged By:** [wzhao18](https://github.com/wzhao18)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 12:37:27
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/66158/files) (7):**
  - `docs/serving/expert_parallel_deployment.md`
  - `tests/kernels/moe/test_flashinfer_moe_ep.py`
  - `tests/models/test_deepseek_v4_fi_moe_ep.py`
  - `vllm/config/kernel.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/fused_moe/experts/flashinfer_moe_ep.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/fused_moe/flashinfer_moe_ep.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/fused_moe/oracle/mxfp4.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds MXFP4 expert support to the flashinfer_moe_ep_cutedsl backend on SM107 (Rubin) by extending the capability gate, adding a per-weight-format FlashInfer version probe, selecting FlashInfer's Sm107_Mxfp8_Mxfp4_Bf16_Cutedsl_MegaMoeConfig at runtime, and passing checkpoint MXFP4 weights (with E8M0 scales reinterpreted as float8_e8m0fnu) through without bf16 dequantization. The FlashInferMoeEp adapter's block_fp8 flag is generalized to a weight_format parameter, and the reload refusal on SM107 is broadened to all formats. Docs, kernel config comments, and tests (CPU selection tests plus a new bit-exact SM107 MXFP4 GPU test) are updated accordingly.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior changes only on SM107: MXFP4 layers now select the CuTeDSL megakernel instead of being refused, and the DeepGEMM megakernel remains restricted to SM100/SM103 via a new kernel clause. SM100/SM103 paths (bf16 requantization) are intended to be unchanged, and the new weight_format argument is threaded through the experts layer and tests. The NVSHMEM sizing bound in docs is raised to (2*top_k+2)*hidden*max_batched_tokens per layer shape/format to account for 1-byte MXFP4 activation staging.

**Potential risks**  
Validation is limited: SM107 end-to-end runs are single-run GSM8K on one node with no CI coverage, and the SM100/SM103 GPU tests were not re-run (all skipped), so the no-behavior-change claim rests on code inspection and CPU tests. The FlashInfer 0.7.2.post1 refusal path was only exercised with a fake module, not a real install. The SwiGLU up clamp is untested, MXFP4 requires hidden/intermediate divisible by 128, and the weight_format cast from weight_quant_dtype assumes only nvfp4/mxfp4/fp8_block reach this path.

**Key insights**  
The change is well-scoped and reuses the existing per-format capability table and probe pattern from the base NVFP4 PR, with a clean generalization of block_fp8 to weight_format. The bit-exact MXFP4 exactness test (E8M0 scale hand-off, gate/up ordering, per-expert scale indexing, MXFP8 scale exchange, gate clamp) is the strongest evidence short of end-to-end, but the lack of SM100/SM103 regression runs and the single-run statistical power (~2-3 point resolution) are the main gaps reviewers should weigh.

## 66. [[Bugfix][Core] Reject batch-sharded sampling with LoRA at startup](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56074)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #56074
- **Author:** [kushaldabbe](https://github.com/kushaldabbe)
- **Merged By:** [TheEpicDolphin](https://github.com/TheEpicDolphin)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 12:13:04
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56074/files) (2):**
  - `tests/test_config.py`
  - `vllm/config/vllm.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a startup validation in VllmConfig._validate_batch_sharded_sampling that rejects the combination of batch-sharded sampling with LoRA when lm_head is a target (default targets or explicit lm_head/.lm_head entries), fixing a crash (NotImplementedError) at the first sampling step for models using compute_logits_local. Also adds tests covering rejection for lm_head targets and allowance for non-lm_head targets, plus a lora_config=None addition to an existing test.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior change: previously accepted configs combining --enable-lora with --enable-batch-sharded-sampling now fail fast at startup with a clear ValueError instead of crashing later. LoRA configs restricted to non-lm_head modules remain permitted. The validation relies on target_modules being None (default) or an iterable of strings, and uses suffix matching to catch prefixed module names.

**Potential risks**  
The lm_head detection is heuristic: target_modules could be a non-string type (e.g., regex or dict) in some LoRA configs, which would break the any()/endswith logic or misclassify targets. Suffix matching on '.lm_head' could theoretically match unintended module names, and the default-targets assumption (None implies lm_head included) may not hold for all model architectures. Test coverage is limited to SimpleNamespace mocks rather than real LoRA config objects.

**Key insights**  
The fix correctly scopes the blocker to lm_head-targeting LoRA per review feedback, preserving valid restricted-target use cases. The root cause is that compute_logits_local skips the TP logits gather required to apply the lm_head LoRA delta. Consider hardening the target_modules type handling and verifying the default-targets assumption against actual LoRA config implementations.

## 67. [[ROCm][Test] Skip the AITER MoE alignment test where AITER isn't supported](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62527)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #62527
- **Author:** [Concurrensee](https://github.com/Concurrensee)
- **Merged By:** [AndreasKaratzas](https://github.com/AndreasKaratzas)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 12:10:45
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62527/files) (1):**
  - `tests/kernels/moe/test_rocm_aiter_moe.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The PR changes the skip condition for test_aiter_moe_alignment_rule_holds_across_block_m in tests/kernels/moe/test_rocm_aiter_moe.py from on_cdna() to is_aiter_found_and_supported(), and updates imports accordingly (adds is_aiter_found_and_supported, drops on_cdna). The goal is to skip the test on gfx90a (CDNA2) and on ROCm installs lacking AITER, where the old gate let it run and fail inside _assert_aiter_supported().

**Technical impact**  
Test-only change with no runtime code impact. The new gate aligns the skip condition with the test's own AITER support requirement, so the test now skips rather than errors on unsupported hardware/configurations while still running on MI300X with AITER. Import surface shifts from vllm.platforms.rocm to vllm._aiter_ops for the support check.

**Potential risks**  
AMD CI only runs this file on AITER-capable GPUs, so the new skip path is not exercised in CI and relies on the author's manual simulation (patching module-level flags). If is_aiter_found_and_supported() semantics diverge from _assert_aiter_supported() in future, the gate could drift again. The PR also drops the previously proposed hidden-size divisibility gate, so the tiny-random/qwen3-next-moe failure in the AITER-ON build remains unaddressed here.

**Key insights**  
Root cause was a mismatch between on_cdna() (true on CDNA2/gfx90a) and the test's actual AITER requirement (CDNA3+ with AITER installed). Using the same predicate the test asserts on is the correct fix. Validation evidence is solid (9 passed on MI300X, 9 skipped under simulated gfx90a and missing AITER), though CI coverage of the skip path is absent and should be noted as a residual gap.

## 68. [[Bugfix] ParserEngine: scan for the tool name incrementally while streaming](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67156)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #67156
- **Author:** [LeizhenZhang](https://github.com/LeizhenZhang)
- **Merged By:** [sfeng33](https://github.com/sfeng33)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 12:09:10
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67156/files) (2):**
  - `tests/parser/engine/test_llama_json.py`
  - `vllm/parser/engine/parser_engine.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The PR fixes an O(N^2) streaming cost in ParserEngine._try_extract_name, which previously re-joined and re-searched all accumulated argument text on every argument chunk until a "name" member appeared. It adds ToolCallSlot.search_name, which incrementally scans only newly appended parts plus a retained partial-match window using the regex module's partial=True search, caching the first match for the slot's lifetime. Tests were added for correctness against full search and for linear scan cost.

**Technical impact**  
Streaming name extraction becomes effectively linear for JSON-envelope parsers (llama3_json, ripple) when output lacks a "name" member, with reported speedups from tens of seconds to about one second at 16k tokens. The change is localized to ToolCallSlot and _try_extract_name, preserves the method signature so the Mistral override is unaffected, and adds three private slot fields. Behavior is claimed identical to the prior leftmost-match semantics, including empty-name and rejected-name cases.

**Potential risks**  
Correctness depends on the partial=True window logic being exactly equivalent to a full search; the PR provides a 200k-string differential and targeted tests, which mitigates but does not eliminate risk for unusual inputs. A long-lived unterminated partial match (e.g. an open name string) still degrades to the old behavior, so worst-case cost is unchanged. The cached name assumes every call passes the same pattern, which is documented but not enforced. CI does not currently run tests/parser, and the author could not exercise the full vllm serve GPU path, so integration coverage is limited.

**Key insights**  
The root cause was repeated str.join plus full regex search per chunk, confirmed by profiling. The incremental window approach is a sound optimization that keeps semantics while cutting complexity. The follow-up rename to search_name clarifies the caching contract. The main residual concerns are the unenforced single-pattern invariant and the lack of CI coverage for the parser test suite.

## 69. [[Quantization] Give linear kernels a common LinearKernel base class](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68019)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68019
- **Author:** [mgoin](https://github.com/mgoin)
- **Merged By:** [vllm-bot](https://github.com/vllm-bot)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 12:05:15
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68019/files) (8):**
  - `vllm/model_executor/kernels/linear/__init__.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/kernels/linear/base.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/kernels/linear/mixed_precision/MPLinearKernel.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/kernels/linear/mxfp4/base.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/kernels/linear/mxfp6/base.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/kernels/linear/mxfp8/Mxfp8LinearKernel.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/kernels/linear/nvfp4/base.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/kernels/linear/scaled_mm/ScaledMMLinearKernel.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Introduces a common LinearKernel ABC in kernels/linear/base.py that owns the shared constructor and interface (is_supported, can_implement, executed_activation_key, input_quant_key, process_weights_after_loading, apply_weights), and rebases all seven linear kernel families (scaled-mm, block-scaled, mixed-precision, NVFP4, MXFP4, MXFP6, MXFP8) onto it, removing duplicated declarations. It also collapses four near-identical selector loops into a shared _first_usable_kernel helper with an optional extra-check callback, and gives MPLinearKernel an is_supported derived from get_min_capability. The goal is a prerequisite refactor for a key-based linear kernel oracle mirroring the MoE one, with no intended change in kernel selection.

**Technical impact**  
Touches the kernel base hierarchy and the selection code paths for MXFP8/MXFP4/MXFP6/NVFP4 and mixed-precision, so it is behavior-adjacent even though selection order, checks and failure-reason strings are claimed identical. The shared constructor now asserts can_implement and is_supported for all families, which could surface new assertion failures for kernels that previously had looser or absent constructor checks. Type annotations tighten from type[Any] to type[LinearKernel], improving mypy coverage of registries. Net reduction of ~347 lines in vllm/ reduces duplication and maintenance burden.

**Potential risks**  
The refactor is large and touches many kernel families; subtle differences in the four collapsed selection loops (check ordering, MXFP4's quant-scheme gate, failure message formatting) could change which kernel is chosen or how errors are reported, despite the author's claim of equivalence. The new base constructor asserts is_supported()/can_implement() at instantiation, which may break kernels that were previously constructed without those checks passing (e.g., in tests or edge configs). MPLinearKernel.is_supported now queries current_platform when no capability is passed, a new code path. Validation is partial: ROCm, XPU and CPU kernels are not exercised beyond mocked selection suites, the MXFP8 end-to-end load was not compared against main, and end-to-end loads were not repeated on the second commit. Two test failures are attributed to pre-existing test-order interactions but were not fully resolved.

**Key insights**  
This is a well-scoped, mostly mechanical consolidation that removes real duplication and sets up future oracle work, but it is not trivial: it changes the class hierarchy and selection plumbing across all linear quant families. Reviewers should focus on verifying that _first_usable_kernel preserves exact check order and failure strings for each family (especially MXFP4's callback gate and NVFP4's use_a16 activation key), and that the new constructor assertions do not reject any kernel that was previously constructible. The PR description is unusually thorough about validation gaps, which is a strength, but the unexercised ROCm/XPU/CPU paths and the unrepeated end-to-end MXFP8 comparison are the main residual risks to confirm before merge.

---

Purely additive recipe metadata; no existing presets or code paths are modified. It introduces a new hardware/strategy/variant combination (h100, single_node_tp, fp8) that the recipe build and schema tooling will now render and validate, expanding the published preset set for this model.

## 71. [[Recipe] MiniMax-M3.1-Flash: add H200 AgentX TP8 DSpark preset](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68486)


### Base Information

- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 11:15:48

- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68486/files) (1):**
  - `recipes/models/MiniMaxAI/MiniMax-M3.1-Flash.yaml`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a new h200_agentx preset to the MiniMax-M3.1-Flash recipe YAML, documenting the InferenceX AgentX lane (pinned to c8054c5) for a single 8-GPU H200 node with TP8, FP8 KV/indexer caches, NVFP4 Marlin MoE, FlashInfer MSA, and three-token DSpark speculative decoding. It defines eight concurrency points (c1-c24) with per-point --max-num-seqs and --max-cudagraph-capture-size, plus a pinned image, env vars, and explicit DSpark speculative-config since it is off by default on Hopper.

**Technical impact**  
This is a data/config-only change to a recipe YAML that drives generated preset JSON and rendered serve commands; it introduces no code changes. It adds a new benchmarkable configuration surface (hardware h200, strategy single_node_tp, variant fp8) that downstream build/validation scripts will render and validate. The explicit speculative-config, Marlin MoE backend, prefetch load strategy, and VLLM_MARLIN_USE_ATOMIC_ADD env are the behaviorally meaningful settings for reproducing the lane.

**Potential risks**  
The preset is tightly coupled to a specific nightly image digest and InferenceX revision, so drift in upstream flags or image behavior could break reproducibility. The CUDA graph capture sizes are derived from a four-tokens-per-sequence assumption (sequence plus three draft tokens) and are not strictly 4x max-num-seqs at higher points (e.g., c12/c16 use 128, c20/c24 use 256), which may be intentional but is worth confirming against the lane. The 1M context with 0.90 memory utilization and 8192 batched tokens could be memory-sensitive on H200 and may fail to start if the image or model changes.

**Key insights**  
The change is well-scoped (one file, +42 lines) and the PR reports passing validate-source, build-recipes-api, test-schema, test-presets, and test-guide-html, plus a manual comparison of rendered commands at c1 and c24. The main review focus should be verifying the per-point capture-size mapping and the speculative-config JSON exactly match the InferenceX lane, since those are the most error-prone parts. Harness-only flags are correctly omitted, keeping the preset focused on serving behavior.

## 72. [[Bugfix][NIXL] Decline remote prefill reads after the producer's KV lease expired](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/66011)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #66011
- **Author:** [hugoabonizio](https://github.com/hugoabonizio)
- **Merged By:** [njhill](https://github.com/njhill)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 10:54:04
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/66011/files) (17):**
  - `docs/design/nixl_kv_cache_lease.md`
  - `docs/features/nixl_connector_usage.md`
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/test_bidirectional_kv_transfer.py`
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/test_multi_connector.py`
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/test_nixl_connector.py`
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/test_nixl_connector_hma.py`
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/test_nixl_heartbeat.py`
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/test_nixl_remote_read_expiry.py`
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/utils.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/nixl/base_scheduler.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/nixl/base_worker.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/nixl/metadata.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/nixl/pull_scheduler.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/nixl/pull_worker.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/nixl/push_worker.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/nixl/stats.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/nixl/utils.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes a P/D disaggregation bug where the decode side could NIXL-read KV blocks that the prefill side had already released and recycled after its lease expired, silently loading other requests' KV/SSM state (NaN logits, illegal memory access). The producer now exports remote_blocks_expiry_time for P blocks plus remote_lease_extension, and the consumer gates every remote read on that deadline (rebased by clock offset, renewed by an exact model of its own heartbeats), declining expired reads into kv_load_failure_policy and a new vllm:nixl_num_declined_expired_reads counter. NIXL_CONNECTOR_VERSION is bumped 16->17 and docs/tests are updated.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior change: under the default kv_load_failure_policy=fail, requests whose producer lease lapsed now fail with an error instead of silently reading corrupted KV; with recompute they are prefilled locally. The gate now runs for all remote-prefill reads, not just bidirectional turn-2, and heartbeats are sent at the top of start_load_kv so replicated-PCP ranks and same-step reads see renewals. New scheduler/worker state (_new_heartbeat_reqs, _remote_leases, HeartbeatInfo.leases) and a shared kv_lease_extension helper are introduced; the safety margin is min(5s, kv_lease_duration/6).

**Potential risks**  
The heartbeat lease model can only lag the producer, so a heartbeat delayed past the lease, a single stall longer than extension-margin, clock drift beyond the margin, or request ids containing commas can cause false declines (requests that would have succeeded now error or recompute). Mixed-version pairs are handled by the version bump, but enforce_handshake_compat=false during rolling upgrades could regress new-producer/old-bidirectional-consumer pairs. The completion-time re-check may also decline reads that actually completed in time. Deployments with routers holding requests longer than kv_lease_duration will see new errors unless they raise the lease or set recompute.

**Key insights**  
The fix reuses existing turn-2 deadline, clock-offset, and heartbeat machinery rather than adding a new transport, and models the producer's renewal rule exactly from the consumer's own heartbeats. Validation is strong: lab reproduction ties 100% of NaN cases to expired leases, and the backport shows 0 NaN with declines only on un-heartbeated requests. The design doc explicitly enumerates what the check does not cover, and a confirmation round-trip before the read is flagged as the follow-up that would remove the remaining heartbeat-propagation assumption.

---

## 73. [[Frontend][DeepSeek-V4.1] Render tool search like deepseek-recipe](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68211)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68211
- **Author:** [robertgshaw2-redhat](https://github.com/robertgshaw2-redhat)
- **Merged By:** [sfeng33](https://github.com/sfeng33)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 10:39:01
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68211/files) (5):**
  - `tests/entrypoints/scale_out/render/test_render.py`
  - `tests/tokenizers_/fixtures/deepseek_v41/tool_search_references.txt`
  - `tests/tokenizers_/test_deepseek_v41.py`
  - `vllm/tokenizers/deepseek_v41.py`
  - `vllm/tokenizers/deepseek_v41_encoding.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The DeepSeek V4.1 prompt renderer now matches deepseek-recipe and vLLM's Rust renderer for tool search: deferred tools are excluded from the system prompt's tool list, and a user turn whose tool results contain tool_reference blocks is followed by a system turn rendering '### Updated Tool Schemas' with the referenced tools' schemas. Previously all tools (including deferred) were listed and references rendered as bare names. New tests and a Rust-derived fixture validate byte-identical output, plus error cases for all-deferred tools and unknown references.

**Technical impact**  
Changes prompt construction in vllm/tokenizers/deepseek_v41.py and deepseek_v41_encoding.py, affecting cached-prefix stability and model inputs for any V4.1 request using tools. Deferred tools are filtered via _is_deferred, schemas are normalized through _tool_schema (dropping defer_loading and null fields), and _add_updated_tool_schemas injects system turns after tool-result user turns. Requests where all tools are deferred or a reference names an unknown tool now raise ValueError (HTTP 400), matching the reference implementation.

**Potential risks**  
Prompt changes alter model behavior for tool-using V4.1 requests, and no model evals were run, so quality regressions are possible despite byte-identical matching to the reference. The _add_updated_tool_schemas logic mutates messages via pop and relies on adjacency heuristics (next_message role not user/tool), which could misplace schema turns in unusual conversation shapes. The all-deferred 400 error is a new failure mode for existing clients that previously worked. The fixture file lacks a trailing newline, a minor fragility if regenerated.

**Key insights**  
The change is well-scoped and backed by cross-implementation validation (deepseek-recipe and Rust renderer fixtures) plus a cached-token improvement claim (11,273/11,273 vs 8,668/11,324). The new tests fail on main, confirming they exercise the behavior change. Out-of-scope items (Harness tool_addition blocks, empty description handling) are explicitly documented. The main residual gap is absence of model evals, which the PR acknowledges.

**Technical impact**  
Changes default startup logging behavior for all model loads: users now see one aggregated table instead of many per-layer selection lines, and per-selection detail (candidates considered, forced vs auto, MoE backend enum) is only visible at DEBUG. Adds a try/except around the weight-loading path in BaseModelLoader that logs the table on failure and re-raises unchanged, plus new public helpers in model_inspection.py that are intended for future RPC/assertion use. Touches platform attention-backend selection logging on CUDA/ROCm/XPU/CPU, so log-level expectations in other tests or tooling may shift.

**Potential risks**  
The summary relies on duck-typed introspection (attribute scanning for FusedMoEKernel and classes under vllm.model_executor.kernels.linear, getattr on quant_method/scheme/experts_cls), which is fragile against out-of-tree plugins and future quant methods; failures are caught and downgraded to a warning, but could silently omit rows. The table is only logged from rank 0 and only reflects that rank's layers under pipeline parallelism, and selections are lost if the process is killed during weight loading. Demoting selection logs to DEBUG may break users or tests that grep for the old INFO lines, and the new try/except in load_model must not alter exception context (a test asserts __context__ is None). Validation was largely CPU-only for the later changes, with no end-to-end test asserting on the log output.

**Key insights**  
The design cleanly separates collection (structured LayerBackend entries) from formatting, enabling future RPC exposure and testability, and deliberately keeps fallback/override logs at INFO/WARNING. The failure-path logging in BaseModelLoader is a thoughtful addition since the runner never sees a failed model, though it is best-effort. Main follow-ups acknowledged by the author: no precision tag on linear rows, long kernel class names, embeddings/lm_head and cache-style layers bucketed as linear/attention, no dedicated off switch, and a likely rebase conflict with #62005 which also edits model_inspection.py and the loader hook.

## 75. [[Bugfix][KV Offload] Report the final SWA tail in first_live_chunk_idx](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68359)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68359
- **Author:** [amirfr3](https://github.com/amirfr3)
- **Merged By:** [orozery](https://github.com/orozery)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 10:22:36
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68359/files) (4):**
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/offloading_connector/test_scheduler.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/offloading/store_triggers/candidates.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/offloading/store_triggers/on_compute.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/offloading/store_triggers/on_handoff.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes a correctness bug in the KV offloading connector where OnComputeStoreHandler.first_live_chunk_idx reported the stored progress, which could be above the start of the final SWA tail segment stored at finish. The scheduler's stale-block scan therefore missed that tail, allowing a finish-time store to copy GPU blocks already reassigned to another request. The fix adds final_swa_tail_start_chunk in candidates.py and returns min(progress, tail_start) from first_live_chunk_idx, sharing _committed_num_tokens and _segment_start_chunk with select_store_candidates. OnHandoffStoreHandler keeps returning None, now with a comment and a guarding test.

**Technical impact**  
Changes scheduler stale-block scanning behavior for sliding-window groups: the scan now starts up to one alignment segment lower, so more blocks are zeroed as stale before a finish-time tail store. This prevents cross-request KV corruption in aborts, normal post-decode finishes, DCP, and blocks leaving the window before finish. The refactor extracts shared helpers without altering select_store_candidates semantics. Adds substantial new scheduler-level tests covering blocks-per-chunk, abort/stop paths, DCP, and reuse timing, plus a handoff partial-tail exclusion test.

**Potential risks**  
The lower scan bound increases stale-scan work per SWA group by up to one alignment segment, a minor cost. The fix relies on _committed_num_tokens being a valid lower bound for running requests and aborts; if that invariant breaks, the bound could be too high and the bug could resurface. The changed branch is gated by prefix_cache_retention_interval=None and alignment_blocks>1, so the end-to-end validation required a test-only harness patch and did not exercise the branch in the default H100 run; live coverage of the exact path is limited. New tests are large and depend on internal scheduler helpers, which may be brittle to future refactors.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is a mismatch between the progress-based first_live_chunk_idx and the segment-aligned start used by select_store_candidates for the final SWA tail, so the stale scan and the store disagreed on which blocks are live. Sharing _committed_num_tokens and _segment_start_chunk between the two paths is the right structural fix. The handoff handler's None is justified because supports_partial_tail excludes non-CoW sliding-window groups, and the new test locks that invariant. The PR is well-validated with failing-on-main tests and a live 2-GPU run reaching the branch, though the default serving path does not trigger it.

---

## 76. [[CI] Make the Nemotron GSM8K offload entries exercise the CPU reload](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67916)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #67916
- **Author:** [khluu](https://github.com/khluu)
- **Merged By:** [orozery](https://github.com/orozery)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 10:21:10
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67916/files) (1):**
  - `tests/evals/gsm8k/test_gsm8k_offloading.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The PR modifies the GSM8K offloading test to make the Nemotron entries actually exercise CPU reload. It adds a per-config num_shots field, bumps both Nemotron configs to 12 shots so prompts exceed the 1088-token Mamba-aligned block size, enables assert_reload, and raises accuracy_threshold to 0.68 to match the higher 12-shot scores. The stated motivation is that 5-shot prompts never filled a block, so offloading was not truly tested, and the old thresholds were noisy on H200.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior of the test changes meaningfully: the Nemotron cases now use longer prompts, assert that a reload occurred, and validate against a different accuracy band (0.68 vs 0.39/0.45). This makes the test more faithful to the offload/reload path but also changes runtime cost (longer prompts, more tokens) and the pass/fail criteria. The new num_shots field defaults to NUM_FEWSHOT, so other configs are unaffected.

**Potential risks**  
The PR description focuses on threshold noise and does not explain the num_shots/assert_reload changes, so the diff and description are inconsistent, which complicates review. Raising thresholds to 0.68 based on a narrow observed range (0.715-0.755) leaves limited margin and could reintroduce flakiness if scores drift. Enabling assert_reload may fail on configs where reload is not reliably triggered, and longer 12-shot prompts increase memory/time pressure on constrained MIG 35GB environments.

**Key insights**  
The core fix is sound in intent: aligning prompt length with block size so offloading is genuinely exercised, plus tightening thresholds to the new score distribution. However, the description should be updated to cover the num_shots and assert_reload changes, and the 0.68 threshold should be justified against variance data rather than a single observed range. Verify that assert_reload is deterministic across runs before merging.

---

## 77. [[ROCm][Recipe] Qwen3.8-Flash-Next: verify BF16 on MI325X with AITER](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/66911)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #66911
- **Author:** [frida-andersson](https://github.com/frida-andersson)
- **Merged By:** [dllehr-amd](https://github.com/dllehr-amd)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 10:15:24
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/66911/files) (1):**
  - `recipes/models/Qwen/Qwen3.8-Flash-Next.yaml`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds MI325X support to the Qwen3.8-Flash-Next recipe by marking the hardware as verified, setting bf16 as the default variant, and adding a bf16 hardware override that uses the ROCm nightly image with VLLM_ROCM_USE_AITER=1 and VLLM_ROCM_USE_AITER_FUSION_SHARED_EXPERTS=1. A short guide section documents the AITER behavior on MI325X. The change is backed by extensive TP4/MTP3 throughput and accuracy benchmarks.

**Technical impact**  
MI325X users now get a validated BF16 configuration that enables AITER kernels (FlyDSL GDN prefill, AiterUnquantizedExperts, fused shared experts) by default, yielding roughly +2% to +8% output throughput and lower TPOT/E2E latency versus AITER off. Accuracy is preserved (GSM8K within one standard error, RULER NIAH 1.00). AITER raises per-rank memory after weight loading (63.2 to 74.7 GiB), reducing KV cache from 10.75M to 10.06M tokens, still sufficient for 38 concurrent 262K-context requests.

**Potential risks**  
The override pins the vllm/vllm-openai-rocm:nightly image, so behavior can drift with nightly builds and is not a stable release. AITER-on reduces KV cache headroom, which could matter for very high concurrency or longer contexts. MI300X and the FP8 checkpoint were not tested, and the recipes schema validator was not run locally, so schema validity is unverified. Two AITER benchmark points showed higher mean TTFT (1K c8, 252K c4), though from single runs.

**Key insights**  
The PR is well-justified with multi-run medians, paired seeds, and alternating server starts, and the fusion-on arm beats fusion-off at every measured point. The decision to keep AITER's default Lamport all-reduce is supported by a dedicated comparison. The main residual concerns are reliance on a nightly image and the untested MI300X/FP8 paths, which are explicitly called out as not covered.

## 78. [[Bugfix][ROCm][MiniMax-M3.1] Fix the decode glue crash with preshuffled MXFP8 weights](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68454)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68454
- **Author:** [vgokhale](https://github.com/vgokhale)
- **Merged By:** [AndreasKaratzas](https://github.com/AndreasKaratzas)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 10:01:15
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68454/files) (1):**
  - `vllm/models/minimax_m3/amd/glue/shared.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This is a bug fix for a startup crash in the MiniMax-M3.1 decode glue on ROCm (MI355X, TP4). A prior change introduced an AITER FlyDSL MXFP8 linear kernel that preshuffles weights in place and views scales as E8M0, but the glue still read those weights as row-major, causing a KeyError 'float8_e8m0fnu' in the Triton dot_scaled GEMM. The fix makes fp8_rows() read weights via row_major_weight() and adds a dn_triton flag so the Triton down GEMM is only used when the down weight is genuinely row-major with uint8 scales, otherwise falling back to the AITER GEMM.

**Technical impact**  
Changes the weight-reading path in fp8_rows() to unshuffle preshuffled weights, and gates the Triton vs AITER down GEMM selection on a new dn_triton flag computed at build time. This restores correct behavior for both preshuffled (AITER FlyDSL) and row-major weight layouts, keeping the DSpark drafter and decode glue consistent. Scope is limited to one file (glue/shared.py) with +19/-2 lines.

**Potential risks**  
The dn_triton detection relies on an identity check (row_major_weight(down) is down.weight.data) to infer whether the weight was preshuffled, which is somewhat fragile if row_major_weight's implementation changes to return a copy or view differently. The change is only validated on MI355X TP4 with a specific checkpoint and AITER version, and CI cannot cover this path, so regressions on other hardware/configs (DSpark 3, no spec decode, AITER off) are not yet confirmed in the PR. Additional nightly validation results are still pending.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is a layout contract mismatch between the AITER FlyDSL MXFP8 kernel (in-place 16x16 shuffle, E8M0 scales) and the decode glue's assumption of row-major weights with uint8 scales. The fix correctly separates the two GEMM routes and reuses the existing row_major_weight helper already used by the DSpark drafter, minimizing new complexity. The identity-based dn_triton check is the main thing reviewers should scrutinize for robustness.

## 79. [[Quantization][GLM-5.3] Let online quantization reach the BF16 attention projections](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64373)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #64373
- **Author:** [suiyoubi](https://github.com/suiyoubi)
- **Merged By:** [wzhao18](https://github.com/wzhao18)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 10:00:05
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64373/files) (4):**
  - `tests/models/glm5next/test_online_quantization.py`
  - `vllm/models/glm5next/common/kda.py`
  - `vllm/models/glm5next/common/model.py`
  - `vllm/models/glm5next/nvidia/specialized_decode/runtime/__init__.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
GLM-5.3-Flash previously built its KDA, MLA, and DSA indexer attention projections with quant_config=None, so online quantization targets silently skipped them. This PR passes the model's quant_config into KDA and MLA (except FP8-serialized/Quark checkpoints whose MLA projections are dequantized to BF16 at load), adds an hf_to_vllm_mapper regex so the FP8 checkpoint's model.layers.N.self_attn skip-list names match vLLM module names, registers the fused in_proj_qkvbfg_a shards in packed_modules_mapping, and makes the specialized decode path reject online quantization at startup. New unit tests cover the FP8 skip-list, online composition, and MTP exclusion cases.

**Technical impact**  
Attention projections now follow the checkpoint skip list and online targets through the existing resolve_quant_method path, enabling MXFP8 online quantization of up to 196 layers on the NVFP4 checkpoint (vs 84 shared-expert layers before). Default behavior without --quantization-config is claimed unchanged (token-identical greedy outputs on FP8 and NVFP4 checkpoints). The specialized latency decode backend now fails fast rather than running on quantized KDA weights it reads as BF16. Shared quantization code is untouched; changes are localized to glm5next model wiring plus a guard in the NVIDIA specialized decode runtime.

**Potential risks**  
Accuracy data shows MXFP8 on all 196 projections shortens reasoning (58-64% of pairs shorter, z up to +11) and trends 1-2 points lower on hard long-output benchmarks, so the opt-in configuration carries a real quality cost; the recommended non-KDA config is behavior-preserving but only validated on NVIDIA, not ROCm. Third-party checkpoints whose skip lists omit a BF16 attention projection would now attempt to load it with the checkpoint method and could fail. The kv_b_proj online quantization path (absorbed into bmm weights) is untested, and the MLA attention module now receives the checkpoint config, changing KV-cache method selection (verified equivalent on GB300 only). End-to-end validation was done outside CI on an earlier PR revision.

**Key insights**  
The root cause was twofold: quant_config=None wiring plus a name mismatch between the checkpoint's model.layers.N.self_attn skip-list entries and vLLM's language_model.model.layers.N.self_attn module names, which silently defeated all 1,509 skip-list entries. The PR correctly scopes the mapper regex to self_attn only so the NVFP4 MTP exclusion (model.layers.45*) keeps its name. The KDA projections dominate both throughput and the reasoning-length shift, which justifies the non-KDA recommended default; the prefill-only disaggregated config is a clever way to get the speedup without touching decode numerics. The specialized-decode startup guard is a good defensive addition.

---

## 80. [[Bugfix][Metrics] Count preemptions in the scheduler, not on resume](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65317)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65317
- **Author:** [shiv4289](https://github.com/shiv4289)
- **Merged By:** [markmc](https://github.com/markmc)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 09:35:16
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65317/files) (5):**
  - `tests/v1/core/test_scheduler.py`
  - `tests/v1/metrics/test_logging_stat_logger.py`
  - `vllm/v1/core/sched/scheduler.py`
  - `vllm/v1/metrics/loggers.py`
  - `vllm/v1/metrics/stats.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Moves the vllm:num_preemptions counter from the frontend IterationStats (derived from the request PREEMPTED event) to the scheduler, reporting it via a new SchedulerStats.num_preempted_reqs field. This fixes undercounting of requests that are preempted and then aborted before resuming, and makes the counter increment at preemption time rather than on the next output. IterationStats.num_preempted_reqs is removed and loggers now read from SchedulerStats.

**Technical impact**  
Scheduler tracks a per-interval _num_preempted_reqs counter, reset in make_stats(), and loggers.py consumes it for both the Prometheus counter and the 'Preemptions:' log line. The PREEMPTED event and vllm:request_num_preemptions are unchanged, so per-request metrics and interval metrics remain intact. A new scheduler test validates counting of an aborted-while-preempted request and that the counter resets between stats calls.

**Potential risks**  
The counter is only incremented when self.log_stats is true, so preemption counting is gated on stats logging; if log_stats is disabled the metric silently stops moving. Behavior depends on make_stats() being called each step to drain the counter, and any path that skips stats collection could delay or drop counts. Not validated on GPU or with data parallelism, and the removed IterationStats field could break any external consumers relying on it.

**Key insights**  
Counting at the scheduler is the correct layer since the frontend cannot apply events for already-dropped aborted requests. The change is well-scoped and backed by a targeted test plus end-to-end CPU evidence showing accurate counts under aborts. The main review concern is the log_stats gating and confirming no downstream code still reads IterationStats.num_preempted_reqs.

---

## 81. [[Frontend][Multimodal] Return mm_hashes from /inference/v1/generate](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68135)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68135
- **Author:** [hickeyma](https://github.com/hickeyma)
- **Merged By:** [DarkLight1337](https://github.com/DarkLight1337)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 09:25:51
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68135/files) (9):**
  - `docs/serving/online_serving/token_in_token_out.md`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/inference/generate.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/inference/generate/types.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/tests.rs`
  - `tests/entrypoints/scale_out/token_in_token_out/test_generate_stream.py`
  - `tests/entrypoints/scale_out/token_in_token_out/test_serving_multimodal_tokens.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/scale_out/token_in_token_out/mm_features.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/scale_out/token_in_token_out/protocol.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/scale_out/token_in_token_out/serving.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a new mm_hashes field to the /inference/v1/generate response (both streaming and non-streaming, Python and Rust frontends) alongside mm_placeholders. The hashes are the processor cache keys the engine actually used, so multi-turn callers can pass earlier media back as features without constructing uuid:/content: keys themselves. Docs and tests are updated to use turn1["mm_hashes"] instead of the uuid workaround.

**Technical impact**  
Extends the public generate response schema with an optional per-modality map of string keys, gated on return_token_ids, multimodal prompt, and non-encoder-decoder, mirroring mm_placeholders. Python adds a helper mm_hashes_from_engine_input plus private response attrs and streaming exclusion logic; Rust refactors placeholder extraction into MmResponseInfo so hashes and ranges stay parallel and sorted together, emitting them only on the first stream chunk. This is additive and backward compatible for existing clients.

**Potential risks**  
The Rust frontend still requires kwargs_data in features, so returned keys cannot yet be round-tripped there, and with --mm-processor-cache-gb 0 the keys are unusable; both are documented but could confuse callers. Correctness depends on hashes and placeholder ranges staying index-parallel per modality, which the shared sort in extract_mm_response_info enforces but any future divergence could silently misalign. Streaming logic must reliably clear mm_hashes after the first chunk to avoid leaking metadata on later chunks.

**Key insights**  
The change fixes a real usability gap where server-side processor/media IO kwargs and uuid-less items produced cache keys clients could never reconstruct. Returning server-computed keys is the right abstraction and avoids duplicating /render's tensor semantics. Test coverage spans unit, streaming, and Rust route tests, but the Rust round-trip limitation means the feature is only fully usable on the Python frontend for now.

## 82. [[ROCm][CI] Force the ASM path in test_online_mxfp4_hadamard[aiter-asm]](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68188)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68188
- **Author:** [mirza-halilcevic](https://github.com/mirza-halilcevic)
- **Merged By:** [AndreasKaratzas](https://github.com/AndreasKaratzas)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 09:16:40
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68188/files) (1):**
  - `tests/quantization/test_input_hadamard.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The PR modifies tests/quantization/test_input_hadamard.py to monkeypatch rocm_aiter_ops.is_asm_fp4_gemm_tuned and is_triton_gemm_afp4wfp4_presh_ws_tuned to always return True in the fused case. This fixes CI failures on MI355 DPX nodes (128 CUs) where AITER's tuned a4w4 config lookup, keyed by CU count, found no rows for the test shape, causing the layer to fall back to the Triton path and fail the input_hadamard_fused assertion.

**Technical impact**  
Test-only change with no production code impact. It decouples the fused Hadamard rotation test from AITER's device-specific tuned config tables, making the test deterministic across CU counts (128 vs 256) while still exercising the ASM GEMM path via AITER's default config for the shape. The same assertions and shapes are preserved.

**Potential risks**  
Monkeypatching internal AITER selection predicates means the test no longer validates that real tuned configs exist for the shape on the target hardware, so a regression in AITER's tuned tables would go undetected here. The 128-CU validation was simulated on a 256-CU device via a fake get_cu_num, so true DPX hardware coverage relies on the CI job rather than the local reproduction.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is environmental (CU-count-keyed AITER config tables lacking 128-CU rows on gfx950), not a code defect, so forcing the path is a reasonable narrow exception to the no-patching-methods guidance. The alternative of skipping on DPX would remove the only MI355 coverage of the fused path. The change is well-scoped, documented, and validated with before/after evidence.

  - `vllm/transformers_utils/config.py`
  - `vllm/transformers_utils/configs/__init__.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/model_runner.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a new multimodal model, Inkling2ForConditionalGeneration (594B MoE, text+image, up to 1M context), plus a DSpark speculative-decoding draft, new CUDA/Triton/Gluon kernels (fused all-reduce+conv+mix, sequence-parallel prefill, relative-bias attention, NVFP4/MXFP8 expert GEMMs), quantization metadata handling, LoRA support, and Inkling2 renderer/parser support in both Python and Rust frontends. It also touches shared code: CMake build flags, ops.h/torch_bindings, model registry, config, and a replicate_hidden option in Inkling's conv state.

**Technical impact**  
Large, self-contained addition (~17k lines) that mostly lives under vllm/models/inkling2, csrc/inkling2, and the Inkling renderer/parser, with small shared-code hooks. It introduces a new quantization method (inkling2) that only quantizes routed experts, a new DSpark draft config resolution path, and a new on_target_weights_replaced hook so the draft re-derives its folded LM head after weight updates. Requires Blackwell (SM100) and NVLink multicast for the fused kernels; MXFP8 at TP4 has significant memory pressure (146 GiB/GPU weights, ~25 GiB KV for a 16K request with default batched tokens).

**Potential risks**  
The new tests are excluded from CI via the tethering allowlist, so regressions in the kernels, model, and DSpark path would not be caught automatically. The fused MNNVL all-reduce and sequence-parallel kernels use custom synchronization (Lamport buffers, mbarriers, system-scope atomics) that is hard to validate and could deadlock or race under unusual batch shapes or multi-node topologies. Shared-code edits (CMake arch gating, quantization override ordering, config tokenizer_mode, parallel_tool_calls plumbing) could affect other models. The Inkling2 detection heuristics in the parser/renderer (presence of <\\|content_xml\\|> and absence of audio tokens) are implicit and could misfire on future vocabularies.

**Key insights**  
The PR is carefully scoped: platform-specific code is isolated under nvidia/ with shared configs/processing at the package root, and numerics are documented to match the reference implementation. Validation is thorough (accuracy evals, NIAH to 1M, compute-sanitizer, serving smoke tests on both checkpoints, Python and Rust frontends) but performed outside CI. The main review concerns are the CI coverage gap for a very large, hardware-specific surface and the complexity of the custom collective kernels.

## 84. [[ROCm][Bugfix] Fix AITER shared-expert fusion routing for gate-less MoE (DeepSeek-V2-Lite)](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64737)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #64737
- **Author:** [xaguilar-amd](https://github.com/xaguilar-amd)
- **Merged By:** [dllehr-amd](https://github.com/dllehr-amd)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 09:11:28
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64737/files) (5):**
  - `tests/kernels/moe/test_rocm_aiter_topk.py`
  - `tests/model_executor/layers/test_fused_shared_expert.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/fused_moe/layer.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/fused_moe/router/aiter_shared_routed_fused_moe_router.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/fused_moe/router/router_factory.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes a silent MoE routing bug on ROCm where AiterSharedRoutedFusedMoERouter assumed every softmax-routed model with fused shared experts appends shared-expert gate logits to router_logits. For gate-less models like DeepSeek-V2-Lite, the last routed expert columns were misread as gate logits, making those experts unselectable and producing wrong shared-expert weights. The fix threads a new has_shared_expert_gate flag from FusedMoEFactory through create_fused_moe_router into the router, which now runs softmax top-k directly over routed logits into the pre-filled AITER buffer when no gate exists, and asserts shared_expert_weight == 1.0 since the buffer is shared across layers.

**Technical impact**  
Restores correct routing for gate-less fused-shared-expert models (DeepSeek-V2-Lite, Mistral Large 4 local impl) while leaving gated paths (Qwen3-Next) unchanged, as confirmed by GSM8K results matching fusion-off baselines. The gate flag is derived from the same condition MoERunner uses to concatenate gate weights, so the router_logits layout matches by construction across backends. Routing stays a single kernel launch in the same in-place branch, and the new assertion guards against per-layer shared-expert weights the shared buffer cannot honor.

**Potential risks**  
The new shared_expert_weight == 1.0 assertion could trip for any future or out-of-tree model that fuses shared experts with a non-unit weight, turning a silent misroute into a hard failure. The fix relies on the factory-derived flag being kept in sync with MoERunner's logit concatenation logic; divergence would reintroduce the bug. The affected CI steps are optional in test-amd.yaml, so regressions may not be caught on PRs by default, and the Mistral Large 4 validation uses a local, non-upstream model implementation.

**Key insights**  
The bug was a shape-compatible silent correctness failure, which is why it produced plausible but wrong outputs rather than crashes; the PR's paired McNemar analysis and before/after GSM8K numbers give strong evidence of the fix. The added router test covers both gated and gate-less cases with BF16/FP32 and verifies selection against the softmax cutoff to handle tie boundaries. The change also unlocks a measurable TPOT improvement (up to 21% at concurrency 1) for gate-less models that previously could not safely use shared-expert fusion.

## 85. [[Bugfix][Metrics] Restart the logging interval clock on empty intervals](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55474)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #55474
- **Author:** [WatchTree-19](https://github.com/WatchTree-19)
- **Merged By:** [markmc](https://github.com/markmc)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 09:10:02
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55474/files) (2):**
  - `vllm/v1/metrics/perf.py`
  - `vllm/v1/spec_decode/metrics.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Both SpecDecodingLogging.log() and PerfMetricsLogging.log() previously returned early on empty intervals without advancing last_log_time, so the next busy interval divided its counters by the entire idle gap, under-reporting throughput and MFU. The fix calls reset() on the empty branch in both classes, which restarts the clock and also clears the accumulators that PerfMetricsDebugLogging divides by the same delta_time. Two failing tests were added in tests/v1/metrics/test_perf_metrics.py.

**Technical impact**  
Corrects throughput and MFU reporting after quiet stretches by ensuring the logging interval clock and related accumulators restart on empty intervals. Using reset() rather than only assigning last_log_time keeps mfu_calc_overhead from exceeding 100% in PerfMetricsDebugLogging. Behavior change is confined to the metrics logging path and does not affect request handling.

**Potential risks**  
The tests were run only against upstream modules with heavy transitive imports stubbed and no GPU was available, so real-environment validation is still pending. Calling reset() on the empty path could subtly alter accumulator semantics for any downstream consumer that expected counters to persist across idle intervals, though the change appears intentional and consistent. Risk is low given the small two-line diff.

**Key insights**  
The bug was a silent correctness issue producing plausible but wrong throughput numbers alongside correct totals, which is easy to miss in review. The subtlety that reset() is required over a bare clock assignment, because PerfMetricsDebugLogging shares delta_time, is the key design point. The change is minimal and well-targeted, but the stubbed-import test setup warrants a follow-up run in a genuine environment.

---

  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/rotary_embedding/base.py`

  - `vllm/v1/attention/backends/rocm_attn.py`
  - `vllm/v1/attention/backends/rocm_segmented_attn.py`
  - `vllm/v1/attention/backends/triton_attn.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Migrates the base RotaryEmbedding from a plain CustomOp to a vLLM IR op (vllm.ir.ops.rotary_embedding plus a query-only variant), adding native, vllm_c (new out-of-place CUDA/ROCm kernel rotary_embedding_out) and aiter providers, IR-op priority wiring on CUDA/ROCm, and matcher updates so QK-norm+RoPE(+KVCache) fusion patterns match a stable IR node without forcing custom_ops=['+rotary_embedding']. It also removes the three +rotary_embedding force-appends in compilation.py, adds a functional_impl mechanism to IR ops, makes the fused RoPE+KV-cache op functional (returns q/k instead of mutating), and adds a post-lowering no-op elimination pass. The goal is to resolve the long-standing #28042 pattern-match failure and complete the ROCm/AITER path.

**Technical impact**  
RoPE now emits the same IR node on every forward path, so fusion passes match regardless of custom_ops and the actual kernel is selected by IR-op priority during lowering. The new rotary_embedding_out C++ kernel enables out-of-place lowering without defensive clones, and the fused_rope_and_unified_kv_cache_update op becomes functional with a signature-compatibility shim for out-of-tree backends. Attention backends gain optional query_out/key_out parameters, and the noop-elimination pass now handles view chains and is mutation-aware. This is a broad, cross-cutting change touching kernels, IR, compilation passes, platform priorities, and multiple attention backends.

**Potential risks**  
The PR is explicitly a draft and runtime-unverified (no GPU/ROCm box), so correctness of the new CUDA kernel, AITER dispatch, and fusion matching is unconfirmed; gsm8k parity is still pending. The functionalization change to fused_rope_and_unified_kv_cache_update and the new post-lowering noop elimination could alter graph semantics or performance in subtle ways, and the mutation-aware reshape bypass logic is delicate. Removing the forced +rotary_embedding could regress paths that still rely on the custom op (MRoPE, DeepseekScaling, flashinfer, key=None cross-layer sharing), and the AITER priority gating depends on env flags that may behave differently in real deployments. The large diff (45 files, +1655/-267) also raises review and integration risk.

**Key insights**  
The design keeps MatcherRotaryEmbedding (unlike the closed #43199) to preserve the AITER QK-norm+RoPE+KVCache fusion added after that PR, and introduces a functional_impl hook so in-place kernels can lower without cloning activations. Kernel selection is now driven by IR-op priority rather than a global custom-op flag, with ROCm preferring AITER when enabled. The change is well-scoped to base RotaryEmbedding with follow-ups deferred, but it must be runtime-verified (unit tests, fusion tests, and accuracy parity) before leaving draft, since static checks alone cannot validate the new kernel or fusion behavior.

## 87. [[CI][Test] Expect the decode-crossed Mamba hit in test_qwen_mtp_mamba_prefix_cache_hit_is_bounded](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68356)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68356
- **Author:** [stefankoncarevic](https://github.com/stefankoncarevic)
- **Merged By:** [AndreasKaratzas](https://github.com/AndreasKaratzas)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 08:15:15
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68356/files) (1):**
  - `tests/v1/e2e/test_hybrid_chunked_prefill.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The test test_qwen_mtp_mamba_prefix_cache_hit_is_bounded is updated to expect a cache hit of 2 * block_size instead of block_size, with an explanatory comment. This aligns the test with the intended behavior introduced by #67053, which now caches the Mamba state at the last boundary crossed during decode, causing wave 2 to resume at two blocks. The change is test-only with no production code modification.

**Technical impact**  
Only the assertion threshold in a single e2e test changes; the output-equality checks and the bounded-hit guarantee remain intact. The test now correctly validates the new sparse-retention caching semantics for hybrid Mamba prefix caching, restoring nightly CI coverage on MI355X and B200. No runtime behavior is affected.

**Potential risks**  
The new expectation is tied to the specific decode length of wave 1 relative to block_size; if wave 1's decode depth or block_size changes, the test could become brittle. B200 was not validated locally, though the reported 1152 == 2 * block_size failure is consistent with the new expectation. The test still fails on any hit beyond the 2 * block_size boundary, so it retains its bounding purpose.

**Key insights**  
The failure was a stale test expectation rather than a regression, since #67053 intentionally extended caching to the last decode-crossed boundary. The nightly-only job meant #67053's CI did not exercise this test, highlighting a coverage gap. The change is minimal and well-justified, with clear validation evidence on MI355X and a plausible explanation for B200.
