## 88. [[Feat] Support multiple structured-output backends on one server](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67335)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #67335
- **Author:** [yzong-rh](https://github.com/yzong-rh)
- **Merged By:** [arpera](https://github.com/arpera)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 08:09:11
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67335/files) (15):**
  - `tests/v1/core/test_scheduler.py`
  - `tests/v1/e2e/general/accuracy_utils.py`
  - `tests/v1/structured_output/test_backend_xgrammar_stop_tokens.py`
  - `tests/v1/structured_output/test_grammar_backends.py`
  - `tests/v1/structured_output/test_structured_output_manager.py`
  - `tests/v1/structured_output/test_validation.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/generate/beam_search/offline.py`
  - `vllm/v1/engine/core.py`
  - `vllm/v1/structured_output/__init__.py`
  - `vllm/v1/structured_output/backend_guidance.py`
  - `vllm/v1/structured_output/backend_lm_format_enforcer.py`
  - `vllm/v1/structured_output/backend_outlines.py`
  - `vllm/v1/structured_output/backend_types.py`
  - `vllm/v1/structured_output/backend_xgrammar.py`
  - `vllm/v1/structured_output/utils.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The StructuredOutputManager previously latched a single grammar backend per engine and rejected requests resolving to a different backend with a 400 error. This PR replaces the single backend/backend_name fields with a dict cache of backends keyed by name, lazily instantiating xgrammar, guidance, outlines, or lm-format-enforcer per request, and moves token bitmask allocation into a shared allocate_token_bitmask utility in utils.py used by all backends and callers.

**Technical impact**  
Enables mixed structured-output workloads (e.g. strict tool calling via xgrammar plus guidance-only schemas like multipleOf) on one server under backend=auto. The per-backend allocate_token_bitmask abstract method is removed from StructuredOutputBackend and its implementations, so all backends now share a single manager-owned bitmask sized to model vocab width; grammar compilation is routed to the correct backend instance. Guidance's tokenizer construction now requires the model vocab size to match the tokenizer vocab size, raising VLLMValidationError on mismatch.

**Potential risks**  
Behavior change in guidance: previously it used max(vocab_size, len(tokenizer)) and now passes vocab_size directly, and Mistral tokenizers with mismatched vocab sizes now raise instead of proceeding, which could break existing deployments. The shared bitmask assumes all backends tolerate the same logits-width sizing; tests xfail guidance/outlines when model vocab is smaller than tokenizer vocab, indicating known edge cases. Multiple backends now coexist in memory, increasing resource usage, and clear_backend must correctly destroy all of them. The removed PR49625 regression test means the old single-backend rejection path is no longer guarded, though it is intentionally obsolete.

**Key insights**  
The refactor cleanly centralizes bitmask allocation and backend caching, reducing duplication across backends. The validation evidence is strong, showing all four mixed constraints passing on both Qwen and Mistral models where main failed. Reviewers should focus on the guidance vocab-size strictness change, memory/lifecycle of multiple backends, and whether the shared bitmask sizing is correct for all backends under speculative decoding.

## 89. [[ROCm][Bugfix] W4A16 dense prefill cache: drop the load-time memory budget check](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64744)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #64744
- **Author:** [zohaibm-amd](https://github.com/zohaibm-amd)
- **Merged By:** [dllehr-amd](https://github.com/dllehr-amd)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 08:04:22
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64744/files) (2):**
  - `tests/kernels/quantization/test_rdna_hybrid_w4a16.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/kernels/linear/mixed_precision/rdna_hybrid_w4a16.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Removes the load-time memory budget check (_fits_in_memory_budget) from the RDNA hybrid W4A16 prefill dequant path and deletes its three unit tests, simplifying the test helper accordingly. The check was found to be stricter than the engine's own KV-cache sizing (counting allocator-cached blocks, a fixed 4 GiB activation headroom, and other-process memory), so it rejected configs that actually fit; the engine's KV-cache startup check already covers the genuinely over-budget case.

**Technical impact**  
Weight loading for kernel_config.rdna_hybrid_w4a16_prefill_dequant=enable no longer raises a kernel-specific ValueError; over-budget configs now fail later at the engine's KV-cache sizing step with a generic error. No runtime kernel behavior changes, only load-time validation is removed, and the informational caching log remains. Test coverage for the removed check is dropped while the cached-copy test still exercises the caching path.

**Potential risks**  
Over-budget configurations now fail after the profile run rather than mid-load, and the error message no longer names the prefill_dequant option, which could make diagnosis harder for users. The all-or-nothing caching invariant is now implicitly protected only by the engine's KV check, so any future change to that check could reintroduce partial-cache or OOM scenarios. Validation is limited to gfx1151 hardware, so behavior on other ROCm targets is less evidenced.

**Key insights**  
The PR correctly identifies that duplicating memory-budget logic in the kernel layer diverged from the engine's authoritative sizing and caused false rejections. Removing redundant validation in favor of a single source of truth is a sound simplification, though the trade-off in error clarity and failure timing should be documented for users. The end-to-end table showing previously failing utilizations now starting with valid KV sizes supports the claims.

## 91. [[ROCm][Perf][MoE] Dequantize only distinct routed experts in OCP MX emulation](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68086)

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a new Triton module (ocp_mx_routed_dequant.py) that deduplicates a batch's routed experts on-GPU and dequantizes only those distinct experts for MXFP4 MoE weights on ROCm with bf16 activations. The existing per-slot gather path in ocp_mx_emulation_moe.py is refactored into a _dequantize_routed method that selects the fused path when gating conditions hold, otherwise falling back to the prior per-slot or full-dequant behavior. Tests are extended to cover the fused path, a bit-exactness check against quark, and dedup edge cases.

**Technical impact**  
Replaces a host-sync-free but slot-redundant dequant with a two-launch dedup (histogram/rank then renumber) plus a 2-D-grid dequant kernel that skips unused slots, keeping shapes dependent only on topk_ids.shape so it stays CUDA-graph capturable. Reported gains are large (2.1x throughput at concurrency 4, up to ~20x on skewed routing in layer microbenchmarks) with bit-identical outputs versus quark's default dq_mxfp4 apart from NaN payloads. Gating is conservative (ROCm, bf16, w_mxfp4*, contiguous weights/scales, no dynamic fp8 a2 scale), so other schemes and dtypes keep the previous path.

**Potential risks**  
Peak transient memory scales with C = min(numel, E) bf16 experts, which can be much larger than main's synced eager torch.unique for skewed batches (about 21 GB vs 5 GB at I=2048), a real OOM risk for large expert counts. Correctness depends on subtle invariants: compacted used slots, -1 termination, do_not_specialize guards (the one-id case silently breaks without it), and unused slots never being read, which is only safe because dynamic fp8 a2 scale is rejected. Bit-identity is only against quark's default HIP impl; the triton impl differs on scale-0 subnormals. The GEMM config lookup uses C as expert count, which may mis-tune kernels for some shapes.

**Key insights**  
The design cleanly separates dedup into two launches to avoid an intra-kernel global-memory handoff and barrier, and relies on compacted used slots so the dequant loop can early-exit at the first -1, making unused slots nearly free. Validation is unusually thorough (exhaustive code/scale check vs quark, 840 dedup cases, 612 end-to-end configs, 3000 graph replays, NaN-poisoned unused slots), and the PR explicitly documents the memory trade-off and the triton-impl divergence. The main open concern is the transient memory blowup for skewed large batches, which reviewers should weigh against the throughput wins.

## 92. [[Benchmark] Add --random-mm-video-encode-fps to random-mm](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65493)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65493
- **Author:** [sahirema](https://github.com/sahirema)
- **Merged By:** [dllehr-amd](https://github.com/dllehr-amd)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 07:48:44
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65493/files) (4):**
  - `docs/benchmarking/cli.md`
  - `rust/src/bench/tests/cli_parity.rs`
  - `rust/src/bench/tests/python_serve_flags.txt`
  - `vllm/benchmarks/datasets/datasets.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a new --random-mm-video-encode-fps CLI flag (default 30.0, matching the previously hardcoded value) that is threaded through sample() and generate_mm_item() into generate_synthetic_video(), replacing the hardcoded fps=30 in the MP4 writer. The motivation is that num_frames in --random-mm-bucket-config only maps to the model's processed frame count when the container encode rate matches the target model's native sampling fps. Also updates docs, the Rust CLI-parity allowlist (PYTHON_ONLY), and the python_serve_flags.txt snapshot.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior is backward compatible by default, but setting the flag changes the wall-clock duration represented by a fixed num_frames, which changes how much video content a duration-based resampler (e.g. Qwen2.5-VL at ~2fps) actually samples; the PR reports a 3.5x input-token difference at 2fps vs 30fps for the same bucket config. Argument validation is moved to parse time via a nested _parse_positive_float type function, giving a clear error instead of a deep cv2.VideoWriter failure. The Rust parity snapshot is kept in sync by allowlisting the flag as Python-only, so VLLM_USE_RUST_BENCH=1 delegation does not reject it.

**Potential risks**  
The Rust-side changes (cli_parity.rs and the regenerated snapshot) were not validated with a live cargo test, so parity correctness rests on manual review and snapshot regeneration only. No new unit test covers the positive-float rejection path, and the flag's effect on token counts is validated only empirically on one model/backend, so other models with different resampling behavior may see unexpected token inflation. The nested helper duplicates a pattern rather than centralizing validation, which is minor but slightly reduces reuse.

**Key insights**  
The change is small and well-scoped, with a sensible backward-compatible default and a clear rationale tied to a real benchmarking pitfall (num_frames not equaling processed frames). The main gaps are the unverified Rust parity test run and the absence of a dedicated unit test for the new argument validator; both are explicitly flagged by the author, which is good practice. Overall a reasonable benchmark-tooling improvement with low blast radius outside the random-mm dataset path.

## 93. [[ROCm][CI] Make test_amd_ci_fast_exit independent of inherited signal state](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67567)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #67567
- **Author:** [AndreasKaratzas](https://github.com/AndreasKaratzas)
- **Merged By:** [AndreasKaratzas](https://github.com/AndreasKaratzas)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 07:45:42
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67567/files) (2):**
  - `.buildkite/scripts/hardware_ci/run-amd-test.sh`
  - `tests/tools/test_amd_ci_fast_exit.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The test helper _run now passes a preexec_fn that resets SIGSEGV, SIGBUS and SIGABRT to SIG_DFL and unblocks them in the child, so an inherited ignored/blocked fault signal on CI pods no longer turns expected crashes into exit 0. The AMD CI preflight script additionally logs the node name, KFD topology node count, and SigBlk/SigIgn masks to surface bad nodes in the log.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior of the test changes only in the child process signal disposition, making the crash-exit assertions robust to inherited signal state; assertions themselves are unchanged. The shell change is diagnostic-only logging and does not alter CI pass/fail logic. preexec_fn is used, which is generally discouraged in multithreaded parents but acceptable in this single-threaded pytest context.

**Potential risks**  
preexec_fn is unsafe in multithreaded processes and could deadlock in rare cases, though the test parent is effectively single-threaded. The root cause of the bad signal state on the two MI250 nodes is not proven, so the fix may mask rather than resolve the underlying node issue; the abort case returning -11 instead of -6 was not reproduced. The new preflight line relies on VLLM_CI_K8S_NODE_NAME and /sys/class/kfd paths that may be absent, though it degrades gracefully.

**Key insights**  
The fix correctly targets the real mechanism: ignored/blocked fault signals survive fork/exec and subprocess does not reset them, so the child's raise(SIGSEGV) returns and exits 0. The added preflight logging is a pragmatic way to gather evidence on the suspected bad nodes without blocking the fix. The PR is well-scoped and validated with simulated bad signal states, but the underlying node problem (missing amdgpu/KFD) remains open with cluster owners.

## 94. [[Feature][Multimodal] Name every uncached item in the cache-miss error](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68429)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68429
- **Author:** [danielquintas8](https://github.com/danielquintas8)
- **Merged By:** [DarkLight1337](https://github.com/DarkLight1337)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 07:40:58
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68429/files) (3):**
  - `docs/features/multimodal_inputs.md`
  - `tests/entrypoints/multimodal/openai/chat_completion/test_video.py`
  - `vllm/multimodal/processing/processor.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The cache-miss error in _get_cache_missing_items previously raised at the first item lacking data, forcing clients into one round trip per missing item. The PR now collects all missing items across modalities and raises a single ValueError naming each by modality and index, plus a new test and a docs note.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior change is limited to the error message content: the exception type, raise location (before cache update), and HTTP 400 mapping are unchanged, and single-item messages remain identical. Multi-item messages now aggregate indices per modality, joined with commas and semicolons, which breaks any client or test matching the full old single-item message when multiple items miss.

**Potential risks**  
Message format is now a de facto contract; clients or tests doing exact full-message matching on multi-miss cases will break. The PR overlaps with #55583 and #44543 on the same lines, so rebases are needed and the Qwen2.5-Omni copy would retain the old behavior. Validation was done on a CPU macOS build rather than full CI, so coverage relies on the entrypoints multimodal job.

**Key insights**  
Aggregating misses aligns the client-facing error with the engine-side MultiModalCacheMissError philosophy of recovering the whole request in one retry. The change is small and well-tested, but the message-format coupling and concurrent PR conflicts are the main things to watch before merge.

## 95. [[ROCm][Perf] Cache AiterFlashAttention K/V cache views per tensor](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/66541)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #66541
- **Author:** [phambinhfin](https://github.com/phambinhfin)
- **Merged By:** [dllehr-amd](https://github.com/dllehr-amd)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 07:36:00
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/66541/files) (3):**
  - `tests/v1/attention/test_rocm_aiter_fa_kv_view_cache.py`
  - `vllm/v1/attention/backends/rocm_aiter_fa.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/utils.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a per-impl cache of the K/V views produced by AiterFlashAttentionImpl._split_kv_cache, keyed on the kv_cache tensor identity plus its data pointer, so the ROCM_AITER_FA backend stops rebuilding transpose/split/unbind views (and FP8 retypes) on every attention and KV-cache-update call. A new use_typed flag returns cached FP8 views for quantized KV caches, and clear_layer_kv_caches now drops the cache on teardown. This mirrors prior FlashAttention and Triton optimizations and targets eager-mode decode overhead.

**Technical impact**  
Touches the hot path of a performance-critical attention backend: forward, do_kv_cache_update, and do_rope_and_kv_cache_update now share one cached view tuple instead of recomputing it. The identity plus data_ptr keying means in-place re-pointing of the tensor storage correctly invalidates the cache, and the cache is reset in clear_layer_kv_caches for profiling teardown and shutdown. Reported gains are ~2.5-3.9 us saved per call and 4.2-4.8% eager throughput/TPOT improvement, with compiled mode unchanged since uniform decode batches replay a full CUDA graph.

**Potential risks**  
Correctness depends on the assumption that a layer's KV cache tensor only changes via initialize_kv_cache, extend_kv_cache, and clear_layer_kv_caches; any future path that mutates the tensor in place without changing data_ptr or rebinding could serve stale views. The class-level default _kv_view_cache = None is shared as a fallback but is overwritten per instance, so it is safe yet slightly unusual. Not all paths were validated: shuffle layout end to end, do_rope_and_kv_cache_update (Qwen3 never exercises it), gfx950, and test_attention_backends.py were not run, leaving some coverage gaps. Holding tensor references in the cache could also extend KV cache lifetime if teardown paths miss the reset.

**Key insights**  
The change is well-scoped and backed by strong evidence: microbenchmarks, in-process step timing, serving benchmarks, bit-identical token/logprob checks, and a targeted CPU test suite that fails without the change. The data_ptr check is a pragmatic guard against in-place re-pointing, and the FP8 view caching removes a second redundant retype. Main review focus should be on confirming all KV cache mutation paths invalidate correctly and that the teardown reset is comprehensive.

## 96. [[ROCm][MLA][DCP] Serve the non-causal DSpark draft on TRITON_MLA under DCP](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64261)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #64261
- **Author:** [xaguilar-amd](https://github.com/xaguilar-amd)
- **Merged By:** [dllehr-amd](https://github.com/dllehr-amd)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 07:19:15
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64261/files) (3):**
  - `tests/v1/attention/test_mla_noncausal.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/attention/mla_attention.py`
  - `vllm/v1/attention/backends/mla/triton_mla.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR enables TRITON_MLA to serve a non-causal multi-token MLA draft (DSpark) under decode context parallelism (DCP) on gfx942. The root cause was that MLACommonMetadataBuilder.__init__ clamps the reorder_batch_threshold to 1 under DCP unless supports_dcp_with_varlen is passed, which broke FULL cudagraph capture of the multi-token draft block. The fix lets the common builder keep the spec-decode threshold under DCP when the group is non-causal and the backend declares supports_non_causal_multi_token_dcp, and TRITON_MLA now declares that flag only on gfx942 (CDNA 3). Two new unit tests guard the threshold behavior for non-causal and causal groups.

**Technical impact**  
Changes the reorder-threshold initialization logic in MLACommonMetadataBuilder, affecting metadata classification for all MLA backends under DCP. TRITON_MLA gains DCP support for non-causal multi-token decode on gfx942 only, while other ROCm targets keep current behavior. FlashInfer and TokenSpeed MLA are unaffected since they already pass supports_dcp_with_varlen=True. ROCM_AITER_MLA behavior changes only for a narrow non-causal DCP config with no configured verify route (gfx950 with interleave size != 1), which the PR argues was already broken on main.

**Potential risks**  
The flag is evaluated per metadata builder, so the draft's TRITON_MLA builder also classifies the target's causal verify batches as decode; the PR notes draft layers never run attention on that metadata, but a future drafter running causal attention through the same builder under DCP would hit a runtime assert rather than produce wrong output. The gfx942-only gate means other ROCm targets still reject forced TRITON_MLA drafts under DCP, and widening it would need separate validation. The ROCM_AITER_MLA behavior change is justified by code reading rather than direct testing, and the change relies on the assumption that all rows in a non-causal block share one KV extent.

**Key insights**  
The PR is well-validated with unit tests, end-to-end GSM8K at DCP 1 and 8, RULER NIAH at 64k, and benchmark comparisons, showing saturated accuracy and token-identical retrieval between DCP sizes. The design deliberately avoids setting supports_dcp_with_varlen=True in TRITON_MLA to prevent causal multi-token verify from hitting the forward_mqa DCP assert. The two new tests are meaningful because they fail against the variants they guard against, confirming they test the intended behavior rather than passing trivially.

---

## 97. [[Docs] Raise the bar for PR descriptions](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68404)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68404
- **Author:** [fxmarty-amd](https://github.com/fxmarty-amd)
- **Merged By:** [hmellor](https://github.com/hmellor)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 07:00:13

  - `.github/PULL_REQUEST_TEMPLATE.md`
  - `AGENTS.md`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR updates contributor-facing documentation to require stronger evidence for newly added tests: AGENTS.md now asks for a reproducible pytest command and exact pytest summary line including time, the PR template Validation hint requests the pytest summary with test time and the commit SHA tested, and the pr-checklist skill adds a matching requirement. The motivation is to make reviewer expectations explicit for new tests, following discussion in an earlier PR.

**Technical impact**  
No runtime or behavioral code changes; only markdown docs, the PR template comment, and an agent skill instruction are modified. The practical impact is on contributor workflow and review expectations, not on the codebase itself.

**Potential risks**  
Low risk since no code paths are affected. The main concern is minor inconsistency in wording across the three files (AGENTS.md scopes to unit tests, the template mentions newly added and modified tests, and the skill scopes to newly added tests), which could cause confusion about exact requirements. There is also a small chance contributors find the added disclosure burden unclear if not consistently enforced.

**Key insights**  
The change is a documentation-only policy tightening that standardizes test evidence requirements for new tests, including commit SHA and pytest summary with timing. It is scoped narrowly to avoid burdening all PRs, but aligning the phrasing across AGENTS.md, the PR template, and the checklist skill would improve clarity.

## 98. [[ROCm][Disagg][CI] Fix the NIXL cells broken by UCX 1.23's ROCm DMABUF default](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/66514)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #66514
- **Author:** [avininjamay8](https://github.com/avininjamay8)
- **Merged By:** [AndreasKaratzas](https://github.com/AndreasKaratzas)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 06:56:05
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/66514/files) (4):**
  - `.buildkite/amd-disagg/cluster.sh`
  - `.buildkite/amd-disagg/models.yaml`
  - `.buildkite/amd-disagg/pipeline-disagg.yaml`
  - `.buildkite/amd-disagg/vllm_disagg.sh`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes broken DeepSeek-V3 NIXL disagg CI cells on the stock UCX 1.23 image. cluster.sh disables UCX_ROCM_COPY_DMABUF (UCX 1.23 enables ROCm DMABUF registration by default, causing OOM in TP8 and HIP error 209 in EP8/EP16), removes tcp/lo from UCX_TLS/UCX_NET_DEVICES to force RDMA-only KV transfer, and spreads across all ionic NICs. vllm_disagg.sh sets VLLM_NIXL_SIDE_CHANNEL_HOST to the node IP so decode no longer handshakes with localhost and silently recomputes. pipeline-disagg.yaml raises ACCURACY_NUM_CONCURRENT to 32 for EP8/EP16 cells to fit the 105-minute limit, and models.yaml retunes Kimi-K3 2P2D EP16 memory (prefill 0.82, decode 0.80) with sequence/batch caps to avoid negative KV cache and startup check failures.

**Technical impact**  
Behavioral change to CI infrastructure and runtime env for NIXL cells: KV now flows exclusively over RDMA across all 8 ionic NICs (TP8 transfer time drops from 31-38 ms to ~7 ms per 137 MB), and the side-channel host fix ensures correct cross-node handshakes. Memory and concurrency tuning changes the accuracy workload profile and model startup parameters for Kimi-K3 EP16. Reported GSM8K scores now pass for all previously failing cells (0.94-0.95 range).

**Potential risks**  
The UCX_NET_DEVICES default now depends on _IB_DEVICES being populated correctly; if that variable is empty or malformed the substitution could yield an invalid device list. Removing tcp/lo eliminates a TCP fallback, so any environment lacking functional ionic RDMA will fail rather than degrade. Raising ACCURACY_NUM_CONCURRENT to 32 increases load and could surface new flakiness or timeouts on slower nodes. Higher GPU memory utilization (0.82/0.80) for Kimi-K3 EP16 leaves less headroom and may be sensitive to model or driver changes.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is an upstream UCX 1.23 default change (ROCm DMABUF registration) interacting with NIXL, not a vLLM bug, so pinning UCX_ROCM_COPY_DMABUF=no is a targeted workaround. The VLLM_NIXL_SIDE_CHANNEL_HOST defaulting to localhost is a subtle correctness bug that silently degrades to recompute rather than erroring, making it hard to detect without transfer metrics. The PR is well-validated with before/after GSM8K results and transfer timing evidence, though the NIC list construction and increased concurrency warrant monitoring in CI.

---

## 99. [[ROCm][CI][Test] Spawn the workers in the DSv4.1 mono merge AB test](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68391)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68391
- **Author:** [stefankoncarevic](https://github.com/stefankoncarevic)
- **Merged By:** [AndreasKaratzas](https://github.com/AndreasKaratzas)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 06:51:40
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68391/files) (1):**
  - `tests/models/test_dsv41_mono_layers.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The PR adds a single monkeypatch.setenv("VLLM_WORKER_MULTIPROC_METHOD", "spawn") in the merge AB test helper of tests/models/test_dsv41_mono_layers.py. Since #68027 workers are forked and inherit the pytest process's import of the module (made without DSV41_MONO_MERGE_AB), so _patch_merge_ab() never runs and the test fails with KeyError: (False, 6). Forcing spawn restores the prior behavior where workers import the module with the env var set.

**Technical impact**  
Test-only change with no production code impact. It restores the intended worker import path so the merge AB patches are applied, fixing test_mono_decode_matches_the_model[8-8] on MI355 and increasing graph capture memory to the expected 1.48 GiB. Validation on MI355X TP8 shows both [8-8] and [8-28] passing.

**Potential risks**  
Spawn-based workers are slower to start and re-import the module, which could add test runtime and memory overhead; the fix also relies on the env var being honored by the runner. The change is scoped to this test helper, so broader fork-vs-spawn behavior elsewhere is unaffected. Limited information on whether other tests share the same latent fork/import issue.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is an implicit dependency on import-time side effects (AITER/HIP init causing spawn) that #68027 removed, leaving forked workers with a stale module import. Explicitly setting the multiprocessing method is a more robust fix than relying on incidental initialization order. The failure was masked by a CI gap between builds 103737 and 103769, so the first observed failure was attributed to an unrelated PR.

## 100. [[Entrypoint] Use tri-state for add_special_tokens in pooling entrypoint.](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68344)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68344
- **Author:** [noooop](https://github.com/noooop)
- **Merged By:** [DarkLight1337](https://github.com/DarkLight1337)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 06:31:20
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68344/files) (10):**
  - `tests/entrypoints/pooling/embed/test_protocol.py`
  - `tests/entrypoints/pooling/scoring/test_io_processor_unit.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/pooling/base/io_processor.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/pooling/base/protocol.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/pooling/embed/io_processor.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/pooling/pooling/io_processor.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/pooling/pooling/protocol.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/pooling/scoring/io_processor.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/pooling/scoring/protocol.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/pooling/types.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The pooling entrypoint's add_special_tokens is changed from a plain bool to a tri-state (bool \\| None) so that None falls back to per-path defaults: the model/tokenizer default (default_cmpl_tok_params) for completion requests and False for chat requests. build_tok_params and _build_pooling_tok_params now take a default_tok_params argument and derive request-level TokenizeParams via dataclasses.replace, and OfflineInputsContext gains an add_special_tokens field plus a build_tok_params helper. This fixes multimodal models whose default_tok_params may be False, which the previous hardcoded True/False defaults ignored.

**Technical impact**  
All call sites of build_tok_params across pooling embed, scoring, and pooling io_processors are updated to pass the appropriate renderer default (chat vs cmpl), and scoring now selects chat vs cmpl tok_params based on whether the input is a list. The chat mixin resolves None to False via a model_validator, while completion leaves None to be resolved from default_tok_params. OfflineInputsContext becomes kw_only with defaults, and tokenization_kwargs still override defaults. Tests are updated to the new signature and to provide default_chat_tok_params.

**Potential risks**  
Behavior changes for existing clients: completion requests that previously got add_special_tokens=True may now get False for multimodal models, and chat requests relying on the old explicit default are now resolved to False. The scoring path's isinstance(data_1[i], list) heuristic for choosing chat vs cmpl params could misclassify mixed or unusual inputs. The refactor to dataclasses.replace and the new required default_tok_params argument is a breaking API change for any external callers of build_tok_params. The model_validator mutating add_special_tokens in place should be verified for serialization and round-trip behavior.

**Key insights**  
The core design is sound: tri-state input with per-path resolution centralizes default handling in default_tok_params rather than hardcoding booleans. The main review focus should be on the scoring chat-vs-cmpl selection heuristic, backward compatibility of the changed build_tok_params signature, and confirming that the chat validator's None-to-False resolution does not conflict with the completion path's fallback semantics. Test coverage was updated but appears to only adjust signatures rather than add explicit tri-state behavior tests.

---

## 101. [[Suggestion] Drop the CUDA graph wrapper bypass flag](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68307)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68307
- **Author:** [LucasWilkinson](https://github.com/LucasWilkinson)
- **Merged By:** [yewentao256](https://github.com/yewentao256)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 06:19:07
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68307/files) (6):**
  - `docs/design/cuda_graphs.md`
  - `tests/v1/cudagraph/test_breakable_cudagraph.py`
  - `vllm/compilation/breakable_cudagraph.py`
  - `vllm/compilation/cuda_graph.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v41/nvidia/decoder_replay_cudagraph.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/cudagraph_utils.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Removes the module-global disable_cudagraph_wrappers()/cudagraph_wrappers_enabled bypass flag and its use in CUDAGraphWrapper, BreakableCUDAGraphWrapper, and the FULL capture path in cudagraph_utils. Instead, the two BreakableCUDAGraphWrapper runners (init_breakable_cg_runner and the DeepSeek V4.1 decoder replay runner) are pinned to runtime_mode=PIECEWISE so the existing mode-mismatch check makes them fall through during FULL capture. Docs and a test for the removed flag are updated accordingly.

**Technical impact**  
Eliminates cross-thread module-global state used to suppress wrapper dispatch during externally managed FULL capture, relying instead on per-wrapper runtime_mode scoping. This simplifies the dispatch logic and removes a thread-shared mutable flag, but shifts correctness onto the assumption that all in-model wrappers are PIECEWISE-scoped and that no wrapper legitimately needs FULL-mode dispatch inside the capture owner.

**Potential risks**  
The change is behavior-preserving only if every in-model wrapper is indeed PIECEWISE and the pinned runners are only invoked on PIECEWISE paths; any future or overlooked wrapper created with FULL or default runtime_mode could now attempt nested capture during FULL capture, causing errors or hangs. The removed test also covered thread-safety of the bypass, so that coverage is lost. Validation is limited to one GPU and a small model, so broader model/backend coverage is not demonstrated.

**Key insights**  
The PR is a reasonable simplification that replaces implicit global suppression with explicit per-wrapper mode scoping, which is easier to reason about. The main review focus should be confirming no remaining wrapper can match FULL during capture and that pinning the breakable runners to PIECEWISE does not alter their intended replay semantics on any path.

## 102. [[Bugfix] [Rust Frontend] Fix HTTP metrics for streaming and cancelled requests](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61856)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #61856
- **Author:** [reidliu41](https://github.com/reidliu41)
- **Merged By:** [BugenZhao](https://github.com/BugenZhao)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 06:08:54
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61856/files) (5):**
  - `rust/src/server/src/middleware/body_guard.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/middleware/load.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/middleware/metrics.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/middleware/mod.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/tests.rs`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The Rust frontend HTTP metrics middleware previously recorded request count and duration immediately after next.run(req).await, which for streaming responses fires before the body is emitted and skips recording entirely when the request future is cancelled. This PR introduces a reusable body_guard helper that wraps the response body and holds a guard whose Drop records metrics exactly once, either when the body is fully consumed/dropped or when the request future is dropped. The load middleware is refactored to reuse the same helper, and regression tests cover SSE body lifetime and pre-response cancellation.

**Technical impact**  
Metrics now reflect full response-body lifetime for streaming endpoints and include cancelled requests (defaulting to a 2xx status group, matching Python's with_cancellation behavior). The generic GuardedBody wrapper delegates all HttpBody methods to the inner body, so body behavior, metric names, labels, excluded routes, and status grouping are preserved. The shared helper removes duplicated body-wrapping code between load and metrics middleware.

**Potential risks**  
Cancelled requests are recorded with a hardcoded 2xx status, which may misrepresent client-aborted requests in dashboards or alerts; this is intentional parity with Python but worth confirming. The guard is only dropped when the body is dropped, so any code path that leaks or holds a response body indefinitely would delay metric emission. Tests rely on timing (50ms sleep) and global METRICS state with serial execution, which could be mildly flaky under load.

**Key insights**  
Holding a Drop guard inside a transparent body wrapper is a clean pattern for measuring end-to-end response lifetime and handling cancellation without double-counting. Extracting it into body_guard and reusing it in load.rs reduces duplication and keeps both middlewares consistent. The added tests meaningfully assert zero metrics before body consumption and duration covering the body gap, which directly validates the bug fix.

## 103. [[Bugfix][Rust Frontend] Return 400 when auto tool parser is unavailable](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63843)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #63843
- **Author:** [Bharath5252](https://github.com/Bharath5252)
- **Merged By:** [BugenZhao](https://github.com/BugenZhao)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 06:06:51
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63843/files) (2):**
  - `rust/src/chat/src/error.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/tests.rs`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The PR fixes a bug where requests using an auto tool parser selection with no matching parser for the model did not return a 400 error. It adds ParserUnavailableForModel to the set of errors mapped to a 400 BadRequest response in rust/src/chat/src/error.rs, and expands the route regression test to cover both ParserSelection::None and ParserSelection::Auto, asserting the specific error messages.

**Technical impact**  
Behavioral change in the chat error-to-HTTP-status mapping: ParserUnavailableForModel now yields a 400 BadRequestError instead of whatever status it previously produced (likely a 500). The test refactor consolidates two scenarios into a table-driven loop, improving coverage of the auto-parser-unavailable path.

**Potential risks**  
The change relies on ParserUnavailableForModel being the correct variant emitted when auto resolution fails; if other code paths surface a different error for the same condition, they may still return the wrong status. The test asserts exact error message strings, making it brittle to wording changes. Limited PR description and no explicit validation details reduce confidence in broader regression coverage.

**Key insights**  
The fix is a small but meaningful correctness improvement: client-side misconfiguration (no parser available for a model) is now classified as a client error rather than a server error. The test now verifies both the disabled and auto-unavailable cases with distinct messages, which is good practice for distinguishing failure modes.

## 104. [[Bugfix][Rust Frontend] Include null finish_reason in streaming chat choices](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63739)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #63739
- **Author:** [DeepakG-ai](https://github.com/DeepakG-ai)
- **Merged By:** [BugenZhao](https://github.com/BugenZhao)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 06:05:21
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63739/files) (3):**
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/openai/chat_completions/types.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/openai/completions/types.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/tests.rs`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The PR adds #[serialize_always] to the finish_reason field on ChatCompletionStreamChoice and CompletionStreamChoice in the Rust frontend so that non-final streaming choices emit an explicit "finish_reason": null instead of omitting the key, matching the Python frontend and OpenAI behavior. It also extends two existing route regression tests to assert null on non-final choices, the final "stop" reason, absence of usage when not requested, and the [DONE] terminator.

**Technical impact**  
Changes the serialized SSE wire format for streaming chat and completions responses in the Rust frontend: non-final chunks now include finish_reason as JSON null while other optional fields (logprobs, stop_reason, token_ids) remain omitted via skip_serializing_none. This improves parity with the Python frontend and OpenAI clients that expect the key to be present. The change is localized to two type definitions and is covered by updated tests.

**Potential risks**  
Clients that relied on the key being absent (e.g., strict schema validation or presence-based branching) could see behavior changes, though this aligns with the documented Python/OpenAI contract. The PR only addresses the finish_reason difference from #63656, so other streaming discrepancies remain. Validation used a scripted mock engine rather than a real model, so end-to-end parity with Python is not fully exercised.

**Key insights**  
Using serde_with's #[serialize_always] alongside skip_serializing_none is a clean, minimal way to force null emission for a single field without affecting sibling optional fields. The regression tests were strengthened to check every non-final choice rather than a single substring, which is a meaningful improvement in test reliability. The change is small and well-scoped, with clear evidence that the assertion failed before the fix.

## 105. [fix(security): default multimodal item limit to 999 for unconfigured modalities](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52602)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #52602
- **Author:** [jperezdealgaba](https://github.com/jperezdealgaba)
- **Merged By:** [BugenZhao](https://github.com/BugenZhao)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 06:03:49
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52602/files) (4):**
  - `rust/src/chat/src/backend/mod.rs`
  - `rust/src/chat/src/multimodal.rs`
  - `rust/src/cmd/src/cli.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/config.rs`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The Rust frontend previously treated modalities missing from --limit-mm-per-prompt as unlimited, diverging from Python's default of 999. This PR introduces DEFAULT_MM_ITEMS_PER_PROMPT = 999 and applies it as a fallback in validate_modality_limits, plus updates docs and tests to reflect the new default. The change closes a pre-inference OOM path where a single request under the 32 MiB body limit could trigger unbounded media-task fan-out.

**Technical impact**  
Behavioral change: unconfigured modalities are now capped at 999 items per prompt instead of being unbounded, aligning Rust with the Python frontend. Explicit counts (including via Options.count) still override the default, and the validation remains pre-fetch/pre-decode. Tests were updated and expanded to cover the default limit, explicit override, and count-less Options fallback.

**Potential risks**  
Any existing clients relying on unlimited unconfigured modalities will now hit MmLimitExceeded errors at 1000+ items, which is a breaking behavior change for those workloads. The 999 constant is hardcoded and duplicated conceptually with Python, so future divergence is possible if one side changes. The Options-without-count case now silently applies the default rather than being treated as profiling-only, which could surprise users who expected no count enforcement.

**Key insights**  
The fix is small but security-relevant, closing a resource-exhaustion vector before inference begins. Test coverage was appropriately strengthened to lock in the new default and override semantics. Consider centralizing the 999 default (e.g., shared config or protocol constant) to prevent drift between Rust and Python frontends, and note the behavior change in release notes.

The skip condition now also allows XPU platforms, and the skip message was updated to mention XPU attention.

## 107. [[ROCm][Perf][GDN] Export Triton and AITER FlyDSL prefill checkpoints on ROCm](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68310)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68310
- **Author:** [JaredforReal](https://github.com/JaredforReal)
- **Merged By:** [Isotr0py](https://github.com/Isotr0py)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 05:51:28
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68310/files) (3):**
  - `tests/v1/e2e/general/test_mamba_prefix_cache.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/mamba/gdn/qwen_gdn_linear_attn.py`
  - `vllm/v1/attention/backends/gdn_attn.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR enables align-mode prefill checkpoint export for Qwen GDN on ROCm, extending the Triton checkpoint path from CUDA-only to cuda_alike and adding the AITER FlyDSL backend. Since FlyDSL cannot store checkpoints, steps that carry a checkpoint fall back to the Triton/FLA prefill (which stores in-loop), while checkpoint-free steps stay on FlyDSL. The change touches the layer's checkpoint alignment/buffer spec, the attention backend builder (building FLA chunk metadata for FlyDSL checkpoint steps), and an e2e test expectation.

**Technical impact**  
On ROCm, both the Triton and AITER FlyDSL prefill backends now declare checkpoint alignment 64 in align mode, so prompts are no longer split at the last Mamba block boundary with prefix caching. The checkpoint buffer reservation is generalized from triton-only to any non-flashinfer backend, and the Triton checkpoint kernel/layer path is treated as platform-neutral. FlashInfer, CuteDSL, HIP, and XPU behavior is unchanged, and gfx1151 (HIP default) is unaffected.

**Potential risks**  
The FlyDSL checkpoint steps run the Triton kernel for the whole step, which is slower per token than FlyDSL; with long prompts nearly every prefill step carries a checkpoint, so this cost is largely hidden in the reported benchmarks but could matter for other workloads. The buffer-spec condition change from '== triton' to '!= flashinfer' broadens allocation to any non-flashinfer backend, which relies on the alignment gate to avoid over-allocation. Correctness on ROCm is validated by kernel and e2e tests plus gsm8k (FlyDSL differs by 3 questions), but coverage is limited to MI355X/gfx950 and one-layer dummy-weight e2e tests.

**Key insights**  
The approach is a minimal (+17/-5) alternative to the ~300-line tail-rerun design in #60659, performing equivalently at the default block size. The Triton checkpoint kernel already runs on ROCm for GLM-5.3-Flash, supporting the platform-neutral claim. Reported TTFT gains are large (-41% to -66% at concurrency 1/4/8) with matching output throughput, and the two ROCm backends converge because most long-prompt prefill steps carry checkpoints and thus run Triton.

---

## 108. [[Bugfix][Rust Frontend] Serve gpt-oss when vllm serve omits the parser flags](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/66998)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #66998
- **Author:** [2arian3](https://github.com/2arian3)
- **Merged By:** [BugenZhao](https://github.com/BugenZhao)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 05:39:28
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/66998/files) (2):**
  - `rust/src/chat/src/output/harmony/mod.rs`
  - `rust/src/chat/src/output/harmony/tests.rs`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The Rust frontend's Harmony parser override validation now accepts ParserSelection::None alongside Auto, so gpt-oss served via `vllm serve` without parser flags no longer 500s. Since #46359, omitted parser flags deserialize to `none`, which the Harmony check previously rejected; this aligns the Rust frontend with Python, which ignores parser flags for Harmony models. Tests were updated to use an explicit `qwen3` for the rejection case and to cover `none` acceptance.

**Technical impact**  
Small, localized change in rust/src/chat/src/output/harmony/mod.rs affecting only which parser selections the Harmony override check permits. Non-Harmony models and the Harmony output processor itself are untouched, so behavior for other models is unchanged. It unblocks the default `vllm serve openai/gpt-oss-20b` path on the Rust frontend, including tool calls on /v1/responses and /v1/chat/completions.

**Potential risks**  
The standalone `vllm-rs serve` CLI now also accepts an explicit `--reasoning-parser none` for gpt-oss, which is a minor semantic broadening (arguably matching Python). A pre-existing Harmony stream-truncation bug (Unexpected EOS mid-message returning 500 instead of `incomplete`) becomes reachable from `vllm serve` but is not introduced here. End-to-end validation is manual (gpt-oss-20b only) with no CI coverage serving gpt-oss on the Rust frontend, and one chat-completions tool-call case diverges from the Python frontend.

**Key insights**  
Root cause is the interaction between #46359's `none` default for omitted parser flags and the Harmony check's Auto-only acceptance; accepting None is the minimal fix. The tradeoff of conflating omitted with explicit `none` is acknowledged, with a cleaner alternative (separate omitted state in bootstrap config) available if stricter rejection is desired. The `--tool-call-parser openai` startup failure and the mid-message EOS 500 are correctly scoped out as separate issues.

## 109. [[Bugfix][Rust Frontend] Constrain to a single tool call when parallel_tool_calls is false](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/66988)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #66988
- **Author:** [2arian3](https://github.com/2arian3)
- **Merged By:** [BugenZhao](https://github.com/BugenZhao)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 05:38:06
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/66988/files) (1):**
  - `rust/src/parser/src/output_grammar.rs`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The Rust frontend's tool_grammar_applies in rust/src/parser/src/output_grammar.rs now returns true for tool_choice auto when parallel_tool_calls is false, so a single-call request builds the tool grammar and the existing stop_after_first logic caps it at one call. Previously such requests were grammar-free and extra calls were only dropped after parsing, wasting tokens and risking truncation. A unit test was added to cover the new behavior.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior changes only for requests with tool_choice auto, non-strict tools, and parallel_tool_calls false on /v1/responses and /v1/chat/completions; those now get a constrained one-call grammar matching the Python frontend. Requests with parallel_tool_calls unset or true remain grammar-free and byte-identical, and other tool-choice arms are untouched. The change reuses existing grammar construction and stop_after_first, so no new machinery is introduced.

**Potential risks**  
The change is a one-line grammar-selection tweak, but it alters constrained decoding for a specific request shape, so any regression would surface as changed output or token counts for parallel_tool_calls false plus auto. Validation used only Qwen3-0.6B and was not run on ROCm, and there is no model eval beyond the before/after comparison, so broader model coverage is limited. The new unit test asserts grammar presence and difference from the function-level grammar rather than exact output, which is reasonable but indirect.

**Key insights**  
The fix correctly places the grammar decision in vllm-parser's output_grammar alongside the rest of tool_grammar_applies, mirroring the Python frontend's approach. The before/after evidence showing exact parity with Python (one call, 26 tokens, no stray whitespace) and byte-identical controls is strong. The change is minimal and well-scoped, with the main residual gap being limited model and hardware coverage.

## 110. [[Tests] Reuse single-rank parallel groups for faster GPU unit tests](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68162)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68162
- **Author:** [fxmarty-amd](https://github.com/fxmarty-amd)
- **Merged By:** [DarkLight1337](https://github.com/DarkLight1337)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 05:36:01
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68162/files) (20):**
  - `.buildkite/test_areas/kernels.yaml`
  - `.buildkite/test_areas/model_executor.yaml`
  - `.buildkite/test_areas/quantization.yaml`
  - `tests/kernels/moe/conftest.py`
  - `tests/kernels/moe/test_expert_substitution.py`
  - `tests/kernels/moe/test_routed_scale_shared_experts.py`
  - `tests/kernels/moe/test_routing.py`
  - `tests/kernels/moe/test_routing_simulator.py`
  - `tests/kernels/moe/test_topk_softplus_sqrt.py`
  - `tests/kernels/moe/test_zero_expert_moe.py`
  - `tests/model_executor/layers/test_fused_shared_expert.py`
  - `tests/quantization/test_fp8.py`
  - `tests/quantization/test_fp8_dequant.py`
  - `tests/quantization/test_input_hadamard.py`
  - `tests/quantization/test_modelopt.py`
  - `tests/quantization/test_modelopt_nvfp4_embedding.py`
  - `tests/quantization/test_online.py`
  - `tests/quantization/test_online_mxfp4.py`
  - `tests/quantization/test_online_shared_expert_fusion.py`
  - `tests/single_rank_group.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This test-only PR extracts the previously duplicated single-rank parallel-group stand-in into a shared helper, tests/single_rank_group.py, providing SingleRankGroup and an override_parallel_groups context/decorator that swaps all eight parallel-state groups and restores them on exit, including across generator yields. It then replaces the dist_init fixture and ad-hoc TP rank/world-size monkeypatches in quantization, shared-expert, routing, expert-substitution and zero-expert tests, and adds skip_global_cleanup marks to eligible tests to avoid redundant teardown. Buildkite source_file_dependencies are updated so the relevant CI jobs rerun when the helper changes.

**Technical impact**  
Tests that only need rank metadata no longer initialize torch.distributed or a FileStore/backend, and they avoid the double cleanup_dist_env_and_memory() (each dominated by a full gc.collect()) that main pays via dist_init plus the autouse cleanup fixture. The decorator preserves pytest signatures and supports yielding fixtures, and the MoE conftest recognizes decorated tests via a _uses_single_rank_groups attribute to keep clean-entry isolation. Reported measured selection drops from ~488 s to ~191 s with identical pass/skip counts (301 passed, 63 skipped).

**Potential risks**  
The stand-in does not emulate real communication or asynchronous collectives, so tests relying on genuine distributed semantics could silently pass or behave differently; multi-rank and CUDA/XPU timing are explicitly unmeasured. skip_global_cleanup removes a global teardown that may matter for some tests, and the PR notes an attempted module-wide skip broke the online FSE checkpoint-weight equality test, showing the cleanup policy is fragile. The conftest coupling to a private _uses_single_rank_groups attribute is implicit and could drift if the decorator changes. Timings are noisy single-run measurements, so the performance claim is not a guarantee.

**Key insights**  
The dominant cost was teardown, not setup: dist_init's cleanup plus the autouse cleanup fixture caused two full gc.collect() calls per test, and the cleanup skips account for most of the ~2.5x gain while the decorators alone were near noise. Only 131 of 301 passing tests actually used dist_init, so the aggregate benefit is narrower than the headline example suggests. The change is well-scoped to tests with no production impact, but the reliance on a private marker attribute and the FSE cleanup exception are the main maintenance watchpoints.

---

## 111. [[Model Loader] Direct reload reruns post-load processing on the cold-start modules (LoRA)](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68345)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68345
- **Author:** [aoshen02](https://github.com/aoshen02)
- **Merged By:** [aoshen02](https://github.com/aoshen02)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 05:22:11
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68345/files) (5):**
  - `tests/model_executor/model_loader/test_direct_reload.py`
  - `tests/model_executor/model_loader/test_reload.py`
  - `vllm/config/vllm.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/model_loader/reload/direct.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/model_loader/utils.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Direct weight reload previously reran post-load processing over all named_modules(), which double-processed LoRA base layers because LoRA wrappers forward their base layer's quant_method. This PR records the cold-start module list in model._ckpt_modules and iterates that list on reload, drops the LoRA refusal in _verify_direct_reload_compat, and parametrizes the LoRA MoE reload test across layerwise and direct modes.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior changes in the model loader: process_weights_after_loading now uses the recorded cold-start module set when available, so modules added after cold start (LoRA wrappers) are skipped and each base layer is processed once. Direct reload now supports LoRA, aligning it with layerwise reload semantics. The change is small (+8/-10 across 5 files) but touches core loading and config validation paths.

**Potential risks**  
Storing _ckpt_modules as a plain attribute on the model is somewhat implicit and relies on __dict__.get fallback; if record_checkpoint_layout is not called or the attribute is stale, behavior silently falls back to named_modules(). Any future post-cold-start module additions that legitimately need PWAL would be skipped. Test coverage depends on a CUDA-only quantized MoE LoRA test, so non-CUDA CI may not exercise the direct+LoRA path.

**Key insights**  
The fix generalizes the layerwise reload rule (process only cold-start modules) to direct reload rather than special-casing LoRA, which is a cleaner design. Validation on GB200 with prompt logprobs before/after reload matching is reasonable evidence. The main review focus should be the robustness of the _ckpt_modules attribute lifecycle and whether the fallback path is adequately tested.

## 112. [[Rust Frontend] Serve the scale-out render routes on the main server](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67889)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #67889
- **Author:** [BugenZhao](https://github.com/BugenZhao)
- **Merged By:** [BugenZhao](https://github.com/BugenZhao)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 05:15:22
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67889/files) (8):**
  - `rust/src/cmd/src/cli.rs`
  - `rust/src/cmd/src/cli/tests.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/grpc/render.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/grpc/tests.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/render.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/render.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/tests.rs`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The PR implements the scale-out render routes (/v1/chat/completions/render and /v1/completions/render) on the Rust main server by making the render handlers generic over a RenderContext trait, moving that trait next to RenderState, and mounting the handlers on both the render-only and engine-backed routers. It also removes the VLLM_MAX_N_SEQUENCES check from render (deferring it to /inference/v1/generate) and drops the hidden max_n_sequences config from the render CLI.

**Technical impact**  
RenderContext's is_served_model is replaced by resolve_model returning LoraModelResolution, so HTTP lowering can resolve LoRA adapters and return 404 for unknown models, matching /inference/v1/generate. The scale-out gate is refactored into a (path, handler) table, so implemented routes are either served or 403. Render no longer enforces n limits, which is a behavior change for both servers but consistent with generate's existing validation.

**Potential risks**  
Removing the n-limit check from render changes behavior for the standalone render server and could surprise clients relying on early rejection, though the PR argues generate still rejects it. The generic handlers and trait move touch shared gRPC and HTTP paths, so regressions in the render-only server or gRPC model checks are possible. The new tests are serial and multi-threaded, which may add CI time.

**Key insights**  
The change reuses existing processors and lowering logic rather than loading a second model, keeping the main server's render output identical to the standalone render server. The refactor centralizes scale-out route gating and unifies model resolution across gRPC and HTTP. The PR is well-tested with new gate, LoRA, and n-limit tests plus a live run, but the n-limit divergence from Python is a deliberate trade-off worth documenting.

## 113. [[1/4][Rust Frontend] Add rust shm obj storage implementation](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67902)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #67902
- **Author:** [Isotr0py](https://github.com/Isotr0py)
- **Merged By:** [BugenZhao](https://github.com/BugenZhao)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 04:44:50
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67902/files) (9):**
  - `rust/Cargo.lock`
  - `rust/Cargo.toml`
  - `rust/src/shm-object-storage/Cargo.toml`
  - `rust/src/shm-object-storage/src/error.rs`
  - `rust/src/shm-object-storage/src/lib.rs`
  - `rust/src/shm-object-storage/src/object_storage.rs`
  - `rust/src/shm-object-storage/src/ring_buffer.rs`
  - `tests/distributed/test_shm_storage.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/device_communicators/shm_object_storage.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a new Rust crate vllm-shm-object-storage (ring_buffer.rs, object_storage.rs, error.rs) porting the writer side of the Python SingleWriterShmRingBuffer/SingleWriterShmObjectStorage to POSIX shared memory, plus a new 0x01 format-marker branch in the Python MsgpackSerde.deserialize and tests for it. Workspace Cargo.toml/Cargo.lock gain the crate and new deps (hmac, memmap2, rustix with shm feature).

**Technical impact**  
Introduces a cross-language shared-memory contract: segment layout ([32-byte HMAC secret][chunks of id/size/payload]), little-endian signed header fields, 8-byte chunk alignment for atomic reader-count loads, and HMAC-SHA256 handle signing. The Python deserialize change is backward compatible because pickle streams start with 0x80, so the 0x01 marker is unambiguous. This is the first of a 4-PR series and is not yet wired into the engine, so runtime behavior is largely unchanged until later PRs.

**Potential risks**  
The unsafe MmapMut mapping and AtomicI32::from_ptr reader-count load rely on alignment and bounds invariants that are only asserted, not enforced by types; a bug there is UB. Cross-process correctness depends on exact byte-layout parity with the Python reader (endianness, i32 header, alignment padding), which is hard to verify from this PR alone. Eviction depends on n_readers being set via set_n_readers; if callers forget, the free threshold is wrong. The deserialize marker check indexes data_view[0] without a length guard, so an empty payload would raise IndexError. Validation section is empty and no Rust-side tests are shown, so coverage of the new crate is unclear.

**Key insights**  
The design is a faithful port with deliberate safety choices: monotonic IDs keep increasing across clear() so stale handles fail the ID check, touches nest for concurrent requests, and put_with serializes in place to avoid a copy. The HMAC secret stored in the segment is readable by any process that can open the shm, so it authenticates handles against accidental reuse rather than malicious readers. The PR description's Claims/Validation sections are empty, which weakens reviewability; the added Python tests only cover the serde marker, not the Rust ring buffer or object storage.

## 114. [[Spec Decode] Pass multimodal embeddings to NemotronH MTP](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68153)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68153
- **Author:** [anhtra3889](https://github.com/anhtra3889)
- **Merged By:** [tomeras91](https://github.com/tomeras91)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 04:44:04
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68153/files) (2):**
  - `tests/v1/worker/test_gpu_target_dependent_ar_speculator.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/nemotron_h_mtp.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
NemotronHMTP now implements SupportsMultiModalEmbeddings and adds an embed_input_ids method that merges the target model's image/audio embeddings into the drafter's input embeddings via _merge_multimodal_embeddings. This removes the text-only fallback warning in DraftModelSpeculator for multimodal Nemotron-H targets and aligns the drafter with Nemotron3_5TranscribeMTP and other multimodal Eagle drafters. A new test covers text-only, merged multimodal, and missing is_multimodal error cases.

**Technical impact**  
Behavioral change limited to the Nemotron-H MTP drafter: when the target supports multimodal inputs, the speculator now forwards inputs_embeds instead of text token embeddings, and NemotronHMultiTokenPredictor.forward already prefers inputs_embeds. The change reuses existing merge utilities and interface helpers, so it is low-complexity and consistent with sibling drafters. End-to-end validation shows no measurable change in acceptance length or output quality across image, video, and text workloads.

**Potential risks**  
The merge path depends on is_multimodal being correctly provided; the code raises ValueError otherwise, which is tested but could surface as a runtime error if a caller omits it. Duplicate merge logic now exists in NemotronHMTP and Nemotron3_5TranscribeMTP (with differing error strings), creating minor maintenance drift risk. Validation is limited to one NVFP4 model on B300, so other Nemotron-H multimodal checkpoints or quantizations are not explicitly covered.

**Key insights**  
The change is primarily a consistency and correctness alignment rather than a performance improvement, since the target hidden state already carries multimodal context and AL is unchanged. The test uses a subclass stub to isolate embed_input_ids without full model construction, which is a reasonable lightweight approach. Consider consolidating the duplicated embed_input_ids logic between NemotronHMTP and Nemotron3_5TranscribeMTP in a follow-up.

## 115. [[Rust Frontend] Use the model's default temperature when a gRPC request omits it](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68253)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68253
- **Author:** [JulienDarve](https://github.com/JulienDarve)
- **Merged By:** [BugenZhao](https://github.com/BugenZhao)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 04:43:52
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68253/files) (3):**
  - `rust/proto/inference.proto`
  - `rust/src/server/src/grpc/convert.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/grpc/tests.rs`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The gRPC conversion path in rust/src/server/src/grpc/convert.rs no longer forces an omitted temperature to 0.0; it leaves it unset so the shared lowering logic resolves it to the model's generation-config default (or 1.0), matching the HTTP API. The proto comment is reworded to state this rule, the old greedy-default unit test is removed, and a new integration test asserts an omitted temperature yields the model default (0.6) in the engine-core request. Motivation is to let Dynamo's vLLM sidecar omit temperature and get model-correct sampling (e.g. Qwen3 at 0.6 with top_p/top_k) instead of greedy.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior change for gRPC clients: omitting temperature now samples at the model default rather than greedy, so clients needing greedy must send temperature: 0. Explicit temperatures, including 0, and HTTP behavior are unchanged. The wire schema is unchanged; only the semantics of an omitted field shift, and the change reuses existing lowering logic rather than adding new code paths. The FakeTextBackend test harness gains a sampling_hints override to exercise the default.

**Potential risks**  
Silent behavioral change for existing gRPC clients that relied on omitted temperature meaning greedy, with no proto_version bump to signal it (only engine_version can distinguish). Two open PRs (#66078 documenting the old 0.0 default in rust/README.md and #56776 moving the greedy fill into validate_sampling_params) would need updates and could conflict. The new test only covers the model-default case; the 1.0 fallback and --generation-config vllm paths are asserted to be covered elsewhere but not directly re-tested here.

**Key insights**  
The root cause was a single temperature.or(Some(0.0)) override that bypassed the shared SamplingHints.default_temperature lowering already used for top_p/top_k/min_p and by HTTP. The PR is well validated with a failing-before unit test and a live before/after comparison showing gRPC-omitted now matches HTTP-omitted and temperature=0.6. The main review concern is client compatibility and coordinating with the two in-flight PRs that assume the old default.

## 116. [[Frontend] Stream diarized_json transcriptions as segment events](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65779)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65779
- **Author:** [twu3202](https://github.com/twu3202)
- **Merged By:** [DarkLight1337](https://github.com/DarkLight1337)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 04:41:43
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65779/files) (10):**
  - `docs/serving/online_serving/speech_to_text.md`
  - `tests/entrypoints/speech_to_text/transcription/test_transcription_validation.py`
  - `tests/models/multimodal/processing/test_moss_transcribe_diarize.py`
  - `tests/models/multimodal/processing/test_transformers_audio.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/speech_to_text/base/serving.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/speech_to_text/transcription/api_router.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/speech_to_text/transcription/protocol.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/speech_to_text/transcription/serving.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/interfaces.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/moss_transcribe_diarize.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds streaming support for response_format=diarized_json on /v1/audio/transcriptions, emitting transcript.text.delta and transcript.text.segment events per finalized segment, then transcript.text.done and [DONE]. Introduces a StreamingDiarizedTranscriptParser interface with a default buffer-and-parse-at-end implementation and a native MOSS parser that emits segments as soon as the next header arrives. Also adds a custom StreamingResponse that closes the generator on disconnect, new token-usage event types, and rewrites MOSS non-streaming parsing to reuse the streaming parser.

**Technical impact**  
Touches the speech-to-text serving path, API router, response protocol, model interface, and MOSS model implementation. The MOSS parser is reworked from a header-scan approach to a stateful token scanner with bounded partial-marker carryover, and non-streaming parse_diarized_transcript now delegates to it, so behavior of existing diarized_json responses depends on the new parser. The default parser avoids quadratic accumulation by buffering fragments and joining once, and the response wrapper ensures engine iterators are closed on client disconnect. Token usage accounting is added for the new streamed format.

**Potential risks**  
The MOSS non-streaming parser semantics change, so subtle differences in edge cases (malformed markers, whitespace handling, overlong timestamps) could regress existing diarized_json output despite the added tests. The default parser still buffers the entire transcript in memory, so large outputs grow linearly. Error handling yields a streaming error after partial segments, which clients must handle. The new response type names may conflict with PR #49119 if it merges first. The native MOSS server regression was not rerun on this revision, and upstream Buildkite has not run, so integration coverage is partial.

**Key insights**  
The design cleanly separates a generic fallback parser from a model-specific streaming parser via a classmethod hook mirroring the existing streaming post-processor pattern. The benchmark shows a large improvement in accumulation cost for the default parser. Tests are thorough on parser semantics with explicit expected values and chunk-boundary independence, and the GPU test validates event ordering, ID/text consistency, and usage against engine metrics. The main review focus should be the MOSS parser rewrite's behavioral equivalence and the disconnect/error lifecycle handling.

  - `rust/src/server/src/render.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/inference/generate/convert.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/openai/chat_completions/convert.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/openai/chat_completions/types.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/openai/completions/convert.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/openai/completions/types.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/openai/responses/types.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/tests.rs`
  - `rust/src/text/src/backend/hf/config.rs`
  - `rust/src/text/src/backend/hf/mod.rs`
  - `rust/src/text/src/backend/mod.rs`
  - `rust/src/text/src/lib.rs`
  - `rust/src/text/src/lower.rs`
  - `rust/src/text/src/output/decoded.rs`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds support for --override-generation-config in the Rust frontend, previously listed as unsupported. HfTextBackend now loads generation_config.json as raw JSON, merges operator overrides key by key (null clears a default), and parses the result into sampling defaults; an override max_new_tokens is tracked as a new DefaultMaxTokens::Cap that bounds client-provided max_tokens, while the model's own value remains a Fallback. The flag is threaded through CLI, frontend config, server state, and forwarded to the managed Python engine. A related change makes skip_special_tokens an Option<bool> across request types and decode options so an unset value can inherit the generation-config default.

**Technical impact**  
Changes the public shape of TextDecodeOptions.skip_special_tokens and the OpenAI request types from bool to Option<bool>, and SamplingHints.default_max_tokens from Option<u32> to Option<DefaultMaxTokens>, affecting all crates that construct or consume these. resolve_max_tokens now distinguishes fallback vs cap semantics, and skip_special_tokens resolution moves into lower_text_request so HTTP, gRPC, and generate paths share one defaulting rule. Sampling hints are computed once at backend load instead of per call, which is a minor efficiency gain but means overrides are fixed at startup.

**Potential risks**  
The Option<bool> migration touches many call sites and could silently change behavior if any path previously relied on the implicit true default; the new tests cover HTTP, gRPC, and generate routes but external consumers of these structs would break. The cap semantics intentionally differ from the model fallback, so a mis-set override max_new_tokens could unexpectedly truncate all client requests. skip_special_tokens from the override config is parsed but not yet applied (documented TODO), a known deviation from Python. One pre-existing test failure is noted but attributed to main.

**Key insights**  
The design cleanly separates model-provided defaults (fallback) from operator overrides (cap), matching Python's override_max_tokens behavior, and reuses the existing SamplingHints plumbing rather than adding a parallel config path. Test coverage is strong, including a live mock-engine check and snapshot tests across all four inference routes. The skip_special_tokens refactor is a reasonable prerequisite for honoring that key later, though it broadens the blast radius beyond the stated feature.
