## 118. [[Perf][Frontend] Load chat `prompt_embeds` from the `base64` str not an encoded copy](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68236)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68236
- **Author:** [LuisRobaina](https://github.com/LuisRobaina)
- **Merged By:** [DarkLight1337](https://github.com/DarkLight1337)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 04:34:15
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68236/files) (4):**
  - `tests/renderers/test_prompt_embeds_payload_validation.py`
  - `tests/renderers/test_sparse_tensor_validation.py`
  - `vllm/renderers/chat_utils/content_parser.py`
  - `vllm/renderers/embed_utils.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The chat content parsers previously called data.encode() on each prompt_embeds base64 string before passing it to safe_load_prompt_embeds, creating an unnecessary bytes copy. Since pybase64 decodes str as fast as bytes, the copy only added latency and a base64-sized allocation, and in the async parser it ran on the event loop (0.70s at 131k x 4096 bf16 rows, 1.46s at 262k), blocking other API requests. The fix widens safe_load_prompt_embeds to accept bytes \\| str and passes the payload through unchanged in both parsers.

**Technical impact**  
Removes a redundant encode() copy and the associated allocation from the hot path, and eliminates event-loop blocking in the async parser, improving API server responsiveness under concurrent load. The type signature of safe_load_prompt_embeds is broadened to bytes \\| str, and all validation checks (including the archive size check) still run on the decoded bytes as before. Tests were added to confirm str and bytes payloads load identically and that non-ASCII str input still raises a 400 client error.

**Potential risks**  
Behavior now depends on pybase64 accepting str input directly; if any code path or dependency version handles str differently (e.g., non-ASCII handling), error semantics could shift, though tests cover the non-ASCII 400 case. Other callers of safe_load_prompt_embeds that pass bytes remain unaffected, but the widened signature should be verified against all call sites. The performance claims rely on the provided benchmark image and are not independently verified here.

**Key insights**  
This is a targeted performance fix that removes a copy which was both wasteful and, in the async path, blocking the event loop. The change is small but behavior-relevant, so it is correctly classified as SUBSTANTIAL. Test coverage appropriately extends existing validation tests to both bytes and str inputs, including the non-ASCII error path, which is the main correctness risk of skipping the encode() copy.

---

## 119. [[Bugfix][Kimi-K3] Reduce the shared expert for MegaMoE without sequence parallelism](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68373)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68373
- **Author:** [abmfy](https://github.com/abmfy)
- **Merged By:** [ZJY0516](https://github.com/ZJY0516)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 03:29:54
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68373/files) (1):**
  - `vllm/models/kimi_k3/nvidia/model.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
A one-line behavioral fix in vllm/models/kimi_k3/nvidia/model.py changes the shared expert's reduce_results flag from False to self.use_mega_moe and not use_sequence_parallel. Previously, with MegaMoE and PP > 1 (where sequence parallelism is disabled), the TP-sharded shared expert produced partial outputs that were never reduced, so each rank continued with an incomplete hidden state and served garbage tokens. The change makes the shared expert reduce its own output in that specific configuration, mirroring DeepSeek-V4's reduce_results=self.use_native_mega_moe.

**Technical impact**  
Corrects numerical correctness for Kimi-K3 with deep_gemm_mega_moe, expert parallel, and pipeline parallelism greater than 1, where the shared expert is TP-sharded rather than replicated. Sequence-parallel and non-MegaMoE paths are explicitly unchanged since the flag evaluates to False there. The PR reports GSM8K accuracy jumping from 0.011 to 0.958 and long-context recall from 0/18 to 18/18 on TP8 x PP2, matching the TP16 no-PP reference, which strongly supports the fix.

**Potential risks**  
The change is gated on a flag expression combining use_mega_moe and use_sequence_parallel, so correctness depends on those attributes being set accurately at construction time; a misconfigured or edge-case combination could reintroduce double-reduction or missing-reduction. No unit test is added, so regression protection relies on the manual multi-GPU validation described. The fix does not address the underlying PP > 1 prefill performance issue, which is deferred to a separate feature PR.

**Key insights**  
The bug was a silent correctness failure (normal throughput, wrong tokens) rather than a crash, making it easy to miss without accuracy evals. The isolation results (dropping draft model or DCP still fails, dropping MegaMoE/EP passes) cleanly implicate the MegaMoE shared-expert reduction path. Aligning with the existing DeepSeek-V4 pattern is a good consistency signal, and the fix is minimal and well-scoped to the affected configuration.

---

## 120. [[Bugfix][KV Offload][P2P] Prevent repeated PD KV fetches after decoder preemption](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67497)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #67497
- **Author:** [waizuichougou](https://github.com/waizuichougou)
- **Merged By:** [orozery](https://github.com/orozery)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 03:24:13
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67497/files) (4):**
  - `docs/features/kv_offloading_usage.md`
  - `tests/v1/kv_offload/tiering/p2p/test_manager.py`
  - `vllm/v1/kv_offload/tiering/p2p/manager.py`
  - `vllm/v1/kv_offload/tiering/p2p/session/client.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes a bug where, after decoder preemption or a partial promotion caused by a full CPU primary tier, the KV Offload P2P tier kept reporting PD cache hits that triggered an unsatisfiable second fetch and a 30-second timeout. The fix adds a mutable pd_fetch_submitted flag to P2PSourceInfo, sets it when the first nonempty PD fetch is submitted in submit_load, and makes lookup return MISS for subsequent PD-tier lookups so missing KV is recomputed. Docs and a comment in session/client.py are updated to reflect the new behavior.

**Technical impact**  
Changes PD-mode lookup semantics from always-HIT to HIT only before the first nonempty fetch, then MISS. This is per-request state retained across preemptions, so available primary/other-tier KV can still load while missing blocks are recomputed. Symmetric P2P probing and empty fetches are unaffected, and no extra control messages are added. The change is localized to the P2P manager and its request state.

**Potential risks**  
The flag is set on submit_load for any non-probe source, so correctness depends on the assumption that PD stores bind to round 0 and only the first nonempty fetch is supplyable; if future changes allow multiple PD fetch rounds this logic would need revisiting. Empty fetches intentionally do not consume the one-time supply, which is subtle and could be misread. Behavior change may alter recompute patterns and performance for PD workloads, though validation shows improved completion times and matching outputs.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is a mismatch between the decoder's repeated PD HIT reporting and the prefiller's single-round (round 0) supply, causing an unsatisfiable fetch and timeout. Tracking the first nonempty fetch submission per request is a minimal, targeted fix that preserves initial PD loading and symmetric probing. Tests cover nonempty/empty fetches, symmetric vs PD paths, fresh request state, and a realistic partial-promotion scenario through a real P2PSession, with strong before/after engine validation.

---

## 121. [[Bugfix][Quantization] Honor activation scheme in MXFP8 linear selection](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65288)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65288
- **Author:** [anhtud](https://github.com/anhtud)
- **Merged By:** [DarkLight1337](https://github.com/DarkLight1337)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 03:19:48
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65288/files) (9):**
  - `.buildkite/test_areas/kernels.yaml`
  - `docs/features/quantization/quark.md`
  - `tests/kernels/quantization/test_mxfp8_kernel_selection.py`
  - `tests/models/quantization/test_mxfp8.py`
  - `tests/quantization/test_fp8.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/kernels/linear/__init__.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/kernels/linear/mxfp8/Mxfp8LinearKernel.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/kernels/linear/mxfp8/emulation.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/kernels/linear/mxfp8/marlin.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes a bug where the MXFP8 linear kernel selector could pick a W8A8 backend (e.g. Humming/FlashInfer) that internally quantizes activations even when the checkpoint requested weight-only execution. The fix threads the requested activation quant key into Mxfp8LinearLayerConfig, resolves it via the existing activation override logic, and adds a _supports_quant_scheme gate so weight-only requests select Marlin or emulation and reject incompatible forced backends. Tests, docs, and Buildkite config are updated accordingly.

**Technical impact**  
Changes kernel selection behavior for MXFP8 dense linear layers: explicit None activation now yields Marlin (or emulation fallback) and raises during selection if an incompatible backend is forced, while omitted activation keys preserve prior W8A8 selection and A16 fallback. The gate is a classmethod check with no hot-path inference cost or synchronization. Adds a new activation_quant_key field to Mxfp8LinearLayerConfig defaulting to kMxfp8Dynamic, and new unit plus E2E regression tests (real Quark checkpoint through LLM.generate).

**Potential risks**  
Backend performance differences (Marlin vs Humming/FlashInfer) were not benchmarked, and SM100/103/120/121 selection was validated only with simulated hardware capability, not physical Blackwell. The E2E test uses a synthetic checkpoint so trained-model accuracy/perplexity is unverified. The PR also touches unrelated test infrastructure (DeepSeek ModelConfig fixture, Eagle default-device scoping) whose full failure was not reproduced on main, and GPU tests were not rerun on the latest merge heads, so CI regressions remain possible.

**Key insights**  
The core insight is that input_quant_key() describes external input/fusion support and does not indicate internal activation precision, so a separate _supports_quant_scheme gate was needed to distinguish self-quantizing kernels from weight-only ones. Marlin and emulation explicitly accept both None and kMxfp8Dynamic to retain the A16 fallback, while other MXFP8 kernels default to requiring dynamic activations. Validation is strong on H200 (red/green/red with identical binaries) but leaves Blackwell execution and accuracy evaluation as open items.

## 122. [[Bugfix][XPU] Forward num_splits to the XPU flash attention kernel](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/58131)

- **PR Number:** #58131

- **Merged By:** [yewentao256](https://github.com/yewentao256)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 03:05:18
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/58131/files) (3):**
  - `tests/v1/determinism/test_batch_invariance.py`
  - `tests/v1/determinism/utils.py`
  - `vllm/_xpu_ops.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The XPU flash attention shim in vllm/_xpu_ops.py previously accepted num_splits but silently dropped it, so the max_num_splits=1 set under VLLM_BATCH_INVARIANT never reached the kernel. This PR forwards it as num_splits_kv (None when <=0) and moves the parameter out of the stale 'not used' comment block. It also adds a _prompt_with_tokens helper in the determinism test utils and switches the bs1-vs-bsN test to ~2k-token prompts so KV spans multiple kernel blocks and split-count inconsistencies become observable.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior changes only on XPU: batch-invariant runs now pass num_splits_kv=1 to flash_attn_varlen_func, aligning the shim with the CUDA API contract and the flash_attn backend's intent. The test change lengthens prompts substantially, increasing runtime (~4.4% on the affected CUDA test) but making the determinism suite actually exercise multi-block KV reduction. CUDA paths are untouched by the source change.

**Potential risks**  
The new _prompt_with_tokens helper loads a tokenizer via AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(TEST_MODEL) at test time, adding a network/cache dependency and a module-level global; tokenization may not yield exactly target_tokens if the filler text is too short, though the max(4, ...) guard mitigates this. Forwarding num_splits_kv assumes the XPU kernel accepts that kwarg, which is unverified in the diff. A known residual decode-vs-prefill mismatch remains and is explicitly out of scope, so XPU batch invariance is still not fully achieved with FLASH_ATTN.

**Key insights**  
The root cause was a silent parameter drop masked by a misleading 'not used' comment, and the test suite could not detect it because prompts were too short to span multiple KV blocks. The PR correctly separates the shim fix from the deeper kernel-side decode/prefill reduction mismatch, which belongs in vllm-xpu-kernels. Reviewers should confirm the num_splits_kv kwarg name matches the installed XPU kernel version and consider whether the tokenizer load should be cached or skipped on non-XPU runs.

## 123. [[CI] Upgrade to pytest 9.2 and fix the tests that break on it](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67673)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #67673
- **Author:** [LantaoJin](https://github.com/LantaoJin)
- **Merged By:** [khluu](https://github.com/khluu)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 02:47:40
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/67673/files) (12):**
  - `requirements/test/cpu.txt`
  - `requirements/test/cuda.in`
  - `requirements/test/cuda.txt`
  - `requirements/test/kv_connectors_cuda.txt`
  - `requirements/test/rocm.in`
  - `requirements/test/rocm.txt`
  - `requirements/test/xpu.in`
  - `requirements/test/xpu.txt`
  - `tests/kernels/moe/test_flashinfer.py`
  - `tests/model_executor/test_minicpmv.py`
  - `tests/v1/structured_output/test_backend_xgrammar.py`
  - `tests/v1/worker/test_xpu_model_runner.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Bumps the test dependency to pytest >= 9.2.0 (and schemathesis >= 4.26.2, the first release supporting pytest 9.2) across the cuda/rocm/xpu requirement files and regenerates the lock files. It also fixes the tests that break on 9.2: instance-level monkeypatch.setattr calls in test_minicpmv.py and test_flashinfer.py are moved to the class, the single @pytest.mark.forked test switches to vLLM's fork_new_process_for_each_test, and a fixture/classmethod decorator order is corrected in test_backend_xgrammar.py.

**Technical impact**  
pytest 9.2 changes monkeypatch undo semantics (delattr instead of shadowing inherited attributes) and removes item._nodeid, which broke schemathesis and pytest-forked. Patching the class instead of an object.__new__ instance avoids nn.Module.__delattr__ failures, and using the in-repo fork helper sidesteps the unreleased pytest-forked fix. Lock files also drop hypothesis-jsonschema, junit-xml, and starlette-testclient and move pluggy to 1.7.0 and jsonschema-rs to 0.55.1.

**Potential risks**  
The change is test-only but touches shared test infrastructure and many lock files, so a bad lock could affect CI reproducibility. Patching at the class level is broader than instance patching and relies on monkeypatch scoping to restore state; the --forked hints in some tests/quantization docstrings remain broken until pytest-forked ships its fix. The PR description notes one remaining CI failure (test_hisparse.py) was an unrelated main breakage, so full green CI on this PR is not fully demonstrated in the provided info.

**Key insights**  
The author provides detailed before/after validation tables and root-cause explanations for each breakage, which is strong evidence for a dependency bump. The schemathesis pin is deliberately held at 4.26.2 rather than latest to minimize test-generation churn. Reviewers should confirm the class-level patches preserve intended test isolation and that the regenerated locks match the .in files.

## 124. [[Bugfix][NIXL] Report failed pulls per request with several KV cache groups](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61499)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #61499
- **Author:** [vMaroon](https://github.com/vMaroon)
- **Merged By:** [NickLucche](https://github.com/NickLucche)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 02:44:06
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61499/files) (2):**
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/test_nixl_connector.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/nixl/base_worker.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The NIXL connector worker now gates reporting of invalid block IDs on a new _report_invalid_block_ids flag, which is true only when the model is not HMA and has exactly one KV cache group. Previously, any non-HMA model reported block IDs on a failed pull, which caused Scheduler._handle_invalid_blocks to raise and kill the engine core on models with multiple KV cache groups (e.g. HiSparse, where extra groups are outside the transfer). Failed pulls are now surfaced only via failed_recving so the request fails or is recomputed while the engine keeps serving. A parametrized unit test covers the multi-group case.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior changes only for non-HMA models with more than one KV cache group: failed pulls no longer populate invalid_block_ids, so the scheduler no longer raises and instead handles the request through failed_recving (FINISHED_ERROR or recompute under kv_load_failure_policy). Single-group and HMA paths are unchanged. The condition mirrors the scheduler's own group-count check, keeping the worker within the scheduler's current contract without depending on pending scheduler-side recovery PRs.

**Potential risks**  
The fix is a workaround that suppresses block-level failure reporting rather than enabling the scheduler to handle it, so multi-group models lose per-block invalidation granularity and rely on whole-request failure/recompute, which may be costlier. The flag is computed once at init from kv_cache_config, so any later mutation of group configuration would not be reflected. The production validation is strong but the underlying NIXL_ERR_REMOTE_DISCONNECT cause remains unaddressed, and the new test only exercises the setup-failure path, not real remote disconnects.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is a contract mismatch: the worker reported block IDs the scheduler cannot map back to requests when multiple KV cache groups exist, and the scheduler's response was to crash the engine. Gating on the same condition the scheduler checks is a minimal, low-risk containment fix with a clear TODO to remove once _handle_invalid_blocks supports multiple groups. The PR is well validated with both unit tests and a documented production rollout on 36 decode engines.

## 125. [[ROCm] Add AITER CK DASH attention for Unlimited-OCR-2](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/66031)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #66031
- **Author:** [gty111](https://github.com/gty111)
- **Merged By:** [Isotr0py](https://github.com/Isotr0py)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 02:39:36
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/66031/files) (9):**
  - `.buildkite/test_areas/attention.yaml`
  - `.buildkite/test_areas/models_multimodal.yaml`
  - `docs/models/supported_models.md`
  - `examples/generate/multimodal/vision_language_multi_image_offline.py`
  - `tests/models/multimodal/generation/test_unlimited_ocr.py`
  - `tests/models/multimodal/processing/test_unlimited_ocr.py`
  - `tests/v1/attention/test_unlimited_ocr2.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/config.py`
  - `vllm/v1/attention/ops/dash_attention.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds ROCm support for Unlimited-OCR-2 DASH attention by defaulting to ROCM_AITER_FA on ROCm for both the DASH compute path and the vision encoder, calling AITER's CK mha_varlen_fwd directly with logical lengths and padded KV offsets. It also reverts temporary ROCm test skips, adds AMD CI coverage, a ROCm-specific processing test, a new CK segment/GQA/padding test, docs, and removes a hardcoded FLASH_ATTN backend from the example.

**Technical impact**  
Changes default backend selection in verify_and_update_model_config for ROCm, extends the allowed backend set to include ROCM_AITER_FA, and adds a new code path in flash_dash_attention that bypasses AITER's ASM dispatcher in favor of CK varlen attention while preserving the existing LSE/prior merge. Test parametrization is refactored to be platform-aware, and CI now runs the previously skipped attention and multimodal tests on AMD hardware.

**Potential risks**  
The CK path relies on min_seqlen_q=0 for empty segments and padded cu_seqlens_k, which is subtle and could regress on different AITER/CK versions or shapes; the PR itself notes numerical flakiness is unresolved and MI300 and clean-build validation are pending. Human review and upstream CI are incomplete, and the new ROCm default could affect users who relied on prior behavior. The mm_encoder_attn_backend default is only applied when unset, so explicit overrides still take precedence.

**Key insights**  
The change is well-scoped, reusing existing routing and merge logic without scheduler changes, and is backed by substantial local validation (attention, generation, processing tests plus MPDocBench). The new ROCm test explicitly poisons padding with NaN to verify masked segments are ignored, which is a strong correctness check. Remaining gaps are MI300 coverage, clean-build validation, and the acknowledged numerical flakiness.

## 126. [[CI] Restore the default device after test_fc_layer_quant_config_usage](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68350)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68350
- **Author:** [bogdan-petkovic](https://github.com/bogdan-petkovic)
- **Merged By:** [AndreasKaratzas](https://github.com/AndreasKaratzas)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 01:46:43
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68350/files) (1):**
  - `tests/model_executor/test_eagle_quantization.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The PR fixes a test isolation bug in tests/model_executor/test_eagle_quantization.py where test_fc_layer_quant_config_usage called torch.set_default_device(device) without restoring the prior default. It introduces a default_device fixture that saves the original default device, sets it for the test, and restores it on teardown, mirroring existing patterns in other test files. This prevents the GPU default device from leaking into subsequent tests in the same pytest process.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior is confined to the test suite; no production code changes. The fixture ensures the default device is reset after each parametrized run, so later tests such as test_qwen3_omni_audio_encoder_has_no_gpu_sync create host tensors as expected instead of GPU tensors. This restores correct cross-test isolation in the Model Executor CI shard.

**Potential risks**  
The fixture depends on the pytest 'device' fixture being available and on torch.get_default_device/set_default_device semantics; if the original default device is itself a GPU in some environment, restoration preserves that state rather than forcing CPU, which is correct but worth noting. The change is narrow and validated by the author's local MI300X runs, though broader CI shard ordering could still surface other device-leak issues.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is a leaked global torch default device, not a bug in the Qwen3-Omni audio encoder, and the fix correctly targets the leaking test via a save/restore fixture. The PR includes clear before/after reproduction evidence showing the failure on main and the pass with the change. Only AMD CI exposed it because the NVIDIA Model Executor job is disabled, highlighting a coverage gap.

## 127. [[ROCm][Test] Check the AITER delayed mHC pre against the residual it wrote](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68300)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68300
- **Author:** [AndreasKaratzas](https://github.com/AndreasKaratzas)
- **Merged By:** [shen-shanshan](https://github.com/shen-shanshan)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 01:20:46
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68300/files) (1):**
  - `tests/kernels/mhc/test_mhc_kernels.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The ROCm AITER delayed mHC pre test previously computed the torch reference pre from a torch-computed residual, but on gfx942 AITER truncates the bf16 residual while torch rounds to nearest even, causing the pre to drift up to 0.25 and the test to fail. The change reorders the test so the expected pre is computed from the residual the op actually wrote (actual[0]), while the residual itself is still compared against the torch reference at one bf16 ULP.

**Technical impact**  
Test-only change in tests/kernels/mhc/test_mhc_kernels.py; no production code is modified. It aligns the pre comparison with AITER's own seam-test approach and makes the test pass on gfx942 with both truncating and RNE-rebuilt module_mhc, while leaving gfx950 behavior unchanged. The residual correctness check against the torch reference is preserved.

**Potential risks**  
Deriving the expected pre from the op's own residual output weakens independence of the pre check: a bug in the residual that is within the residual tolerance could propagate into the pre comparison. The residual is still validated separately at one bf16 ULP, which bounds this risk, but the pre assertion is no longer fully end-to-end against torch. The underlying AITER truncation on gfx94x remains unfixed and is tracked separately.

**Key insights**  
The failure is an arch-dependent rounding mismatch (truncation on gfx942 vs RNE on gfx950) rather than a kernel bug, and CI only runs kernels/mhc on MI355 so it never surfaced there. The fix correctly scopes the test to what the op guarantees per-arch and documents the rationale in the docstring. Validation across 10 runs on both rounding modes is solid evidence, though the change is test-only and does not address the AITER truncation itself.

## 128. [[Perf] Optimize DeepSeek V4/V4.1 vision operations with Triton kernels](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68097)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68097
- **Author:** [chaunceyjiang](https://github.com/chaunceyjiang)
- **Merged By:** [Isotr0py](https://github.com/Isotr0py)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 01:12:59
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68097/files) (3):**
  - `tests/models/test_deepseek_v4_vl.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v4/common/ops/vision.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v4/common/vision.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a new Triton kernel module (vllm/models/deepseek_v4/common/ops/vision.py) implementing fused vision RMSNorm, Q/K rotary embedding, and packed spatial merge, and rewires the DeepSeek V4/V4.1 vision tower to use them with a PyTorch fallback for non-CUDA paths. The goal is to cut image-encoding latency (claimed 13-21% per image, ~15.8% packed) while preserving exact reference numerics. Tests were added to assert bit-exact equivalence against the reference implementations.

**Technical impact**  
Behavioral change to the vision encoder hot path: RMSNorm is split into a square kernel plus a norm/scale kernel that deliberately keeps the FP32 mean reduction in PyTorch to preserve reduction order, and Q/K rotary is fused into a single kernel that writes separate Q and K buffers while leaving V untouched. Spatial merge is fused into one gather/scale/store kernel. All kernels are gated on current_platform.is_cuda() and contiguity, with the original PyTorch code retained as fallback, so non-CUDA and edge cases should behave as before.

**Potential risks**  
The RMSNorm path allocates a full FP32 squared buffer and still calls torch.mean, so memory overhead and part of the reduction cost remain; the exactness claim depends on enable_fp_fusion=False and matching reduction order, which could silently diverge on other Triton/CUDA versions or architectures. The rotary kernel assumes qkv.stride(0) layout and specific head/dim constexprs, and the spatial merge kernel assumes indices/mask shapes and contiguity; misconfiguration could produce wrong results rather than errors. Benchmarks are single-GPU (H20) and exclude preprocessing/H2D, so real end-to-end gains may be smaller. Tests are CUDA-only and will be skipped or fail on non-CUDA CI runners.

**Key insights**  
The design prioritizes bit-exact output over maximum fusion, notably keeping the variance reduction in PyTorch rather than fully fusing RMSNorm, which limits the speedup but protects BF16 residual accumulation. Ablations show Q/K RoPE contributes the most and spatial merge the least, suggesting further effort should target the norm/rotary path. The fallback structure and exact-match tests are good practice, but the PR would benefit from broader hardware validation and explicit handling of non-contiguous or unusual input layouts in the kernels.

---

## 129. [[ROCm][CI] Measure process-local GPU memory in the Qwen3-VL leak test](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68342)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #68342
- **Author:** [AndreasKaratzas](https://github.com/AndreasKaratzas)
- **Merged By:** [DarkLight1337](https://github.com/DarkLight1337)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 00:57:01
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/68342/files) (1):**
  - `tests/models/multimodal/generation/test_memory_leak.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The Qwen3-VL memory leak test now measures process-local GPU memory via torch.accelerator.memory_allocated() and memory_reserved() instead of device-wide total-minus-free, fixing false failures caused by other processes on the same GPU. On ROCm it parametrizes over TRITON_ATTN, ROCM_ATTN, and ROCM_AITER_FA (skipped off gfx9) rather than pinning TRITON_ATTN, and asserts zero growth separately on allocated and reserved memory.

**Technical impact**  
Changes test behavior and CI coverage: the AMD job runs test_no_memory_leak three times (~1 min extra), and the leak check is now scoped to the test process's PyTorch allocations. The new attention_backend fixture defaults to None for non-ROCm platforms, preserving single-run behavior there. The assertions are stricter in that they check two metrics but narrower in scope than the prior device-wide reading.

**Potential risks**  
Device memory allocated outside PyTorch within the process (e.g., raw hipMalloc in a custom op) is no longer detected, as the PR acknowledges; the KV cache is the main such allocation and is made once at startup. The parametrization and gfx9 skip logic depend on on_gfx9() and ATTENTION_BACKENDS being correct, and the added CI runs increase runtime and GPU contention sensitivity. Validation is limited to MI355X with idle GPUs, so behavior under shared-GPU CI conditions is inferred rather than directly shown.

**Key insights**  
The root cause analysis is well-supported: the old device-wide measurement counted other processes and even a bare HIP context (+2 MiB), explaining the ROCM_ATTN and ROCM_AITER_FA failures, and the statistical insignificance of the earlier backend split is correctly noted. The fix is a reasonable trade-off that trades some coverage (non-PyTorch device allocations) for reliability. The PR is thorough on evidence and limitations, though the change is test-only and does not affect production code.

---

## 130. [[Perf][Frontend] Bound completed hypothesis histories in online beam search](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/66841)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #66841
- **Author:** [tgghzyh](https://github.com/tgghzyh)
- **Merged By:** [DarkLight1337](https://github.com/DarkLight1337)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-18 00:26:06
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/66841/files) (2):**
  - `tests/samplers/test_beam_search_online.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/generate/beam_search/online.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The online beam search in vllm/entrypoints/generate/beam_search/online.py now prunes the completed (EOS) hypothesis list to at most beam_width entries after each step, using the existing sort_beams_key with a stable sort to preserve tie ordering. This bounds retained token/logprob histories, which previously could grow quadratically with decoding steps. Two parameterized tests were added to verify the bound and correct displacement behavior.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior of returned results is intended to be unchanged since finished hypotheses have immutable scores and only the top beam_width can ever be returned; the stable sort matches the final ranking semantics. Memory for retained finished histories drops substantially in long generations with frequent EOS candidates, at the cost of an extra sort on steps that exceed the width. Active search, engine requests, and offline implementation are untouched.

**Potential risks**  
Correctness depends on the assumption that finished hypotheses never change score and that sort_beams_key is identical to the final ranking key; any divergence in tie-breaking or key semantics could alter which hypotheses are returned. The extra sort adds minor CPU overhead on EOS-heavy steps, though reported timings show no regression. Validation is limited to specific fixtures and a small set of models/prompts, so edge cases around length penalty ties or stop-reason handling may not be fully covered.

**Key insights**  
The fix is a small, well-targeted change with strong empirical validation including API output equivalence, memory measurements, and deterministic replay analysis of a timing anomaly. The added tests directly encode the ranking invariant and the displacement case, making regressions detectable. The main residual concern is reliance on the sort key matching final ranking exactly, which the tests partially but not exhaustively cover.

---
